## Part XIX. General constructions (HWZ Chapter 19, §§19.1–19.8)

Chapter 19 (Springer 2021, pp. 863–957; 2017 numbers in parentheses) builds the polyfold structures of Chapter 17 from combinatorial data. The input is a groupoidal category $\mathcal S$ (a category in which every morphism is an isomorphism and every morphism set is finite), a uniformizer construction $F$ assigning to each object $\alpha$ a set $F(\alpha)$ of uniformizers $\Psi : G \ltimes O \to \mathcal S$ at $\alpha$, and a transition construction $\mathcal F$, a class of families of local maps $F_h$ of the transition sets $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ with the properties (19.3) (A)–(F). From this the chapter produces a topology on the orbit space $|\mathcal S|$, topologies and then M-polyfold structures on the transition sets, and so a polyfold construction in the sense of Chapter 17. §19.1 (pp. 863–868) defines the data (Definitions 19.1.1–19.1.3, 19.1.5–19.1.7, Remarks 19.1.4, 19.1.8 (2017: Definitions 19.1–19.5, Remarks 19.1, 19.2)). §19.2 (pp. 869–872) defines the natural topology $T$ on $|\mathcal S|$ (Definition 19.2.1, Theorem 19.2.2, Remarks 19.2.3, 19.2.4 (2017: Definition 19.6, Theorem 19.1, Remarks 19.3, 19.4)). §19.3 (pp. 872–879) defines the topologies $T_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ on the transition sets (Lemma 19.3.1, Definition 19.3.2, Theorem 19.3.3, Propositions 19.3.4, 19.3.5, 19.3.7, Remark 19.3.6, Lemma 19.3.8 (2017: Definition 19.7, Theorem 19.2, Propositions 19.1, 19.2, Remark 19.5)). §19.4 (pp. 879–885) gives the properness conditions and the metrization criteria (Definition 19.4.1, Propositions 19.4.2, 19.4.4, 19.4.5, 19.4.7, Lemma 19.4.3, Theorem 19.4.6, Corollary 19.4.8 (2017: Definition 19.8, Propositions 19.4, 19.3, 19.5, 19.6, Theorem 19.3)). §19.5 (pp. 886–890) builds the polyfold structure (Theorems 19.5.1, 19.5.3, Corollary 19.5.2, Remark 19.5.4 (2017: Theorems 19.4, 19.5, Corollary 19.1, Remark 19.7)) and transfers (ZHOU), (LUC), (SLUC), (LR), (SLR) to basic constructions (Definitions 19.5.5, 19.5.6, 19.5.8, 19.5.10, Propositions 19.5.7, 19.5.9, 19.5.11). New in the 2021 edition are Definition 19.1.5, Lemmas 19.3.1, 19.3.8, Proposition 19.3.7, Lemma 19.4.3, Corollary 19.4.8 and all of Definitions 19.5.5–19.5.10 with Propositions 19.5.7, 19.5.9, 19.5.11. §19.6 (A Strong Bundle Version, pp. 890–905) carries the construction to a functor $\mu : \mathcal S \to \mathrm{Ban}$ and its category $E_\mu$: strong bundle uniformizers, their transition constructions, the topologies on $|E_\mu|$ and on the transition sets, the strong bundle structures on the transition sets, and an alternative approach through pull-backs with the coherence property. §19.7 (pp. 905–930) treats finite-to-one covering functors $P : \mathcal A \to \mathcal B$: uniformizers for $P$, the topology of $|\mathcal A|$, the local structure of $|P|$, metrizability and paracompactness, and the induced proper polyfold covering structures. §19.8 (Covering Constructions for Strong Bundles, pp. 930–957) combines the two, first for bundle covering pull-backs and then for bundle covering squares, ending with Theorem 19.8.22.

This Part follows the book's order. §§XIX.1–XIX.3 treat §19.1: groupoidal categories, uniformizers and uniformizer constructions (§XIX.1), the transition sets with the properties (A)–(F) (§XIX.2), restriction, equivalence, transition and basic constructions and Remark 19.1.8 (§XIX.3). §§XIX.4–XIX.5 treat §19.2: the natural topology and Theorem 19.2.2 (§XIX.4), Remarks 19.2.3 and 19.2.4 (§XIX.5). §§XIX.6–XIX.7 treat §19.3: the topologies $T_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ with Lemma 19.3.1, Definition 19.3.2 and Proposition 19.3.4 (§XIX.6), and the structure maps with Proposition 19.3.5, Remark 19.3.6, Proposition 19.3.7, Lemma 19.3.8 and Theorem 19.3.3 (§XIX.7). §§XIX.8–XIX.10 treat §19.4: the properness conditions with Proposition 19.4.2 and the orbit-chart lemmas behind it (§XIX.8), Lemma 19.4.3 and Proposition 19.4.4 (§XIX.9), the metrization criteria (§XIX.10). §§XIX.11–XIX.14 treat §19.5: Theorem 19.5.1 and Corollary 19.5.2 (§XIX.11), Theorem 19.5.3 and Remark 19.5.4 (§XIX.12), (ZHOU) with Proposition 19.5.7 and the pointwise forms of the regularity conditions (§XIX.13), (LUC), (SLUC), (LR), (SLR) with Propositions 19.5.9 and 19.5.11 (§XIX.14). §XIX.15 collects the witnesses of §§19.1–19.5. The sections on §§19.6–19.8 follow.

**What is proved, and what is not.** All eight sections of Chapter 19 are formalized.

*Formalized as printed.* Definitions 19.1.1, 19.1.3, 19.1.5–19.1.7 and Remarks 19.1.4, 19.1.8, the transition sets and the properties (19.3) (A)–(F) (§§XIX.1–XIX.3). Definition 19.2.1 and Remarks 19.2.3, 19.2.4, the first with its example of a natural topology that is not Hausdorff (§§XIX.4–XIX.5). All of §19.3: Lemma 19.3.1, Definition 19.3.2, Theorem 19.3.3 (1)–(6), Propositions 19.3.4, 19.3.5, 19.3.7, Remark 19.3.6 and Lemma 19.3.8 (§§XIX.6–XIX.7). Definition 19.4.1, Proposition 19.4.5, Theorem 19.4.6, Proposition 19.4.7 and Corollary 19.4.8 (§§XIX.8, XIX.10). Corollary 19.5.2, Theorem 19.5.3 (1), (2), Remark 19.5.4, Definitions 19.5.5, 19.5.6 and Propositions 19.5.7, 19.5.9 (§§XIX.11–XIX.14).

*Restated.* Definition 19.1.2, with "$G$ is the isotropy group of $\alpha$" as a separate predicate, unused in §§19.1–19.5 (§XIX.1). Proposition 19.4.2, with (v3) read with the limit in the fibre, as its proof uses it (§XIX.8). Definitions 19.5.8 and 19.5.10, with $G$ read as the isotropy group $G_{\bar o}$ and the levels $(F^i, \mathcal F^i)$ read through the raised translation groupoids (§XIX.14).

*Stronger.* Proposition 19.4.4 without the properness condition, as in the 2017 text (§XIX.9); with it Corollary 19.4.8 needs no hypothesis (§XIX.10). Theorem 19.5.1 without the metrizability of $T$, with the naturality of the structure made precise (§XIX.11).

*Explicit.* Theorem 19.2.2 with $F(\alpha) \neq \emptyset$ read as part of a uniformizer construction, the standing reading of §17.1 (§XIX.4). Proposition 19.5.11, from the ep-groupoid case through Proposition 19.5.7 (§XIX.14).

*Narrower.* Lemma 19.4.3, verified at points $o$ that are the source of an element of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$, which is the case Proposition 19.4.4 uses (§XIX.9).

*Does not hold as we read it.* Lemma 19.4.3 at a point that is not a source (§XIX.9). In §§19.6–19.8: Proposition 19.7.18 (the local homeomorphism property of $|P|$), Corollary 19.7.20 (3) and Theorem 19.7.25 (4b), all on the absolute value example $|\mathcal A| \cong \mathbb R \to |\mathcal B| \cong [0, \infty)$, the forms where the isotropy maps $G_a \to G_{P(a)}$ are onto being proved; and the divisibility remark after Lemma 19.7.19, which is not used later.

*Not formalized.* The geometric meaning of $F(\varphi)$ in Remark 19.1.4, which is prose; the sentence of Remark 19.5.4 that a metrizable neighbourhood of a compact set is sometimes all one needs; Definition 19.5.8 with "every $g \in G$" taken literally for a uniformizer that is not centred. In §§19.6–19.8, Remarks 19.6.14 and 19.8.11 are outlook (G.272, G.274, nothing to formalize).

*Points* (§§XIX.3–XIX.14). G.257–G.268 are the points of §§19.1–19.5, all formalized: G.257 the shrinkings of Remark 19.1.8 (§XIX.3), G.258 the smooth structures in the example of Remark 19.2.3 (§§XIX.5, XIX.11), G.259 the continuity of $\bar\Psi$ in Proposition 19.3.7 and G.260 the choice of $W(h)$ in Lemma 19.3.8 (§XIX.7), G.261 the two easy exercises in the proof of Lemma 19.4.3 (§XIX.9), G.262 the averaging of $\beta$ in Proposition 19.4.5 (§XIX.10), G.263 Theorem 19.5.3 from the discussion of the section and G.264 the neighbourhoods of Remark 19.5.4 (§XIX.12), G.265 Proposition 19.5.7, called obvious (§XIX.13), G.266 the levels $(F^i, \mathcal F^i)$, G.267 Proposition 19.5.9 without proof and G.268 Proposition 19.5.11 from the ep-groupoid case (§XIX.14). G.269–G.275 are the points of §§19.6–19.8, treated in their sections.

**The decisions that shape the chapter.**

(1) *$F(\alpha) \neq \emptyset$ on every class.* Definition 19.1.3 allows $F(\alpha) = \emptyset$; with $F \equiv \emptyset$ the collection $T$ of Definition 19.2.1 is $\{\emptyset\}$ and does not contain $|\mathcal S|$. Theorem 19.2.2 and everything after it take the hypothesis that every isomorphism class contains an object $\alpha$ with $F(\alpha) \neq \emptyset$, the standing reading of §17.1 for covering families (Findings, Chapter 19, item 1).

(2) *One representative, and independence.* A transition construction is a quotient of transition data by the equivalence of Definition 19.1.5; a basic construction carries the class, and the text's "we take a representative" is the representative chosen by the quotient. Every topology and structure of §§19.3–19.5 is defined from that representative, and a lemma shows that any other representative gives the same topology (Lemma 19.3.1) and the same M-polyfold structure (Theorem 19.5.1, natural).

(3) *Orbit charts in the abstract.* The topological arguments of §§19.2–19.4 are proved once, for an abstract orbit chart: a continuous open map $\pi : O \to Z$ from a space with an action of a finite group whose fibres are the orbits, and for a local homeomorphism with finitely many sheets. The basic construction then supplies these charts, $q \mapsto |\Psi(q)|$ and $s : M(\Psi, \Psi') \to O$. This separates what is general topology from what is special to (A)–(F).

(4) *Properness through closures.* Of the three properness conditions of Definition 19.4.1, (v2) (closure of an image of a chart inside the image of the chart) is the one carried through; (v1) and (v3) are proved equivalent to it, (v3) in the reading with a limit. The sequential arguments of the text are replaced by neighbourhood arguments where this is shorter (Lemma 19.3.8, Proposition 19.4.5), and kept where the text needs them (Lemma 19.4.3).

(5) *Descent for the smooth structure.* The M-polyfold structure on $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is obtained by Chapter 9's descent from the disjoint union of the domains $V(o_h)$ of the maps $F_h$, the transition condition being (B). The structure maps are then sc-smooth because they become the structure maps of the domains after composing with the local sc-diffeomorphism $s$.

(6) *Added hypotheses are named and witnessed.* Where a statement is proved under a hypothesis the text does not print (the source condition in Lemma 19.4.3, the Hausdorff condition for (3) ⇒ (2) of Proposition 19.4.2 with (v3) as printed), the hypothesis is named and witnessed, with a remark on the strength of the witness (§XIX.15).

**Map.**

- `Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples`: the translation, reflection, trivial $\mathbb Z/3$, empty and glued basic constructions; Remark 19.2.3 (§§XIX.4, XIX.5, XIX.15).
- `Chapter19/NaturalTopology`: Definition 19.2.1, Theorem 19.2.2, Remark 19.2.4, and all of §19.3 (§§XIX.4–XIX.7).

*Topology and metrizability (§§19.3–19.4).*
- `Chapter19/TopologyLemmas`: regularity from a local homeomorphism, finite sheets, orbit charts, (v1)–(v3) for orbit charts, invariant Urysohn functions, the metrization criteria in abstract form (§§XIX.7–XIX.10).
- `Chapter19/Metrizability`: Definition 19.4.1, Proposition 19.4.2, Lemma 19.4.3, Propositions 19.4.4, 19.4.5, Theorem 19.4.6, Proposition 19.4.7, Corollary 19.4.8 (§§XIX.8–XIX.10).
- `Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples`: the glued lines for (v3), the exponential example for Lemma 19.4.3, the witnesses of §19.4 (§§XIX.8, XIX.9, XIX.15).

- `Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureGCT`: the GCT and the polyfold construction of a basic construction, Theorem 19.5.3 (§XIX.12).
- `Chapter19/TopologyLemmasTwo`, `Chapter19/CompactNeighbourhoods`: metrizable neighbourhoods of compact sets, Remark 19.5.4 (§XIX.12).
- `Chapter19/BasicRegularity`: Definitions 19.5.5, 19.5.6, 19.5.8, 19.5.10, Propositions 19.5.7, 19.5.11 (§§XIX.13–XIX.14).
- `Chapter19/BasicRegularityPointwise`: the pointwise (LUC), its invariance, Proposition 19.5.9 (§§XIX.13–XIX.14).

*Strong bundles and coverings (§§19.6–19.8).*

Throughout, $\pi : \mathrm{obj}(\mathcal S) \to |\mathcal S|$ is the map to isomorphism classes; for a uniformizer $\Psi : G \ltimes O \to \mathcal S$ the transition set $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ consists of the triples $h = (o, \varphi, o')$ with $\varphi : \Psi(o) \to \Psi'(o')$, with source $s(h) = o$ and target $t(h) = o'$; $F_h : V(o) \to M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is the map of a representative $F$ of $\mathcal F$, and $f_h = t \circ F_h$.

### XIX.1 Definitions 19.1.1–19.1.3, Remark 19.1.4 (2017: Definitions 19.1–19.3, Remark 19.1): groupoidal categories and uniformizer constructions, pp. 863–865

**Statement.** *Definition 19.1.1* (p. 863; same) ⟦Chapter17/GroupoidalCategory · GroupoidalCategory⟧: a groupoidal category is a category $\mathcal S$ in which every morphism is an isomorphism and every morphism set $\mathrm{mor}(\alpha, \beta)$ is finite ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · GroupoidalCategory.definition_19_1_1⟧. *Definition 19.1.2* (p. 864; restated) ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer⟧: a uniformizer at $\alpha$ is a functor $\Psi : G \ltimes O \to \mathcal S$ from the translation groupoid of a finite group $G$ acting by sc-smooth maps on an M-polyfold $O$, injective on objects and fully faithful, with $\Psi(\bar o) = \alpha$ for some $\bar o$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.IsAt⟧. The condition that $G$ is the isotropy group of $\alpha$ is the separate predicate that $\bar o$ can be chosen invariant under $G$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.IsCentredAt⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.IsCentred⟧. *Definition 19.1.3* (p. 865; same) ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · GroupoidalCategory.UniformizerConstruction⟧: a uniformizer construction assigns to every object $\alpha$ a set $F(\alpha)$ of uniformizers at $\alpha$. *Remark 19.1.4* (p. 865; same) ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.Functorial⟧: if $F$ is a functor, every $F(\varphi) : F(\alpha) \to F(\alpha')$ is a bijection ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.remark_19_1_4⟧.

**Proof of Definition 19.1.1.** *Step 1.* The structure holds a groupoid structure on objects and morphisms and the finiteness of every $\mathrm{mor}(\alpha, \beta)$. *Step 2* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · GroupoidalCategory.definition_19_1_1⟧. The three clauses of the definition (every morphism has a two-sided inverse, $\varphi^{-1} \circ \varphi = 1$ and $\varphi \circ \varphi^{-1} = 1$, finite morphism sets) are read off.

**The uniformizers of a construction.** The uniformizers occurring in $F$ form a type ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.Unif⟧, over which the transition data of §XIX.2 are indexed. Two non-emptiness conditions are defined, neither part of Definition 19.1.3: every object has a uniformizer ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.HasUniformizers⟧, and every isomorphism class contains an object with a uniformizer ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.HasClassUniformizers⟧. The first implies the second by induction on the class ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.HasUniformizers.hasClassUniformizers⟧. The second is the hypothesis of Theorem 19.2.2 (§XIX.4).

**Proof of Remark 19.1.4.** *Step 1.* A functorial construction is given by maps $F(\varphi)$ on the uniformizers, sending $F(\mathrm{src}\,\varphi)$ into $F(\mathrm{tgt}\,\varphi)$, with $F(1_\alpha) = \mathrm{id}$ and $F(\varphi' \circ \varphi) = F(\varphi') \circ F(\varphi)$ ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.Functorial⟧. *Step 2* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.remark_19_1_4⟧. $F(\varphi^{-1})$ is a two-sided inverse of $F(\varphi)$ on $F(\alpha)$ and $F(\alpha')$: $F(\varphi^{-1}) \circ F(\varphi) = F(\varphi^{-1} \circ \varphi) = F(1_\alpha) = \mathrm{id}$, and symmetrically. So $F(\varphi)$ is a bijection $F(\alpha) \to F(\alpha')$.

*Readings.* The text's "$G$ is the isotropy group of $\alpha$" in Definition 19.1.2 is kept as a predicate rather than as part of a uniformizer, since no statement of §§19.1–19.5 uses it; the definition is thereby more general. With $\bar o$ invariant under $G$, full faithfulness gives $G \cong \mathrm{Aut}(\alpha)$. The witnesses are centred (§XIX.15).

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The uniformizer is Chapter 17's (Definition 17.1.3 without the base point); Definition 19.1.2 adds only the base point, as a property. Remark 19.1.4 also discusses the geometric meaning of $F(\varphi)$; that part is prose and not formalized, and no functorial uniformizer construction is given as a witness.

### XIX.2 Transition sets and the properties (19.3) (A)–(F), pp. 865–867

**Statement** (same). *The transition sets* (p. 865) ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.Trans⟧: for uniformizers $\Psi$, $\Psi'$, $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is the set of triples $(o, \varphi, o')$ with $\varphi : \Psi(o) \to \Psi'(o')$, with the structure maps unit, inverse and multiplication of Chapter 17 ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · GroupoidStr.TransitionSet⟧, ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · GroupoidStr.TransitionSet.unit⟧, ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · GroupoidStr.TransitionSet.inv⟧, ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · GroupoidStr.TransitionSet.mul⟧, ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · GroupoidStr.TransitionSet.mul_eq_comp⟧, and the map $\bar\Psi(g, q) = (q, \Psi(g, q), g * q)$ from $G \times O$ to $M(\Psi, \Psi)$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.hat⟧. *Transition data* (pp. 866–867) ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData⟧: for every $h \in M(\Psi, \Psi')$ with $s(h) = o$, an open $V(o) \ni o$ and a map $F_h : V(o) \to M(\Psi, \Psi')$ with $F_h(o) = h$, such that (A) $s \circ F_h = \mathrm{id}$, $f_h = t \circ F_h$ is an sc-diffeomorphism onto an open set, and $F_h(q) = \bar\Psi(g, q)$ when $h = \bar\Psi(g, o)$; (B) $F_{F_h(q)} = F_h$ near $q$; (C) $F_{u(o)} = u$ on $V(o)$; (D) $F_{\iota(h)}(f_h(q)) = \iota(F_h(q))$ where defined; (E) $m(F_{h'}(f_h(q)), F_h(q)) = F_{m(h', h)}(q)$ where defined; (F) for $h_1 \neq h_2$ with the same source the images of a small neighbourhood of $o$ under $F_{h_1}$, $F_{h_2}$ are disjoint.

**Proof of the consequences used later.** The text states (A)–(F) and uses a few consequences without comment; they are proved here.

*Step 1, the map $f_h$* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.fmap⟧. $f_h = t \circ F_h$, and $f_h(o) = o'$ by $F_h(o) = h$ ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.fmap_src⟧.

*Step 2, continuity and openness* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.continuousOn_fmap⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isOpen_image_fmap⟧. By (A), $f_h$ is an sc-diffeomorphism of $V(o)$ onto its image, so it is continuous on $V(o)$ and maps open subsets of $V(o)$ to open sets. The restriction of $f_h$ to an open $N \subseteq V(o)$ is again an sc-diffeomorphism onto its image ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isScDiffeoOn_fmap_restrict⟧.

*Step 3, the orbit of $f_h(q)$* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.π_obj_eq⟧. For $q \in V(o)$, $F_h(q)$ is a morphism from $\Psi(s F_h(q)) = \Psi(q)$ to $\Psi'(f_h(q))$, so $|\Psi(q)| = |\Psi'(f_h(q))|$.

*Step 4, the shrinking step used throughout* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.exists_nbhd_fmap⟧. For an open $V' \ni t(h)$, the set $N = V(o) \cap f_h^{-1}(V')$ is open by Step 2, contains $o$ by Step 1, and $f_h(N) \subseteq V'$.

*Step 5, the first identity of (E)* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.mul_fmap⟧. The text lists $f_{h'} \circ f_h = f_{m(h', h)}$ together with the second identity of (E); it follows from the second by applying $t$, since $t \circ m = t \circ \mathrm{pr}_1$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text writes the domains $V(o)$ as depending on $o$; they depend on $h$, as the text's later use makes clear, and are indexed by $h$. The pointedness $F_h(o) = h$ is a field; the text has it implicitly in "$F_h : (V(o), o) \to (M(\Psi, \Psi'), h)$". Only the second identity of (E) is a field, the first being Step 5.

### XIX.3 Restriction, Definitions 19.1.5–19.1.7, Remark 19.1.8 (2017: Definitions 19.4, 19.5, Remark 19.2), pp. 867–868

**Statement** (same). *Restriction* (p. 867) ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.restrict⟧: replacing every $V(o)$ by a smaller open neighbourhood gives transition data. *Definition 19.1.5* (p. 867, new in the 2021 edition) ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.Equiv⟧: $F$ and $F'$ are equivalent if for every $h$ there is an open $V \ni s(h)$ inside both domains with $F_h = F'_h$ on $V$. *Definition 19.1.6* (p. 867) ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionConstruction⟧: a transition construction is an equivalence class. *Definition 19.1.7* (p. 868) ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · GroupoidalCategory.BasicConstruction⟧: a basic construction is a pair $(F, \mathcal F)$. *Remark 19.1.8* (p. 868): after shrinking, the domains can be taken so that (A), (C), (D), (E) and (B) hold in the forms the text lists.

**Proof that restriction gives transition data.** *Step 1* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.restrict⟧. The new domain of $h$ is $Q(h) \cap V(o)$ for an open $Q(h) \ni o$; the maps are unchanged. *Step 2.* (A), (C), (D), (E) are statements on points of the domains and pass to smaller domains. (B) passes because its domain condition is an intersection of domains. For (F), the open set $V$ given by (F) for $F$ is intersected with $Q(h_1) \cap Q(h_2)$, and the disjointness of images passes to subsets.

**Proof that Definition 19.1.5 is an equivalence relation.** *Step 1, reflexivity* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.equiv_refl⟧: take $V = V(o)$. *Step 2, symmetry* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.Equiv.symm⟧: the condition is symmetric. *Step 3, transitivity* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.Equiv.trans⟧: given $V$ for $F, F'$ and $V'$ for $F', F''$, take $V \cap V'$. The relation is a setoid ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.setoid⟧, and a restriction is equivalent to the original ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.equiv_restrict⟧, which is the text's remark that the restriction is "as good as" $F$.

**Definitions 19.1.6 and 19.1.7.** The transition construction is the quotient by this setoid ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionConstruction⟧; a basic construction holds $F$ and $\mathcal F$ ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · GroupoidalCategory.BasicConstruction⟧. Its representative is the one the quotient chooses ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · GroupoidalCategory.BasicConstruction.rep⟧, and its class is $\mathcal F$ ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · GroupoidalCategory.BasicConstruction.mk_rep⟧.

**Proof of Remark 19.1.8 (point G.257).** The text says that by shrinking one obtains neighbourhoods with the listed properties; each is proved.

*Step 1, (A) and (D)* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.remark_19_1_8_inv⟧. For $h = (o, \varphi, o')$, apply the shrinking step (§XIX.2, Step 4) with $V' = V(\iota(h))$, the domain of $F_{\iota(h)}$ at $o'$. On the resulting $N$, $f_h$ is an sc-diffeomorphism onto the open set $f_h(N) \ni o'$ (§XIX.2, Step 2), and (D) holds at every $q \in N$ because $f_h(q) \in V(\iota(h))$.

*Step 2, the special form of (A)* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.remark_19_1_8_hat⟧. For $h = \bar\Psi(g, o)$, the field of (A) gives $F_h(q) = \bar\Psi(g, q)$ on $V(o)$, and then $f_h(q) = g * q$.

*Step 3, (C)* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.remark_19_1_8_unit⟧: the domain $V(o)$ of $u(o)$ itself.

*Step 4, (E)* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.remark_19_1_8_mul⟧. For composable $h$, $h'$, let $N \ni o$ be given by the shrinking step with $V'$ the domain of $F_{h'}$, and set $V(o) = N \cap V(m(h', h))$, $V(o') = f_h(V(o))$ and $V(o'') = f_{h'}(V(o'))$. Both $f_h$ and $f_{h'}$ are sc-diffeomorphisms on these sets (§XIX.2, Step 2), and the two identities of (E) hold on $V(o)$ by the field (E) and §XIX.2, Step 5.

*Step 5, (B)* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.remark_19_1_8_stability⟧: for $q$ in $V(o)$, $F_{F_h(q)} = F_h$ on $V(F_h(q)) \cap V(o)$, an open neighbourhood of $q$, by the field (B).

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text treats the equivalence of Definition 19.1.5 as evidently an equivalence relation; the three steps above are short. Remark 19.1.8 is stated in the text as a list of shrinkings; each item is a separate statement, all proved from the shrinking step.

### XIX.4 Definition 19.2.1, Theorem 19.2.2 (2017: Definition 19.6, Theorem 19.1): the natural topology, p. 869

**Statement.** *Definition 19.2.1* (same) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.NatOpen⟧: $W \subseteq |\mathcal S|$ belongs to $T$ if every $z \in W$ has an object $\alpha$ with $|\alpha| = z$, a $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$, a point $\bar o$ with $\Psi(\bar o) = \alpha$ and an open $V \ni \bar o$ with $|\Psi(V)| \subseteq W$. *Theorem 19.2.2* (explicit), under the reading that every class contains an object $\alpha$ with $F(\alpha) \neq \emptyset$: (1) $T$ is a topology ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_2_2_one⟧; (2) for $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ and open $V \subseteq O$, $|\Psi(V)|$ is open ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_2_2_two⟧; (3) $|\Psi| : G \backslash O \to |\mathcal S|$ is a homeomorphism onto an open set containing $|\alpha|$ ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_2_2_three⟧; (4) $T$ is first countable ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_2_2_four⟧; (5) $T$ is locally metrizable ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_2_2_five⟧.

**Proof of (1).** *Step 1, the empty set and unions* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.natOpen_empty⟧, ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.natOpen_sUnion⟧. The empty set satisfies the condition vacuously; for a union, a point $z$ lies in some member $W$, and the data for $z$ in $W$ serve for the union.

*Step 2, the whole space* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.natOpen_univ_iff⟧. $|\mathcal S| \in T$ if and only if every class contains an object $\alpha$ with $F(\alpha) \neq \emptyset$: given such $\alpha$ and $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$, choose $\bar o$ with $\Psi(\bar o) = \alpha$ and $V = O$; conversely the condition at $z$ produces such an $\alpha$. This is where the reading enters.

*Step 3, binary intersections* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.natOpen_inter⟧. Let $z \in W_1 \cap W_2$, with data $(\alpha_i, \Psi_i, \bar o_i, V_i)$ for $W_i$. Since $|\alpha_1| = |\alpha_2| = z$ there is a morphism $\varphi : \alpha_1 \to \alpha_2$, and $h = (\bar o_1, \varphi, \bar o_2) \in M(\Psi_1, \Psi_2)$. By the shrinking step of a representative (§XIX.2, Step 4) there is an open $N \ni \bar o_1$ inside $V_1 \cap V(\bar o_1)$ with $f_h(N) \subseteq V_2$. For $q \in N$, $|\Psi_1(q)| = |\Psi_2(f_h(q))|$ (§XIX.2, Step 3), so $|\Psi_1(N)| \subseteq |\Psi_1(V_1)| \cap |\Psi_2(V_2)| \subseteq W_1 \cap W_2$. The data $(\alpha_1, \Psi_1, \bar o_1, N)$ serve.

*Step 4* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · GroupoidalCategory.BasicConstruction.natTop⟧, ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.isOpen_natTop_iff⟧. The topology $T$ is assembled from Steps 1–3, with the representative of $\mathcal F$ used in Step 3; its open sets are exactly the members of $T$. (1) is the conjunction ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_2_2_one⟧.

**Proof of (2).** *Step 1.* Let $z = |\Psi(o)|$ with $o \in V$. By the reading there is $\alpha'$ with $|\alpha'| = z$ and some $\Psi' \in F(\alpha')$, with $\Psi'(\bar o') = \alpha'$. *Step 2.* A morphism $\Psi'(\bar o') \to \Psi(o)$ gives $h \in M(\Psi', \Psi)$ with $s(h) = \bar o'$ and $t(h) = o$. *Step 3.* By the shrinking step there is an open $N \ni \bar o'$ with $f_h(N) \subseteq V$; then $|\Psi'(N)| \subseteq |\Psi(V)|$ by §XIX.2, Step 3. So $|\Psi(V)|$ satisfies the condition at $z$ ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_2_2_two⟧.

**Proof of (3).** *Step 1, continuity* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.continuous_π_obj⟧. For $W \in T$ and $o$ with $|\Psi(o)| \in W$, the data of $W$ at $|\Psi(o)|$ give $(\Psi', \bar o', V')$ with $|\Psi'(V')| \subseteq W$; with $h \in M(\Psi, \Psi')$ from $o$ to $\bar o'$ and $N = V(o) \cap f_h^{-1}(V')$, $|\Psi(N)| \subseteq W$. So $q \mapsto |\Psi(q)|$ is continuous. *Step 2, injectivity on orbits.* If $|\Psi(q)| = |\Psi(q')|$, there is a morphism $\Psi(q) \to \Psi(q')$, and fullness gives $g$ with $g * q = q'$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.exists_smul_eq_mor⟧. So the induced map $|\Psi| : G \backslash O \to |\mathcal S|$ is injective. *Step 3, openness.* The image of an open set of $G \backslash O$ is the image of its (open) preimage in $O$, which is open by (2). *Step 4* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_2_2_three⟧. A continuous injective open map is an open embedding, and $|\alpha| = |\Psi(\bar o)|$ is in the image.

**Proof of (4) and (5).** *Step 1, the orbit chart* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.isOrbitChart⟧. By Step 1 of (3), (2) and Step 2 of (3), $q \mapsto |\Psi(q)|$ is continuous, open, and its fibres are the $G$-orbits: an orbit chart in the sense of §XIX.8. *Step 2, (5)* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_2_2_five⟧. For $z$, choose $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ with $|\alpha| = z$; by (3), the image of $|\Psi|$ is an open neighbourhood of $z$ homeomorphic to $G \backslash O$, which is metrizable as the orbit space of a finite group acting on a metrizable space ⟦Chapter17/GroupoidalCategory · metrizableSpace_translation_orbitSpace⟧. *Step 3, (4)* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_2_2_four⟧. A metrizable neighbourhood is first countable, and the neighbourhood filter of $z$ in $|\mathcal S|$ is the image of that in the open subspace.

**The reading $F(\alpha) \neq \emptyset$.** With the empty uniformizer construction ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.emptyBasic⟧ on a non-empty groupoidal category, a set belongs to $T$ if and only if it is empty ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.emptyBasic_natOpen_iff⟧, so $|\mathcal S| \notin T$ and (1) does not hold for it ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.theorem_19_2_2_empty_family⟧. Every statement from Theorem 19.2.2 on therefore takes the hypothesis that every class meets an object with a uniformizer, the standing reading of §17.1 for covering families (Findings, Chapter 19, item 1); its witness is the reflection construction (§XIX.15).

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text proves (1) for finite intersections of $k$ sets directly; the formal proof does two sets and lets the topology axioms do the rest. Step 3 of (1) is the only place where a representative of $\mathcal F$ is needed for $T$; Lemma 19.3.1 (§XIX.6) shows the topologies of §19.3 do not depend on it, and $T$ does not either, since its definition does not mention $\mathcal F$.

### XIX.5 Remarks 19.2.3, 19.2.4 (2017: Remarks 19.3, 19.4): a natural topology that is not Hausdorff, and metrizability, pp. 870–872

**Statement** (same). *Remark 19.2.3* (p. 870) ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.remark_19_2_3⟧: the natural topology need not be Hausdorff, with the example of two lines glued along $x > 0$. *Remark 19.2.4* (p. 872) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.remark_19_2_4⟧: if $T$ is Hausdorff, regular and second countable, it is metrizable, and $(\mathcal S, T)$ is a GCT ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.toGCT⟧.

**Proof of Remark 19.2.3.** *Step 1, the category* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.gluedLines⟧. Objects are the pairs $(x, \pm 1)$, $x \in \mathbb R$; there is one morphism $(x, \epsilon) \to (x, \epsilon')$ when $\epsilon = \epsilon'$ or $x > 0$, and none otherwise ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.GluedMor⟧. So $(x, 1)$ and $(x, -1)$ are isomorphic exactly when $x > 0$ ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.gluedLines_π_eq_iff⟧, and $|(0, 1)| \neq |(0, -1)|$ ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.gluedLines_π_ne⟧.

*Step 2, the uniformizers* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.gluedUnif⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.gluedU⟧. For $\epsilon = \pm 1$, $\Psi_\epsilon : \{1\} \ltimes \mathbb R \to \mathcal S$, $x \mapsto (x, \epsilon)$, injective on objects and fully faithful; $F(x, \epsilon) = \{\Psi_\epsilon\}$, so every object has a uniformizer ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.gluedBasic_hasUniformizers⟧ and every uniformizer is centred ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.gluedBasic_isCentred⟧.

*Step 3, the transitions* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.gluedData⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.gluedBasic⟧. For $h = (x, \varphi, x)$, $F_h(q) = (q, \varphi_q, q)$ with the unique morphism, defined for all $q$ when $\Psi = \Psi'$ and for $q > 0$ otherwise. These are keyed transition data ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.ofKey⟧ (§XIX.15): an element is determined by its source and its key (here the pair of signs), so (A)–(F) follow, $f_h$ being the identity of $\mathbb R$ on its domain.

*Step 4, the natural topology* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.gluedBasic_natOpen⟧. A set $W$ is open if and only if both $\{x : |(x, 1)| \in W\}$ and $\{x : |(x, -1)| \in W\}$ are open in $\mathbb R$.

*Step 5, not Hausdorff* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.remark_19_2_3⟧. Every neighbourhood of $|(0, 1)|$ contains $|(r/2, 1)|$ for small $r > 0$, every neighbourhood of $|(0, -1)|$ contains $|(r/2, -1)|$, and these two classes are equal. So the two distinct points $|(0, \pm 1)|$ cannot be separated.

**Proof of Remark 19.2.4.** *Step 1* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.remark_19_2_4⟧. A regular, Hausdorff, second countable space is metrizable (Urysohn's metrization theorem, Mathlib's form for $T_3$ second countable spaces). *Step 2* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.toGCT⟧. Chapter 17's GCT is a groupoidal category with a metrizable topology on the orbit space; Step 1 supplies it.

*Readings.* The text's example in Remark 19.2.3 mentions that the transition sets carry natural smooth structures (point G.258). These are the structures of Theorem 19.5.1, which is proved without the metrizability of $T$ (§XIX.11), so they exist in this example although $T$ is not Hausdorff; neither (v1) nor (v2) of §19.4 holds for it (§XIX.8).

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text draws the glued line; the formal example is the groupoidal category above, with the text's two charts and the transitions on $x > 0$.

### XIX.6 Lemma 19.3.1, Definition 19.3.2, Proposition 19.3.4 (2017: Definition 19.7, Proposition 19.1): the topologies on the transition sets, pp. 872–874

**Statement** (same). *The collection* $T^F_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ (p. 872) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.IsOpenTr⟧: $W \subseteq M(\Psi, \Psi')$ such that every $h \in W$ has an open $V \ni s(h)$ inside $V(o)$ with $F_h(V) \subseteq W$; it is a topology ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isOpenTr_topology⟧. *Lemma 19.3.1* (p. 872, new in the 2021 edition) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.lemma_19_3_1⟧: equivalent transition data give the same collection. *Definition 19.3.2* (p. 873) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.definition_19_3_2⟧: $T_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ is the topology of any representative ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionConstruction.topology⟧, ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.M⟧. *Proposition 19.3.4* (p. 874) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_3_4⟧: (1) $F_h(W)$ is open for open $W \subseteq V(o)$; (2) $F_h$ is continuous on $V(o)$ and an open embedding there; (3) $T_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ is Hausdorff.

**Proof that $T^F_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ is a topology.** *Step 1* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isOpenTr_topology⟧. The whole set: take $V = V(o)$. Unions: the data for $h$ in a member serve for the union. *Step 2, intersections.* For $h \in W_1 \cap W_2$, with $V_1$, $V_2$ for $W_1$, $W_2$, take $V_1 \cap V_2$. The topology ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.topology⟧ makes $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ a space ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.Tr⟧ whose open sets are the members of the collection ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isOpen_tr_iff⟧.

**Proof of Lemma 19.3.1.** *Step 1* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isOpenTr_of_equiv⟧. Let $F \sim F'$ and $W$ open for $F$. For $h \in W$, the condition for $F$ gives $V$ with $F_h(V) \subseteq W$, and the equivalence gives an open $V' \ni s(h)$ with $F_h = F'_h$ on $V'$. Then $V \cap V'$ lies in the domain of $F'_h$, and $F'_h(V \cap V') = F_h(V \cap V') \subseteq W$. *Step 2* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.lemma_19_3_1⟧. By symmetry of the equivalence, the two collections are equal.

**Definition 19.3.2.** By Lemma 19.3.1 the topology descends to the quotient ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionConstruction.topology⟧. For a basic construction, $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ with this topology ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.M⟧ is the space of the definition ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.definition_19_3_2⟧; its open sets are those of any representative ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.isOpen_M_iff⟧, and for two representatives the identity is a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.trHomeomorph⟧.

**Proof of Proposition 19.3.4 (1).** *Step 1* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isOpen_image_map⟧. Let $W \subseteq V(o)$ be open and $h^* = F_h(q) \in F_h(W)$. By (B), $F_{h^*} = F_h$ on $V(q) \cap V(o)$, a neighbourhood of $q$ (here $V(q)$ is the domain of $F_{h^*}$). So $V = W \cap V(q) \cap V(o)$ is open, contains $q = s(h^*)$, and $F_{h^*}(V) = F_h(V) \subseteq F_h(W)$.

**Proof of (2).** *Step 1, continuity* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.continuousAt_map⟧, ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.continuousOn_map⟧. For $q \in V(o)$ and an open $W \ni F_h(q)$, the condition of $W$ at $h^* = F_h(q)$ gives $V \ni q$ with $F_{h^*}(V) \subseteq W$, and by (B) $F_h = F_{h^*}$ near $q$. *Step 2, the source map* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.continuous_src⟧. $s$ is continuous: for open $U \subseteq O$ and $h \in s^{-1}(U)$, $V = U \cap V(o)$ satisfies $F_h(V) \subseteq s^{-1}(U)$ by (A). *Step 3, the sheet* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.sheet⟧. $F_h$ on $V(o)$ and $s$ on $F_h(V(o))$ are inverse to each other by (A), both continuous, with open domain and image (by (1)); so they form a partial homeomorphism. *Step 4* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isOpenEmbedding_restrict_map⟧. The restriction of $F_h$ to $V(o)$ is therefore an open embedding.

**Proof of (3).** *Step 1* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.t2Space⟧. Let $h_1 \neq h_2$. If $s(h_1) = s(h_2)$, (F) gives an open $V \ni s(h_1)$ in both domains with $F_{h_1}(V)$, $F_{h_2}(V)$ disjoint; these are open by (1) and contain $h_1$, $h_2$. *Step 2.* If $s(h_1) \neq s(h_2)$, separate the sources in the Hausdorff space $O$ and pull back by the continuous map $s$.

**Proposition 19.3.4 for every representative** ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_3_4_topology⟧. The statement is made for any representative $F$ of $\mathcal F$ with the topology of the class; by Lemma 19.3.1 this is the topology of $F$, and the steps above apply to $F$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text defines $T_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ by a representative and states Lemma 19.3.1 for its independence; the formal definition is on the quotient, which is the same thing. The partial homeomorphism of Step 3 is introduced here because it is what Proposition 19.3.5 and Lemma 19.3.8 use.

### XIX.7 Proposition 19.3.5, Remark 19.3.6, Proposition 19.3.7, Lemma 19.3.8, Theorem 19.3.3 (2017: Proposition 19.2, Remark 19.5, Theorem 19.2): the structure maps, pp. 873–879

**Statement** (same). *Proposition 19.3.5* (p. 875) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_3_5⟧: (1) $s$ and (2) $t$ are local homeomorphisms, (3) $u : O \to M(\Psi, \Psi)$ is continuous, (4) $\iota : M(\Psi, \Psi') \to M(\Psi', \Psi)$ is a homeomorphism, (5) $m$ is continuous on the fibred product. *Remark 19.3.6* (pp. 875–876): (a) $u$ is an open embedding ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.remark_19_3_6_a⟧, (b) $m$ is a local homeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.remark_19_3_6_b⟧. *Proposition 19.3.7* (p. 877, new in the 2021 edition) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_3_7⟧: $\bar\Psi : G \times O \to M(\Psi, \Psi)$ is a homeomorphism. *Lemma 19.3.8* (p. 878, new in the 2021 edition) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.lemma_19_3_8⟧: $T_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ is regular. *Theorem 19.3.3* (p. 873) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_3_3⟧: $T_{\Psi,\Psi'}$ is regular, Hausdorff and locally metrizable, with (1)–(6) as above.

**Proof of Proposition 19.3.5 (1), (2).** *Step 1, $s$* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isLocalHomeomorph_src⟧. At $h$, the inverse of the sheet of §XIX.6, Step 3 is a partial homeomorphism with source $F_h(V(o)) \ni h$ which is $s$ there. *Step 2, $\iota$* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.continuous_inv⟧. For $h \in M(\Psi, \Psi')$ and an open $W \ni \iota(h)$, the condition of $W$ at $\iota(h)$ gives an open $V' \ni t(h)$ with $F_{\iota(h)}(V') \subseteq W$. The shrinking step gives an open $N \ni s(h)$ with $f_h(N) \subseteq V'$, and by (D), $\iota(F_h(q)) = F_{\iota(h)}(f_h(q)) \in W$ for $q \in N$. So $\iota^{-1}(W)$ satisfies the condition at $h$, and $\iota$ is continuous. As $\iota \circ \iota = \mathrm{id}$, it is a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.invHomeomorph⟧. *Step 3, $t$* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isLocalHomeomorph_tgt⟧. $t = s \circ \iota$, a local homeomorphism composed with a homeomorphism.

**Proof of (3) and Remark 19.3.6 (a).** *Step 1* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.continuous_unit⟧. By (C), $u = F_{u(o)}$ on $V(o)$, which is continuous by Proposition 19.3.4 (2). *Step 2* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isOpenEmbedding_unit⟧. $u$ is injective, $s \circ u = \mathrm{id}$, and $u(U)$ is open for open $U$: near each $u(o)$ it is $F_{u(o)}(U \cap V(o))$, open by Proposition 19.3.4 (1). A continuous injective open map is an open embedding.

**Proof of (5) and Remark 19.3.6 (b).** *Step 1, the fibred product* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.FibProd⟧, ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.mulMap⟧. $M(\Psi', \Psi'')\, {}_s\!\times_t M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is the subspace of pairs $(h', h)$ with $s(h') = t(h)$, and $m(h', h)$ is the composite. *Step 2, the neighbourhood* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.mulNbhdDom⟧. For $(h', h)$, let $N = V(m(h', h)) \cap V(o) \cap f_h^{-1}(V(o'))$, open and containing $o$. The set of pairs $(F_{h'}(f_h(q)), F_h(q))$, $q \in N$ ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.mulNbhd⟧, is an open neighbourhood of $(h', h)$ in the fibred product ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.mulNbhd_spec⟧. *Step 3.* On it, by (E), $m(F_{h'}(f_h(q)), F_h(q)) = F_{m(h', h)}(q)$ ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.mulMap_eq_of_mem⟧. So near $(h', h)$, $m$ is $F_{m(h',h)} \circ s$, continuous ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.continuous_mulMap⟧. *Step 4.* The same formula shows that $m$ maps this neighbourhood homeomorphically onto $F_{m(h', h)}(N)$, with inverse $a \mapsto (F_{h'}(f_h(s a)), F_h(s a))$ ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.mulNbhdHomeomorph⟧; so $m$ is a local homeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isLocalHomeomorph_mulMap⟧.

**Proof of Proposition 19.3.7 (point G.259).** The text calls the continuity of $\bar\Psi$ clear; it is proved.

*Step 1, bijectivity.* $\bar\Psi$ is a bijection $G \times O \to M(\Psi, \Psi)$, since $\Psi$ is fully faithful and injective on objects (Chapter 17). *Step 2, continuity* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.hatHomeomorph⟧. $G$ is discrete, so it suffices that $q \mapsto \bar\Psi(g, q)$ is continuous for each $g$. By (A), $F_{\bar\Psi(g, o)}(q) = \bar\Psi(g, q)$ on $V(o)$, continuous by Proposition 19.3.4 (2). *Step 3, the inverse.* The inverse is $a \mapsto (g_a, s(a))$, where $g_a$ is the group element of $a$. $s$ is continuous, and $g_a$ is locally constant: near $a = \bar\Psi(g, o)$ the open set $F_a(V(o))$ consists of elements $\bar\Psi(g, q)$, by (A). *Step 4* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.hatHomeomorph_apply⟧: the homeomorphism is $\bar\Psi$.

**Proof of Lemma 19.3.8 (point G.260).** *Step 1, an abstract lemma* ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.regularSpace_of_isLocalHomeomorph_of_t2⟧. A Hausdorff space $X$ with a local homeomorphism $e : X \to Y$ into a regular space is regular. Let $x$ have a neighbourhood $s$; choose a partial homeomorphism $e$ at $x$ and set $W = e.\mathrm{source} \cap \mathrm{interior}(s)$. By regularity of $Y$, choose a closed neighbourhood $C$ of $e(x)$ inside $e(W)$, and set $N = W \cap e^{-1}(C)$. *Step 2.* The closure of $N$ stays in $W$. Let $y \in \overline N$. Then $e(y) \in \overline{e(N)} \subseteq C \subseteq e(W)$, so $e(y) = e(w)$ for some $w \in W$. If $y \neq w$, separate them by open $P \ni y$, $Q \ni w$ in the Hausdorff space $X$; the set $P \cap e^{-1}(e(Q \cap W))$ is an open neighbourhood of $y$, so it meets $N$ in some $z$, and $e(z) = e(q)$ with $q \in Q \cap W$. Injectivity of $e$ on its source gives $q = z$, so $z \in P \cap Q$, which is empty. Hence $y = w \in W$. *Step 3.* $\overline N$ is a closed neighbourhood of $x$ inside $s$. *Step 4* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.regularSpace⟧. $s : M(\Psi, \Psi') \to O$ is a local homeomorphism (Proposition 19.3.5 (1)), $O$ is metrizable hence regular, and $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is Hausdorff (Proposition 19.3.4 (3)).

**Proof of Theorem 19.3.3.** *Step 1, local metrizability* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.exists_isOpen_metrizableSpace⟧. The source of a partial homeomorphism of $s$ at $h$ is homeomorphic to an open subset of the metrizable $O$ ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.exists_isOpen_metrizableSpace_of_isLocalHomeomorph⟧. *Step 2* ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_3_3⟧. Regularity is Lemma 19.3.8, Hausdorff is Proposition 19.3.4 (3), (1)–(5) are Proposition 19.3.5, and (6) is Proposition 19.3.7.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text proves Lemma 19.3.8 with sequences and a choice of $W(h)$; the neighbourhood argument above needs neither, only the Hausdorff property and one sheet of $s$, and it is a general fact used again in §19.6. The text proves the continuity of $m$ in Proposition 19.3.5 (5) and the local homeomorphism property in Remark 19.3.6 (b) separately; both come from the one neighbourhood of Step 2. The order of the text (Theorem 19.3.3 stated first, proved by the later items) is reversed in the formal proof.

### XIX.8 Definition 19.4.1, Proposition 19.4.2 (2017: Definition 19.8, Proposition 19.4): the properness conditions, pp. 879–881

**Statement.** *Definition 19.4.1* (p. 879; same): at every $\Psi$ and $o$ there is an open $U \ni o$ such that (v1) for $h_k \in M(\Psi, \Psi')$ with $s(h_k) \in U$ and $t(h_k)$ convergent, a subsequence of $h_k$ converges ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.PropernessV1⟧; (v2) $\overline{|\Psi(U)|} \subseteq |\Psi(O)|$ ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.PropernessV2⟧; (v3) for $q_k \in U$ with $|\Psi(q_k)|$ convergent, a subsequence of $q_k$ converges in $\overline U$ ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.PropernessV3⟧. The read form (v3) asks moreover that the limit $p$ satisfy $|\Psi(p)| = z$, the limit in the fibre ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.PropernessV3Lim⟧. *Proposition 19.4.2* (p. 880; restated) ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_4_2⟧: (v1) ⇔ (v2) ⇔ (v3) with the limit.

**The orbit-chart lemmas.** An orbit chart of a finite group $G$ acting on $O$ is a continuous open map $\pi : O \to Z$ whose fibres are the $G$-orbits ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.IsOrbitChart⟧; the quotient map of an action is one ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.isOrbitChart_quotientMk⟧, and so is $q \mapsto |\Psi(q)|$ for $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ (§XIX.4) ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.isOrbitChart_unif⟧. (v2) and (v3) are defined for an orbit chart at a point ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.OrbitChartV2⟧, ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.OrbitChartV3⟧, ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · Chapter19.OrbitChartV3Lim⟧, and (v1) for a pair of maps $s$, $t$ ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.TransitionV1⟧. An orbit chart induces a homeomorphism of $G \backslash O$ onto its image ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.IsOrbitChart.homeomorph⟧, so the image is Hausdorff ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.IsOrbitChart.t2Space_range⟧ (separate $g x$ from $y$ for each of the finitely many $g$ and intersect) and metrizable when $O$ is ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.IsOrbitChart.metrizableSpace_range⟧.

*Step 1, (v2) gives (v3) with the limit* ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · Chapter19.IsOrbitChart.tendsto_subseq_of_closure_subset_lim⟧, ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · Chapter19.IsOrbitChart.orbitChartV3Lim_of_v2⟧. Let $U$ be given by (v2), with the same $U$ for (v3). For $q_k \in U$ with $\pi(q_k) \to z$, $z \in \overline{\pi(U)} \subseteq \pi(O)$, say $z = \pi(\bar q)$. Since $\pi$ is open and the fibre over $z$ is the finite orbit $G \bar q$, for one $g$ the point $\bar q$ is a cluster point of $g q_k$, by finiteness of $G$; $O$ being first countable, a subsequence of $g q_k$ converges to $\bar q$, so the subsequence of $q_k$ converges to $g^{-1} \bar q$ ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.IsOrbitChart.tendsto_subseq_of_closure_subset⟧. The limit lies in $\overline U$ and has $\pi(g^{-1} \bar q) = z$. The version without the limit condition follows ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.IsOrbitChart.orbitChartV3_of_v2⟧, ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · Chapter19.OrbitChartV3Lim.orbitChartV3⟧.

*Step 2, (v3) with the limit gives (v2)* ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · Chapter19.orbitChartV2_of_v3Lim⟧. If $Z$ is first countable, a point $z \in \overline{\pi(U)}$ is a limit of $\pi(q_k)$ with $q_k \in U$; (v3) with the limit gives $p$ with $\pi(p) = z$, so $z \in \pi(O)$.

*Step 3, (v3) as printed and Hausdorff* ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.IsOrbitChart.orbitChartV2_of_v3⟧. If $Z$ is Hausdorff and Fréchet–Urysohn, (v3) as printed gives (v2): the subsequence $q_{k_j} \to p$ gives $\pi(q_{k_j}) \to \pi(p)$ and also $\to z$, so $\pi(p) = z$ by uniqueness of limits.

*Step 4, regular spaces and local compactness.* For a regular $Z$, (v2) holds at every point ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.IsOrbitChart.orbitChartV2_of_regularSpace⟧. If $O$ is locally compact, (v3) as printed holds: choose $U$ with compact closure, and sequential compactness of the metrizable $\overline U$ gives the subsequence ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · Chapter19.orbitChartV3_of_locallyCompactSpace⟧.

**Proof of Proposition 19.4.2.** *Step 1, the sequence lemma* ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.exists_tendsto_subseq_of_tendsto_src⟧. If $a \in M(\Psi, \Psi')$ and $h_k \in M(\Psi, \Psi')$ with $s(h_k) \to s(a)$, a subsequence of $h_k$ converges to an element with source $s(a)$. Proof: let $N$ be the intersection of the neighbourhoods of §XIX.7 (proof of (5), Step 2) for the pairs $(\bar\Psi'(g, t(a)), a)$, $g \in G'$, finitely many. For large $k$, $s(h_k) \in N$, and $h_k$ and $F_a(s(h_k))$ have the same source, so by fullness of $\Psi'$, $h_k = m(\bar\Psi'(g_k, t F_a(s h_k)), F_a(s h_k))$ for some $g_k \in G'$. Along a subsequence $g_k = g$ is constant, and then (E) with the special form of (A) gives $h_k = F_m(s(h_k))$ for $m = m(\bar\Psi'(g, t(a)), a)$, which has source $s(a)$; the continuity of $F_m$ gives $h_k \to m$. No properness is used. *Step 2, (v1) gives (v3) with the limit* ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.propernessV3Lim_of_v1⟧. Let $U$ be given by (v1), $q_k \in U$ with $|\Psi(q_k)| \to z$. By §XIX.4, $z = |\Psi'(p')|$ for a uniformizer $\Psi'$ near which $|\Psi'|$ is an open embedding, and with an antitone neighbourhood basis of $p'$ one chooses $p_k \to p'$ with $|\Psi'(p_k)| = |\Psi(q_k)|$ along a subsequence, hence elements $(q_k, \mu_k, p_k) \in M(\Psi, \Psi')$. (v1) gives a convergent subsequence with limit $a$; $s(a) = \lim q_k \in \overline U$ and $t(a) = p'$, so $|\Psi(s(a))| = z$. *Step 3, (v3) with the limit and (v2)* ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.propernessV2_of_v3Lim⟧, ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.propernessV3Lim_of_v2⟧. By the orbit-chart Steps 1 and 2, using that $T$ is first countable (Theorem 19.2.2 (4)). *Step 4, (v3) with the limit gives (v1)* ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.propernessV1_of_v3Lim⟧. For $h_k$ with $s(h_k) \in U$ and $t(h_k) \to p$, $|\Psi(s h_k)| = |\Psi'(t h_k)| \to |\Psi'(p)|$; (v3) with the limit gives a subsequence $s(h_k) \to q$ with $|\Psi(q)| = |\Psi'(p)|$, hence an element $a = (q, \mu, p)$, and the sequence lemma of Step 1 gives a convergent subsequence of $h_k$. *Step 5* ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_4_2_one_iff_two⟧, ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_4_2⟧. Combining Steps 2–4.

**(v3) as printed.** *Step 1* ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_4_2_two_three⟧: (v2) implies (v3) as printed (orbit-chart Step 1). *Step 2* ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_4_2_three_two_hyp⟧: if $T$ is Hausdorff, (v3) as printed implies (v2) (orbit-chart Step 3, $T$ being first countable). The hypothesis $hT$ has the witness of the reflection construction ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.reflectionBasic_t2Space⟧, non-trivial isotropy. *Step 3* ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.propernessV3_of_locallyCompactSpace⟧: if all domains are locally compact, (v3) as printed holds. *Step 4, the glued lines* ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.gluedBasic_propernessV3⟧, ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.gluedBasic_not_propernessV2⟧, ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.proposition_19_4_2_v3_without_limit⟧. The basic construction of Remark 19.2.3 has locally compact domains, so (v3) as printed holds; (v2) does not, since $|(0, -1)|$ lies in the closure of $|\Psi_+(U)|$ for every $U \ni 0$ but not in the image of $\Psi_+$ (a positive point near $0$ exists in every open set containing $0$ ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.exists_pos_mem_of_isOpen⟧); and (v1) does not either ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.gluedBasic_not_propernessV1⟧.

*Readings.* (v3) as printed is not equivalent to (v1) and (v2) as we read it, by Step 4: the text's proof of (3) ⇒ (2) uses that the limit lies in the fibre over $z$, which the printed (v3) does not ask. Proposition 19.4.2 is therefore proved with (v3) read with the limit, and the printed (v3) is related to it under the Hausdorff condition or local compactness (Findings, Chapter 19, item 3).

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text proves the equivalences with sequences throughout; the formal proof separates the general orbit-chart facts from the one point specific to transition sets, the sequence lemma, which also carries Lemma 19.4.3 and Proposition 19.4.4. The text's route to the Hausdorff property through (v1), the closedness of $\{(s(h), t(h))\}$, is kept as a general lemma ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.isClosed_range_of_transitionV1⟧; the formal proof of Proposition 19.4.5 goes through (v2) instead (§XIX.10).

### XIX.9 Lemma 19.4.3, Proposition 19.4.4 (2017: Proposition 19.3): sheets and metrizability of the transition sets, pp. 881–884

**Statement.** *Lemma 19.4.3* (p. 881, new in the 2021 edition; narrower) ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.lemma_19_4_3_hyp⟧: for $o \in O$ that is the source of some element of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$, there are a finite set $H$ of elements with source $o$, an open $U(o) \ni o$ and open $U(h) \ni h$, $h \in H$, such that $s$ maps each $U(h)$ homeomorphically onto $U(o)$ and every element with source in $U(o)$ lies in some $U(h)$. *Proposition 19.4.4* (p. 883; stronger) ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_4_4⟧: $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is metrizable, without the properness condition ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.metrizableSpace_M⟧.

**Proof of Lemma 19.4.3 at sources (point G.261).** *Step 1, the finite set.* $H = \{h : s(h) = o\}$ is finite ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.finite_fiber⟧, since by full faithfulness it is in bijection with a finite group; by the hypothesis it is non-empty. *Step 2, a neighbourhood with no stray elements* ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.exists_nbhd_eq_map⟧. There is an open $N \ni o$ such that every element with source in $N$ is $F_b(s(\cdot))$ of itself for some $b \in H$. Otherwise, with an antitone basis $N_k$ of $o$, there are $h_k$ with $s(h_k) \in N_k$ not of this form. Since $s(h_k) \to o = s(a)$ for some $a$ with source $o$, the sequence lemma (§XIX.8, proof of Proposition 19.4.2, Step 1) gives a subsequence converging to some $m \in H$; it eventually lies in the open neighbourhood $F_m(V(o))$ of $m$, so $h_k = F_m(s(h_k))$, which contradicts the choice. This is the text's first exercise. *Step 3, the sheets.* Set $U(o) = N \cap \bigcap_{b \in H} V(b)$, open, finite intersection, and $U(b) = F_b(U(o))$. By Proposition 19.3.4, $U(b)$ is open and $s$ maps it homeomorphically onto $U(o)$, and Step 2 gives the covering property. This is the text's second exercise.

**Lemma 19.4.3 at a point that is not a source.** *Step 1, the example* ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.expBasic⟧. On the trivial group acting on $\mathbb R$, take two uniformizers $\Psi_0$ (the identity) and $\Psi_e$ ($\Psi_e(t) = e^t$) of the groupoidal category of points of $\mathbb R$ with identity morphisms ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.lineUnif⟧, ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.expΨ₀⟧, ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.expΨₑ⟧, with $F(x) = \{\Psi_0\}$ for $x \leq 0$ and $\{\Psi_0, \Psi_e\}$ for $x > 0$ ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.expU⟧, and the transitions $q \mapsto (q, \cdot, \log q)$ or $e^q$ ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.expData⟧. Every class has a uniformizer ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.expBasic_hB⟧, $T$ is the topology of $\mathbb R$ and metrizable ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.expBasic_metrizableSpace⟧, and (v2) holds ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.expBasic_propernessV2⟧. *Step 2* ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.expBasic_not_lemma_19_4_3⟧. At $o = 0$ in the domain of $\Psi_0$, no element of $M(\Psi_0, \Psi_e)$ has source $0$, since $0 \notin$ range of $\exp$. If $H$, $U(o)$, $U(h)$ were as in the lemma, a $t > 0$ in $U(o)$ is a source, so its element lies in some $U(h')$ with $h' \in H$, and then $s(U(h')) = U(o) \ni 0$ makes $0$ a source. *Step 3* ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.not_lemma_19_4_3⟧. So the lemma, read with $H$ a set of elements with source $o$ and $s(U(h)) = U(o)$ for every $h \in H$, does not hold at $0$, though every neighbourhood of $0$ contains a source and the properness condition holds.

*Readings.* The lemma does not hold as we read it at a point that is not a source; the text's proof chooses $h_0$ "if it exists", which leaves this case open (Findings, Chapter 19, item 4). The hypothesis that $o$ is a source has the witness ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.expBasic_exists_trans⟧, weak: the isotropy is trivial, though the two uniformizers are different. The form at sources needs no properness.

**Proof of Proposition 19.4.4.** *Step 1, restriction to the image of $s$* ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · Chapter19.isLocalHomeomorph_codRestrict⟧. $s$ corestricted to its image $s(M) \subseteq O$ is still a local homeomorphism, the image being open. Every point of $s(M)$ is a source, so Lemma 19.4.3 applies at every point of it. *Step 2, finite sheets* ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.paracompactSpace_of_isLocalHomeomorph_of_finiteSheets⟧. An abstract lemma: if $e : X \to Y$ is a local homeomorphism into a paracompact Hausdorff space and every $y$ has a neighbourhood $V$ whose preimage is covered by finitely many open sets on which $e$ is injective, then $X$ is paracompact. Its proof shrinks the neighbourhoods, takes a precise locally finite refinement in $Y$, and pulls it back sheet by sheet. *Step 3* ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.metrizableSpace_of_isLocalHomeomorph_of_finiteSheets⟧. If moreover $Y$ is metrizable, $X$ is paracompact, Hausdorff and locally metrizable, hence metrizable by Smirnov's theorem in Chapter 2's form ⟦Chapter2/Metrizable · metrizableSpace_of_locally⟧. *Step 4* ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.metrizableSpace⟧. Apply Steps 2–3 to $s : M(\Psi, \Psi') \to s(M)$, an open subset of the metrizable $O$, with the sheets $F_b(U(o))$ of Lemma 19.4.3; $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is Hausdorff by Proposition 19.3.4 (3). *Step 5* ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_4_4⟧. The printed statement takes (v2), which the proof does not use.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text proves Proposition 19.4.4 by refining over all $o \in O$ with the sets of Lemma 19.4.3 and cites the Nagata–Smirnov theorem; the formal proof restricts to the image of $s$, where every point is a source, and uses Smirnov's theorem for paracompact locally metrizable spaces. The 2017 Proposition 19.3 had no properness hypothesis, and the formal statement agrees with it (Findings, Chapter 19, item 5).

### XIX.10 Proposition 19.4.5, Theorem 19.4.6, Proposition 19.4.7, Corollary 19.4.8 (2017: Propositions 19.5, 19.6, Theorem 19.3): the metrization criteria, pp. 884–885

**Statement** (same). *Proposition 19.4.5* (p. 884) ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_4_5⟧: under (v2), $T$ is Hausdorff and completely regular. *Theorem 19.4.6* (p. 885): under (v2), $T$ is metrizable if it is second countable ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_4_6_one⟧ or paracompact ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_4_6_two⟧. *Proposition 19.4.7* (p. 885) ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_4_7⟧: $T$ is metrizable if and only if (v2) holds and $T$ is paracompact. *Corollary 19.4.8* (p. 885, new in the 2021 edition) ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.corollary_19_4_8⟧: if $T$ is metrizable, every $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is metrizable.

**Proof of Proposition 19.4.5 (point G.262).** *Step 1, the charts cover* ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.exists_unif_π_eq⟧. Every $z$ is $|\Psi(o)|$ for some uniformizer of the construction, by the reading $F(\alpha) \neq \emptyset$. *Step 2, Hausdorff* ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.t2Space_of_forall_closure_subset_t2⟧, ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.t2Space_of_orbitCharts⟧. A space is Hausdorff if every point $z$ has an open Hausdorff neighbourhood $W$ and an open neighbourhood $N$ with $\overline N \subseteq W$: for $z_2 \neq z_1$, either $z_2 \in W$ and the two are separated in $W$, or $z_2 \notin \overline N$ and $N$, the complement of $\overline N$ separate. For $z_1 = \pi(o_1)$, take $W = \pi(O)$, open and Hausdorff (§XIX.8), and $N = \pi(U)$ with $U$ from (v2) at $o_1$, so $\overline N \subseteq \pi(O)$. *Step 3, an invariant Urysohn function* ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.exists_invariant_urysohn⟧. On the metrizable $O$ with a finite group action, for a $G$-invariant open $V \ni \bar o$, choose by Urysohn $\beta_0 : O \to [0, 1]$ vanishing on the finite closed orbit $G \bar o$ and equal to $1$ off $V$, and average over $G$: $\beta = \frac1{|G|}\sum_g \beta_0(g\,\cdot)$ is $G$-invariant, vanishes at $\bar o$ and is $1$ off $V$. A $\beta_0$ vanishing only at $\bar o$ would not do, since its average at $\bar o$ is the mean of $\beta_0$ over the orbit. This is the averaging the text leaves to the reader. *Step 4, complete regularity* ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.IsOrbitChart.exists_urysohn⟧, ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.completelyRegularSpace_of_orbitCharts⟧. For $z$ and a closed $A \not\ni z$, take a chart $\pi$ at $z = \pi(\bar o)$ with (v2) at $\bar o$ and $U$ with $\overline{\pi(U)} \subseteq \pi(O)$; let $V$ be the $G$-saturation of $U \setminus \pi^{-1}(A)$ and $\beta$ as in Step 3. $\beta$ descends to $\pi(O)$, continuously since $\pi$ is open, and is extended by $1$ outside $\pi(O)$; the extension is continuous because it equals $1$ off $\overline{\pi(V)}$, which lies inside the open set $\pi(O)$ by (v2). It vanishes at $z$ and is $1$ on $A$. *Step 5* ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_4_5⟧: the charts of §XIX.8 satisfy the hypotheses.

**Proof of Theorem 19.4.6.** *Step 1, second countable* ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.metrizableSpace_of_orbitCharts_secondCountable⟧. A completely regular Hausdorff second countable space is metrizable (Urysohn). *Step 2, paracompact* ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.metrizableSpace_of_orbitCharts_paracompact⟧. A paracompact Hausdorff locally metrizable space is metrizable (Smirnov), with local metrizability from Theorem 19.2.2 (5). *Step 3* ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_4_6_one⟧, ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_4_6_two⟧: Proposition 19.4.5 supplies the Hausdorff and complete regularity hypotheses.

**Proof of Proposition 19.4.7.** *Step 1, ⇐* by Theorem 19.4.6 (2). *Step 2, ⇒* ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.paracompact_and_orbitChartV2_of_metrizableSpace⟧. A metrizable space is paracompact (Stone) and regular; for an orbit chart into a regular space (v2) holds at every point (§XIX.8, Step 4). *Step 3* ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_4_7⟧.

**Proof of Corollary 19.4.8.** ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.corollary_19_4_8⟧. By Proposition 19.4.7, metrizability gives (v2), and Proposition 19.4.4 applies; since Proposition 19.4.4 holds without (v2) (§XIX.9), the corollary holds without its hypothesis. The metrizability of $T$ is also obtained from (v2) and a surjective family of charts in a single statement ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.metrizableSpace_natTop_of_surjective⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text derives the Hausdorff property in Proposition 19.4.5 from (v1); the formal proof uses (v2), equivalent by Proposition 19.4.2, and an argument on closures. The Urysohn function is built in one chart and extended by $1$, the averaging of Step 3 being what makes it descend.

### XIX.11 Theorem 19.5.1, Corollary 19.5.2 (2017: Theorem 19.4, Corollary 19.1): the M-polyfold structures on the transition sets, pp. 886–887

**Statement** (stronger, same). *Theorem 19.5.1* (p. 886) ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_5_1⟧: every $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ carries a natural M-polyfold structure ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · BasicConstruction.transPolyfold⟧ for which $s$ and $t$ are local sc-diffeomorphisms, the maps $F_h : V(o) \to F_h(V(o))$ are sc-diffeomorphisms with inverse $s$, and $\bar\Psi : G \times O \to M(\Psi, \Psi)$ is an sc-diffeomorphism. The printed hypothesis that $T$ is metrizable is kept and not used. "Natural" ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_5_1_natural⟧ is read as: (i) it is the only M-polyfold structure on $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ for which $s$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism; (ii) the structure of any representative of $\mathcal F$ agrees with it. *Corollary 19.5.2* (p. 887) ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · BasicConstruction.corollary_19_5_2⟧: $u$, $\iota$ and $m$ are sc-smooth, $m$ on the fibred product.

**Proof of Theorem 19.5.1.** *Step 1, the sheet space* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.SheetSpace⟧, ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.sheetPolyfold⟧. The disjoint union $\Sigma_h V(o_h)$ of the domains, an M-polyfold as a disjoint union of open subsets of the $O$'s. *Step 2, the sheet map* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.sheetMap⟧. $(h, q) \mapsto F_h(q)$ is continuous ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.continuous_sheetMap⟧ and open ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isOpenMap_sheetMap⟧ by Proposition 19.3.4, surjective ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.surjective_sheetMap⟧ since $F_h(o) = h$, and injective on each sheet ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.injOn_sheetMap⟧ since $s \circ F_h = \mathrm{id}$. *Step 3, descent* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isDescentMap_sheetMap⟧. Chapter 9's descent ⟦Chapter9/DescentPolyfold · MPolyfold.IsDescentMap⟧ asks moreover that the transitions between sheets be sc-smooth. Where $F_{h_1}(q_1) = F_{h_2}(q_2) = k$, applying $s$ gives $q_1 = q_2 = q$, and (B) gives $F_{h_1} = F_k = F_{h_2}$ near $q$; so the transition between the two sheets is the identity of $O$ near $q$. *Step 4, the structure* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.transPolyfold⟧. The descended M-polyfold structure ⟦Chapter9/DescentPolyfold · MPolyfold.descend⟧, for which the sheet map is a local sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isLocalScDiffeo_sheetMap⟧. *Step 5, $s$ and $t$* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isLocalScDiffeo_sheetSrc⟧, ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isLocalScDiffeo_sheetTgt⟧, ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isLocalScDiffeo_src⟧, ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isLocalScDiffeo_tgt⟧. On the sheet space $s \circ \mathrm{sheetMap}$ is the inclusion of $V(o_h)$ and $t \circ \mathrm{sheetMap}$ is $f_h$, both local sc-diffeomorphisms by (A). Since the sheet map is a surjective local sc-diffeomorphism, $s$ and $t$ are local sc-diffeomorphisms ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · MPolyfold.IsLocalScDiffeo.of_comp_surjective⟧. *Step 6, the maps $F_h$* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isScDiffeoOn_map⟧. $F_h$ is the sheet map on the sheet $h$, an sc-diffeomorphism onto its open image, with inverse $s$. *Step 7, $\bar\Psi$* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isScDiffeoOn_hatHomeomorph⟧. $s \circ \bar\Psi$ is the projection $G \times O \to O$, a local sc-diffeomorphism (Chapter 17's translation groupoid); a homeomorphism whose composite with a local sc-diffeomorphism is one is an sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isScDiffeoOn_of_isLocalScDiffeo_src_comp⟧.

**Proof of naturality.** *Step 1, the key lemma* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isScDiffeoOn_of_isLocalScDiffeo_src_comp⟧. If $Q$ is an M-polyfold structure on a space $X$ and $e : X \to M(\Psi, \Psi')$ a homeomorphism with $s \circ e$ a local sc-diffeomorphism, then $e$ is an sc-diffeomorphism: locally $e = (s|_{\text{sheet}})^{-1} \circ (s \circ e)$, a composite of local sc-diffeomorphisms (Chapter 8's lemma ⟦Chapter8/TangentFibred · MPolyfold.isLocalScDiffeo_of_comp_eq⟧). *Step 2, uniqueness* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isScDiffeoOn_id_of_isLocalScDiffeo_src⟧. With $e = \mathrm{id}$. *Step 3, independence of the representative* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.equivHomeomorph⟧, ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isScDiffeoOn_of_equiv⟧. For $F \sim F'$, the identity is a homeomorphism (Lemma 19.3.1), and $s$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism for the structure of $F'$; Step 1 applies. *Step 4* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_5_1_natural⟧.

**Proof of Corollary 19.5.2.** *Step 1, a criterion* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · MPolyfold.isScSmoothMap_of_isLocalScDiffeo_comp⟧. A continuous map $f$ into a space with a local sc-diffeomorphism $e$ is sc-smooth if $e \circ f$ is. *Step 2, $u$* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isScSmoothMap_unit⟧: $s \circ u = \mathrm{id}$. *Step 3, $\iota$* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isScSmoothMap_inv⟧: $s \circ \iota = t$, sc-smooth by Theorem 19.5.1. *Step 4, $m$* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isScSmoothMap_mul⟧: the fibred product is a sub-M-polyfold of the product since $s$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism, and $s \circ m = s \circ \mathrm{pr}_2$. *Step 5* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · BasicConstruction.corollary_19_5_2⟧.

*Readings.* The metrizability of $T$ is not used: the structure is built on $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ with $T_{\Psi,\Psi'}$, which is Hausdorff and metrizable without it (Theorem 19.3.3, Proposition 19.4.4). In particular the transition sets of Remark 19.2.3 carry these structures (point G.258). The witness of the kept hypothesis is ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.reflectionBasic_metrizableSpace⟧ (Findings, Chapter 19, item 6).

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text defines the M-polyfold structure by the atlas of the maps $s|_{F_h(V(o))}$ and leaves its naturality informal; the formal structure is the descended one, whose charts are those maps, and "natural" is made precise as uniqueness and independence.

### XIX.12 Theorem 19.5.3, Remark 19.5.4 (2017: Theorem 19.5, Remark 19.7): the polyfold structure, p. 888

**Statement** (same). *Theorem 19.5.3* (p. 888): (1) under (v2), if $T$ is second countable or paracompact, it is metrizable, and $(\mathcal S, T)$ is a GCT ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureGCT · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_5_3_one⟧, ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureGCT · BasicConstruction.toGCTOfMetrizable⟧; (2) if $T$ is metrizable, $F$ and the structures of Theorem 19.5.1 form a polyfold construction ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureGCT · BasicConstruction.toPolyfoldConstruction⟧, the footprints cover $|\mathcal S|$, $\hat\Psi$ is an sc-diffeomorphism, and if the domains are tame the construction, every $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ and every model are tame ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureGCT · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_5_3_two⟧. *Remark 19.5.4* (p. 888) ⟦Chapter19/CompactNeighbourhoods · BasicConstruction.remark_19_5_4⟧: under (v2), $T$ is Hausdorff, completely regular and locally metrizable, every compact $K$ has an open neighbourhood that is metrizable and paracompact, and the conclusion of Theorem 19.5.3 holds when $T$ is second countable or paracompact.

**Proof of Theorem 19.5.3 (point G.263).** The text proves the theorem by "the discussion of this section"; the formal proof assembles it.

*Step 1, (1)* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureGCT · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_5_3_one⟧: Theorem 19.4.6 (1) or (2). *Step 2, local uniformizers* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureGCT · BasicConstruction.toLocalUniformizer⟧. Every $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$ is a local uniformizer of the GCT in the sense of Chapter 17: $|\Psi|$ is a homeomorphism onto an open set by Theorem 19.2.2 (3). *Step 3, the construction* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureGCT · BasicConstruction.gctF⟧, ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureGCT · BasicConstruction.toUnif⟧, ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureGCT · BasicConstruction.ofUnif⟧, ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureGCT · BasicConstruction.toPolyfoldConstruction⟧. The uniformizer sets are the $F(\alpha)$ as local uniformizers; the transition M-polyfolds are those of Theorem 19.5.1 on $T_{\Psi,\Psi'}$, which is Hausdorff (Proposition 19.3.4 (3)) and paracompact, being metrizable (Proposition 19.4.4); $s$, $t$ are local sc-diffeomorphisms (Theorem 19.5.1); $u$, $\iota$, $m$ are sc-smooth (Corollary 19.5.2). *Step 4, covering* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureGCT · BasicConstruction.hasCoveringUniformizers_toPolyfoldConstruction⟧, ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureGCT · BasicConstruction.coveringFamily⟧. Every class meets an $\alpha$ with $F(\alpha) \neq \emptyset$. *Step 5, $\hat\Psi$* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureGCT · BasicConstruction.continuous_hat⟧. $\hat\Psi : G \ltimes O \to M(\Psi, \Psi)$ is continuous, being $\bar\Psi$ (Proposition 19.3.7); so Chapter 17's Lemma 17.1.9 in the form with $\hat\Psi$ continuous applies ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.lemma_17_1_9_of_continuous⟧, and $\hat\Psi$ is an sc-diffeomorphism. *Step 6, tameness* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.isTame_transPolyfold⟧. If the domains are tame, each $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is tame, since $s$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism into a tame M-polyfold; the models are tame by Chapter 17 ⟦Chapter17/ModelEpGroupoid · isTame_modelEpGroupoid⟧. *Step 7* ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureGCT · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_5_3_two⟧. The model functor is an equivalence (Lemma 17.1.15) ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureGCT · BasicConstruction.isEquivalence_modelFunctor⟧, and the comparison of two covering families is a generalized isomorphism (Theorem 17.1.18) ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureGCT · BasicConstruction.isGeneralizedIso_comparison⟧, so the polyfold structure does not depend on the covering family.

**Proof of Remark 19.5.4 (point G.264).** *Step 1, a bump* ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmasTwo · Chapter19.exists_bump_of_isOpen⟧.
