In a completely regular space, for $x$ in an open $W$ there is a continuous $\rho \geq 0$ with $\rho(x) > 0$ and support inside $W$: with a Urysohn function $f$, $f(x) = 0$, $f = 1$ off $W$, take $\rho = \max(1/2 - f, 0)$. *Step 2* ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmasTwo · Chapter19.exists_isOpen_metrizableSpace_of_isCompact⟧. In a completely regular, locally metrizable space, cover a compact $K$ by finitely many sets $\{\rho_i > 0\}$ with $\rho_i$ supported in metrizable open sets $W_i$; their union $U$ is metrizable by the partition criterion of Chapter 2 ⟦Chapter2/Metrizable · metrizableSpace_of_partition⟧, applied to the functions $\rho_i$ on $U$, and paracompact as a metrizable space (Stone). *Step 3* ⟦Chapter19/CompactNeighbourhoods · BasicConstruction.remark_19_5_4⟧. Proposition 19.4.5 and Theorem 19.2.2 (5) give the hypotheses of Step 2, and Theorem 19.5.3 gives the last clause.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text's Theorem 19.5.3 states the polyfold construction; the formal statement lists its parts, including the tameness of every transition set and every model, which the text implies by "tame". The last sentence of Remark 19.5.4, that such a neighbourhood is sometimes all one needs, is prose.

### XIX.13 Definitions 19.5.5, 19.5.6, Proposition 19.5.7: (ZHOU) for basic constructions, p. 889

**Statement** (same). *Definition 19.5.5* (p. 889, new in the 2021 edition) ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · BasicConstruction.definition_19_5_5⟧: for $g$ in the isotropy of $o$ and a level-$1$ point $x_1$ over $o$, the tangent action $Tg$ maps the tangent fibre at $x_1$ to itself, is inverted by $Tg^{-1}$, and is the tangent of the morphism $(g, o)$ of the translation groupoid ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.toEP⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.tangentSmul⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.tangentMor_ep_eq⟧. *Definition 19.5.6* (p. 889, new in the 2021 edition) ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · BasicConstruction.IsZhou⟧: $(F, \mathcal F)$ satisfies (ZHOU) if every class of a smooth object ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · BasicConstruction.IsSmoothClass⟧ has a uniformizer $\Psi$ and a smooth $y$ with (ZHOU) at $y$ for $G \ltimes O$ ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.IsZhouAt⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.isZhouAt_iff⟧. *Proposition 19.5.7* (p. 889, new in the 2021 edition) ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_5_7⟧: if $T$ is metrizable and $(F, \mathcal F)$ satisfies (ZHOU), so does the polyfold of Theorem 19.5.3.

**Proof of Definition 19.5.5.** *Step 1.* The translation groupoid $G \ltimes O$ is an ep-groupoid (Chapter 17), whose morphism $(g, o)$ with $g o = o$ has tangent map $Tg$ on $T_{x_1}O$. *Step 2* ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · BasicConstruction.definition_19_5_5⟧. $Tg$ preserves the fibre by $g o = o$, $Tg^{-1} \circ Tg = T(g^{-1}g) = \mathrm{id}$ by the chain rule, and the tangent of $(g, o)$ is $Tg$ by the action formulas of Chapter 7's examples ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · EpGroupoid.Examples.action_tangentMor_eq⟧.

**The pointwise forms.** (ZHOU) and (LR) of Chapter 7 are properties of all points; they are split into pointwise forms ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · EpGroupoid.IsZhouAt⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · EpGroupoid.isZhou_iff_forall_isZhouAt⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · EpGroupoid.IsLRAt⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · EpGroupoid.isLR_iff_forall_isLRAt⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularityPointwise · EpGroupoid.IsLUCAt⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularityPointwise · EpGroupoid.isLUC_iff_forall_isLUCAt⟧. *Step 1, transport of (ZHOU)* ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · EpFunctor.isZhouAt_of_isZhouAt⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · EpFunctor.isZhouAt_of_isZhouAt_obj⟧. For a full functor that is a local sc-diffeomorphism on objects, (ZHOU) at $x$ gives (ZHOU) at every object isomorphic to its image: the isotropy elements correspond, and the tangent maps are conjugate by the tangent of the local sc-diffeomorphism, the argument of Proposition 10.1.17. Conversely, for a functor that is a local sc-diffeomorphism on objects, (ZHOU) at the image of $x$ gives (ZHOU) at $x$, the other half of that argument. *Step 2, transport of (LUC)* ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularityPointwise · EpFunctor.isLUCAt_obj⟧. The natural representation of the image is the conjugate of that at $x$, by uniqueness of the sections over connected sets; with fullness, (LUC) passes. (LUC) at a point passes to isomorphic points ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularityPointwise · EpGroupoid.isLUCAt_of_isomorphic⟧. *Step 3, the piece functor* ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.full_pieceFunctor⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_pieceFunctor_obj⟧. The functor $G \ltimes O \to X_\Psi$ into Chapter 17's model is full and a local sc-diffeomorphism on objects. *Step 4, the raised groupoids* ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · EP.liftVal⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · EP.exists_liftVal_eq_of_mem⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularityPointwise · EP.isLocalScDiffeo_liftFunctor⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularityPointwise · EP.full_liftFunctor⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularityPointwise · EpFunctor.full_liftOne⟧. The raise $X^i$ maps to $X$ fully and by a local sc-diffeomorphism on objects, and a point of level $i$ is the value of a point of $X^i$.

**Proof of Proposition 19.5.7 (point G.265).** The text calls the proposition obvious; the proof is as follows. *Step 1.* Choose for every smooth class $z$ a uniformizer $\Psi_z$ and $y_z$ with (ZHOU) at $y_z$, and form the covering family of these uniformizers. *Step 2.* Every smooth object of its model $X$ is isomorphic to the image of some $y_z$, by Lemma 17.1.15 and the choice. *Step 3.* By Step 1 of the pointwise forms and Step 3 there, (ZHOU) at $y_z$ for $G_z \ltimes O_z$ gives (ZHOU) at its image in $X$, and (ZHOU) passes to isomorphic objects. *Step 4* ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_5_7⟧. So the model satisfies (ZHOU), which is (ZHOU) for the polyfold (Definition 17.1.22); the comparison of covering families (Theorem 17.1.18) makes it independent of the family. *Step 5, back to the uniformizers* ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · BasicConstruction.isZhou_toEP_of_isZhou⟧. Under (ZHOU) for the polyfold, every translation groupoid $G \ltimes O$ of a uniformizer of $F$ satisfies Chapter 7's (ZHOU): the model of a covering family containing $\Psi$ satisfies (ZHOU) ⟦Chapter17/ModelProperties · GCT.PolyfoldConstruction.IsZhou.model⟧, and (ZHOU) passes back along the piece functor $G \ltimes O \to X_\Psi$, a local sc-diffeomorphism on objects (the converse in Step 1 of the pointwise forms). This is used for Proposition 19.5.11.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text treats (ZHOU) for $(F, \mathcal F)$ as a property of the uniformizers; the formal definition asks it at a smooth point of one uniformizer per smooth class, which is what the proof of Proposition 19.5.7 needs, and every smooth class is detected through the smoothness of the transitions ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · BasicConstruction.mem_smoothSet_iff_of_π_eq⟧.

### XIX.14 Definitions 19.5.8, 19.5.10, Propositions 19.5.9, 19.5.11: (LUC), (SLUC), (LR), (SLR), pp. 889–890

**Statement.** *Definition 19.5.8* (p. 889, new in the 2021 edition; restated): $(F, \mathcal F)$ satisfies (LUC) if every class has a uniformizer $\Psi$ and $\bar o$ at which the translation groupoid has (LUC) for $G_{\bar o}$ ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · BasicConstruction.IsLUC⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.IsLUCAt⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.isLUCAt_iff⟧; (LUC) at level $i$ asks it for $(G \ltimes O)^i$ at the classes of level $i$ ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · BasicConstruction.IsLUCLevel⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · BasicConstruction.IsLevelClass⟧; (SLUC) is (LUC) at every level ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · BasicConstruction.IsSLUC⟧. *Proposition 19.5.9* (p. 890, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularityPointwise · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_5_9⟧: if $T$ is metrizable, (LUC) resp. (SLUC) for $(F, \mathcal F)$ gives (LUC) resp. (SLUC) for the polyfold. *Definition 19.5.10* (p. 890, new in the 2021 edition; restated) ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · BasicConstruction.IsLR⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · BasicConstruction.IsLRLevel⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · BasicConstruction.IsSLR⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.IsLRAt⟧: the same for (LR). *Proposition 19.5.11* (p. 890, new in the 2021 edition; explicit) ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_5_11⟧: if $T$ is metrizable, (ZHOU) for $(F, \mathcal F)$ implies (SLUC) and (SLR), and in particular (LUC) and (LR).

**Readings of the definitions (point G.266).** *Step 1, the group.* The text's "for every $g \in G$" is read for $G_{\bar o}$, the isotropy group at $\bar o$, which is $G$ when the uniformizer is centred and is what (LUC) of Chapter 7 speaks of; with $G$ literal for a uniformizer that is not centred the condition is presumably not implied by (ZHOU), and that form is not formalized. *Step 2, "nontrivial open".* Read as a non-empty open set. *Step 3, the levels.* The text's $(F^i, \mathcal F^i)$ for $\mathcal S^i$ is read through the raised translation groupoids $(G \ltimes O)^i$ at the points of level $i$; a class has level $i$ when it contains $\Psi(q)$ with $q$ of level $i$ ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · BasicConstruction.IsLevelClass⟧, which does not depend on $\Psi$, since the maps $f_h$ are sc-diffeomorphisms and preserve levels ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · BasicConstruction.mem_levelSet_iff_of_π_eq⟧ (Findings, Chapter 19, item 7). *Step 4, (LR) between uniformizers* ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · GroupoidalCategory.Uniformizer.isLRAt_of_isLRAt⟧. (LR) at a point for one uniformizer passes to the corresponding point of another, along $f_h$.

**Proof of Proposition 19.5.9 (point G.267).** The text gives no proof. *Step 1, (LUC) is invariant* ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularityPointwise · EP.isGenIsoInvariant_isLUC_lift⟧. (LUC) of a raised ep-groupoid is invariant under generalized isomorphisms (Proposition 10.1.16 ⟦Chapter10/EquivalenceReduced · EpFunctor.proposition_10_1_16⟧, applied to the raise). *Step 2, one model with (LUC)* ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularityPointwise · BasicConstruction.exists_isLUC_lift_model⟧. From (LUC) at level $i$, choose a uniformizer and point per class, form their covering family; by the transport of §XIX.13 (Step 2 of the pointwise forms) and Step 4 there for the raise, its model has (LUC) at level $i$. *Step 3, level $0$* ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularityPointwise · BasicConstruction.isLUCLevel_zero_of_isLUC⟧. (LUC) is (LUC) at level $0$, through the values of the raised points ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularityPointwise · EP.liftVal_mem_levelSet⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularityPointwise · EP.liftVal_mem_levelSet_self⟧. *Step 4* ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularityPointwise · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_5_9⟧. For (LUC), the model of Step 2 at level $0$; for (SLUC), the model of each level $i$ is compared with that of level $0$ by the generalized isomorphism of Theorem 17.1.18 ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureGCT · BasicConstruction.isGeneralizedIso_comparison⟧, and Step 1 carries (LUC) at level $i$ to the one model.

**Proof of Proposition 19.5.11 (point G.268).** The text refers to the ep-groupoid case. *Step 1.* (ZHOU) for $(F, \mathcal F)$ gives (ZHOU) for the polyfold (Proposition 19.5.7), and then for every translation groupoid $G \ltimes O$ (§XIX.13, Step 5). *Step 2.* Chapter 7's Theorems 7.4.13 and 7.4.24 for ep-groupoids with (ZHOU) give (LUC) and (LR) at every level ⟦Chapter8/SmoothRegularAssembly · EP.theorem_7_4_13_all_of_isZhou⟧, ⟦Chapter8/SmoothRegularAssembly · EP.theorem_7_4_24_all_of_isZhou⟧. *Step 3.* The point of level $i$ of a class is the value of a raised point ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · EP.exists_liftVal_eq_of_mem⟧, and level membership does not depend on the uniformizer (point G.266, Step 3). *Step 4* ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularity · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_5_11⟧. So (SLUC) and (SLR) hold, and at level $0$, (LUC) and (LR).

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text states Propositions 19.5.9 and 19.5.11 under the standing metrizability of §19.5; the formal statements take it as the argument of the polyfold construction. Proposition 19.5.11 is stated for the basic construction, as printed; its passage to the polyfold is Proposition 19.5.9.

### XIX.15 Witnesses for §§19.1–19.5

**Statement** (extra). Every definition and every added hypothesis of §§19.1–19.5 has a witness; they are collected here.

*Step 1, transition data from a key map* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.keyMap⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData.ofKey⟧. Given a key on the morphisms such that an element of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is determined by its source and key, the key is compatible with $u$, $\iota$, $m$ and $\Psi(g, \cdot)$, and $q \mapsto t(F_h(q))$ is an sc-diffeomorphism, $F_h(q)$ is the element over $q$ with the key of $h$, defined on the open set of the $q$ over which there is one; (A)–(F) follow, (F) from the uniqueness of the element with given source and key. *Step 2, translations* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.tautUniformizer⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.translationData⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.translationBasic⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.translationBasic_hasUniformizers⟧: the translation groupoid of a group action with its tautological uniformizer.

*Step 3, the reflection* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.reflectionBasic⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.reflectionBasic_witness⟧. $\mathbb Z/2 = \mathbb Z^\times$ acting on $\mathbb R$ by $x \mapsto \pm x$, centred at $0$, with non-trivial isotropy. It satisfies Theorem 19.2.2 ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.reflectionBasic_theorem_19_2_2⟧, the reading $F(\alpha) \neq \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.reflectionBasic_hB⟧, metrizability ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.reflectionBasic_metrizableSpace⟧, (v2) (by Proposition 19.4.7) ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.reflectionBasic_propernessV2⟧, the Hausdorff hypothesis of Proposition 19.4.2 (by Proposition 19.4.5) ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.reflectionBasic_t2Space⟧, Theorem 19.5.1 ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureExamples · GroupoidalCategory.reflectionBasic_theorem_19_5_1⟧, Corollary 19.5.2 ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureExamples · GroupoidalCategory.reflectionBasic_corollary_19_5_2⟧, Theorem 19.5.3 (1) (paracompact from metrizable) ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureExamples · GroupoidalCategory.reflectionBasic_theorem_19_5_3_one⟧, tameness ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureExamples · GroupoidalCategory.reflectionBasic_isTame⟧, ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureExamples · GroupoidalCategory.reflectionBasic_theorem_19_5_3_two_tame⟧, (LUC) for the reflection ep-groupoid ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureExamples · GroupoidalCategory.reflectionBasic_isLUC⟧, Remark 19.5.4 ⟦Chapter19/CompactNeighbourhoods · GroupoidalCategory.reflectionBasic_remark_19_5_4⟧ and Proposition 19.5.9 for (LUC) ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularityPointwise · GroupoidalCategory.reflectionBasic_proposition_19_5_9⟧. The abstract orbit-chart hypotheses have the same witness ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.orbitCharts_witness_reflection⟧, and the finite-sheet hypothesis the two sheets of $\mathbb Z^\times \times \mathbb R \to \mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.finiteSheets_witness_snd⟧.

*Step 4, the trivial $\mathbb Z/3$* ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.z3Basic⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · GroupoidalCategory.z3Basic_isCentred⟧. $\mathbb Z/3$ acting trivially on $\mathbb R$: the witness of (ZHOU) and (SLUC) for a basic construction, weak since the action is trivial and not effective. It satisfies the reading $F(\alpha) \neq \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureExamples · GroupoidalCategory.z3Basic_hB⟧, metrizability ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureExamples · GroupoidalCategory.z3Basic_metrizableSpace⟧, every class is smooth ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureExamples · GroupoidalCategory.z3Basic_isSmoothClass⟧, (ZHOU) holds since $g q = q$ everywhere ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureExamples · GroupoidalCategory.z3Basic_isZhou⟧, and Propositions 19.5.7, 19.5.9, 19.5.11 apply ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureExamples · GroupoidalCategory.z3Basic_proposition_19_5_7⟧, ⟦Chapter19/BasicRegularityPointwise · GroupoidalCategory.z3Basic_proposition_19_5_9⟧, ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureExamples · GroupoidalCategory.z3Basic_proposition_19_5_11⟧.

*Step 5, the examples on which statements do not hold.* The empty construction (§XIX.4), the glued lines (§§XIX.5, XIX.8) with ⟦Chapter19/MetrizabilityExamples · GroupoidalCategory.gluedBasic_hB⟧, and the exponential example (§XIX.9).

*Readings.* Every hypothesis of §§19.1–19.5 is satisfiable, and the witnesses of the reading $F(\alpha) \neq \emptyset$, of metrizability, (v2), the Hausdorff hypothesis and (LUC) have non-trivial isotropy; only (ZHOU) and (SLUC) have the weak witness of a trivial action, and the source condition of Lemma 19.4.3 that of trivial isotropy between two different uniformizers (Findings, Chapter 19, item 18).

### XIX.16 The functor $\mu$, the category $E_\mu$, Definition 19.6.1, Remark 19.6.2, Definition 19.6.3 (2017: §19.6, Definitions 19.9, 19.10, Remark 19.8), pp. 890–892

**Statement.** *The functor $\mu$ and the category $E_\mu$* (pp. 890–891, same) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.BanFunctor⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.cat⟧: a functor $\mu$ from the groupoidal category $\mathcal S$ to Banach spaces is given by the data of the text's first description: a total space $E$ with a projection $P : E \to \mathcal S$ on objects, on every fibre $P^{-1}(\alpha)$ the structure of a Banachable topological vector space, and for every morphism $\varphi$ a bounded linear lift $\hat\varphi : P^{-1}(s(\varphi)) \to P^{-1}(t(\varphi))$ with (1) $\hat 1_\alpha = \mathrm{Id}$ and (2) $(\varphi' \circ \varphi)^\wedge = \hat\varphi' \circ \hat\varphi$. The category $E_\mu$ has the elements $e$ of the fibres as objects and the pairs $(\varphi, e)$ with $s(\varphi) = P(e)$ as morphisms $e \to \hat\varphi(e)$. Each $\hat\varphi$ is a topological linear isomorphism ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.liftEquiv⟧, and the morphisms of $E_\mu$ over $\varphi$ are the pairs $(\varphi, e)$, $e \in \mu(s(\varphi))$. The projection is a functor $P : E_\mu \to \mathcal S$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.proj⟧ with orbit map $|P| : |E_\mu| \to |\mathcal S|$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.orbitProj⟧, and the zero section $|\alpha| \mapsto |(\alpha, 0)|$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.zeroOrbit⟧ satisfies $|P| \circ 0 = \mathrm{id}$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.orbitProj_zeroOrbit⟧. *Definition 19.6.1* (p. 891, same) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.StrongBundleUniformizer⟧: a strong bundle uniformizer at $\alpha$ is a functor $\bar\Psi : G \ltimes K \to E_\mu$ which is a uniformizer of $E_\mu$ (injective on objects and fully faithful), with the structure (1) of a strong bundle over a uniformizer $\Psi$ of $\mathcal S$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.StrongBundleStr⟧, such that $\Psi$ is at $\alpha$. Its parts (2) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleUniformizer.definition_19_6_1_two⟧ and (3) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleUniformizer.definition_19_6_1_three⟧ are theorems, and $\Psi$ is determined by $\bar\Psi$, on objects ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.toBase_obj_eq⟧ and on morphisms ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.toBase_mor_eq⟧. *Remark 19.6.2* (p. 892, restated) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizer.remark_19_6_2⟧: the remark allows strong bundles $W \to E$ over ep-groupoids as models in place of $G \ltimes K \to G \ltimes O$. It is verified through its zero-section consequence for the uniformizers of a bundle covering square (Definition 19.8.10): $\hat\Gamma(0_{\hat e}) = (\hat\Psi(\hat e), 0)$ and $\Gamma(0_o) = (\Psi(o), 0)$. The general variant is the one that Definition 19.8.10 (3) uses; it is not built separately (point G.269). *Definition 19.6.3* (p. 892, same) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction⟧: a strong bundle uniformizer construction $\bar F$ gives for every object $\alpha$ a set $\bar F(\alpha)$ of strong bundle uniformizers at $\alpha$. It induces a uniformizer construction $F$ of $\mathcal S$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.base⟧ and one of $E_\mu$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.toE⟧, and every class of $E_\mu$ meets the second iff every class of $\mathcal S$ meets $F$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.hasClassUniformizers_toE_iff⟧.

**Proof of the construction of $\mu$ and $E_\mu$.** *Step 1, the action.* For a pair $q = (\varphi, e)$ with $s(\varphi) = P(e)$ the formalization writes $\hat\varphi(e)$ as $\mathrm{act}(q)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.act⟧; it lies over $t(\varphi)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.P_act⟧. Axiom (1) gives $\mathrm{act}(1_{P(e)}, e) = e$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.act_unitPair⟧ and axiom (2) gives $\mathrm{act}(\psi\gamma, e) = \mathrm{act}(\psi, \mathrm{act}(\gamma, e))$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.act_comp⟧. *Step 2, $\hat\varphi$ is invertible.* Applying (2) to $\varphi^{-1} \circ \varphi = 1_{s(\varphi)}$ and then (1) gives $\widehat{\varphi^{-1}}(\hat\varphi(e)) = e$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.act_inv_act⟧. The fibres $\mu(s(\varphi^{-1}))$ and $\mu(t(\varphi))$ are equal only up to the equation $s(\varphi^{-1}) = t(\varphi)$. So the inverse is $\widehat{\varphi^{-1}}$ composed with the identifications of fibres over equal objects ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.fiberCast⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.liftInv⟧. The second inverse law is the first applied to $\varphi^{-1}$, with $(\varphi^{-1})^{-1} = \varphi$. Both maps are bounded, so $\hat\varphi$ is a topological linear isomorphism ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.liftEquiv⟧. *Step 3, the groupoid $E_\mu$* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.groupoidStr⟧: $s(\varphi, e) = e$, $t(\varphi, e) = \hat\varphi(e)$, unit $(1_{P(e)}, e)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.unitPair⟧, inverse $(\varphi^{-1}, \hat\varphi(e))$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.morInv⟧. The composite of $(\varphi', \hat\varphi(e))$ and $(\varphi, e)$ is $(\varphi' \circ \varphi, e)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.morComp⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.morComp_val⟧; the operation is total, with a value of no meaning off composable pairs. The target of a composite is the right one by Step 1 ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.act_morComp⟧. Associativity, the unit laws and the inverse laws are those of $\mathcal S$ in the first component; the target of the inverse is $e$ by Step 2 ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.act_morInv⟧. *Step 4, finiteness* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.cat⟧: a morphism $e \to e'$ is determined by its first component, since the second is the source $e$. That component is a morphism $P(e) \to P(e')$ of $\mathcal S$, and there are finitely many. So $E_\mu$ is a groupoidal category. *Step 5, $P$ and its orbit map.* $(\varphi, e) \mapsto \varphi$ is a functor ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.proj⟧, compatible with composition ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.cat_comp_fst⟧. Its orbit map is $|P|$, with $|P|(|e|) = |P(e)|$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.orbitProj_π⟧. Every $e$ is isomorphic to $\hat\varphi(e)$ by the morphism $(\varphi, e)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.π_act⟧. So if $|P(e)| = |\alpha|$, a morphism $\varphi : P(e) \to \alpha$ moves $e$ to $\hat\varphi(e)$ in the fibre over $\alpha$, in the same class ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.exists_mem_fiber_of_π_eq⟧. *Step 6, the zero section.* The zero of $\mu(\alpha)$ is the object $(\alpha, 0)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.zero⟧. Linearity gives $\hat\varphi(0) = 0$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.act_zero⟧, so isomorphic objects of $\mathcal S$ have isomorphic zeros ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.π_zero_eq⟧. Hence $|\alpha| \mapsto |(\alpha, 0)|$ is well defined on classes ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.zeroOrbit⟧. Since $P(\alpha, 0) = \alpha$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.P_zero⟧, $|P| \circ 0 = \mathrm{id}$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.orbitProj_zeroOrbit⟧.

**Proof of Definition 19.6.1.** *Step 1, the structure (1).* A strong bundle uniformizer structure on a uniformizer $\bar\Psi : G \ltimes K \to E_\mu$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.StrongBundleStr⟧ consists of the following fields:
- a Hausdorff, paracompact M-polyfold $O$ on which $G$ acts by sc-smooth maps;
- a functor $\Psi : G \ltimes O \to \mathcal S$ that is injective on objects, full and faithful;
- a projection $p : K \to O$ with linear fibres and a strong bundle structure on it, whose level-$0$ M-polyfold $K[0]$ is the M-polyfold $K$ of $\bar\Psi$;
- equivariance $p(g \ast a) = g \ast p(a)$, every $g \ast \cdot$ a strong bundle map covering $g \ast \cdot$ on $O$;
- the commutative diagram $P \circ \bar\Psi = \Psi \circ (\mathrm{Id} \ltimes p)$, on objects ($P(\bar\Psi(a)) = \Psi(p(a))$) and on morphisms (the first component of $\bar\Psi(g, a)$ is $\Psi(g, p(a))$);
- topological linear isomorphisms $K_o \cong \mu(\Psi(o))$ that agree with $\bar\Psi$ on objects.

The underlying uniformizer of $\mathcal S$ is $\Psi$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.toBase⟧. *Step 2, consequences of the diagram.* $\bar\Psi(g, a) = (\Psi(g, p(a)), \bar\Psi(a))$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.mor_eq⟧, since the second component of a morphism of $E_\mu$ is its source. The target of this morphism is $\bar\Psi(g \ast a)$, so $\bar\Psi(g \ast a) = \Psi(g, p(a))^\wedge(\bar\Psi(a))$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.obj_smul⟧. *Step 3, zeros.* The fibre isomorphism $K_o \cong \mu(\Psi(o))$ is linear, so it sends $0_o$ to $0$: $\bar\Psi(0_o) = (\Psi(o), 0)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.obj_fiberZero⟧. *Step 4, "$\Psi$ is completely determined by $\bar\Psi$".* On objects, $\Psi(o) = P(\bar\Psi(0_o))$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.toBase_obj_eq⟧, because $0_o$ lies over $o$ and $P \circ \bar\Psi = \Psi \circ p$. On morphisms, $\Psi(g, o)$ is the first component of $\bar\Psi(g, 0_o)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.toBase_mor_eq⟧. *Step 5, (2) and (3).* A strong bundle uniformizer at $\alpha$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.StrongBundleUniformizer⟧ is the functor, the structure and the condition that $\Psi$ is at $\alpha$. (2) is the list of the fields "injective on objects, full, faithful" for $\bar\Psi$ and for $\Psi$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleUniformizer.definition_19_6_1_two⟧. For (3) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleUniformizer.definition_19_6_1_three⟧, "$\Psi$ at $\alpha$" gives $\bar o$ with $\Psi(\bar o) = \alpha$, and Step 3 gives $\bar\Psi(0_{\bar o}) = (\alpha, 0)$.

**Proof of Remark 19.6.2 (the zero sections).** For a member of the uniformizer construction of a bundle covering square ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizer.remark_19_6_2⟧, the functors $\hat\Gamma$ (over the ep-groupoid $\hat E$) and $\Gamma$ are topological linear isomorphisms on the fibres. This is part of Definition 19.8.10 (2), (3). So the zero of the fibre over $\hat e$ (resp. $o$) goes to the zero over $\hat\Psi(\hat e)$ (resp. $\Psi(o)$), by the same linearity argument as Step 3 above.

**Proof of Definition 19.6.3.** *Step 1, the data* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · GroupoidalCategory.StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction⟧: for every object $\alpha$ a set $\bar F(\alpha)$ of uniformizers of $E_\mu$, together with a structure (1) for each functor occurring in some $\bar F(\alpha)$, and the condition that the members of $\bar F(\alpha)$ are at $\alpha$. So the structure is a function of the functor ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.strU⟧, and a member of $\bar F(\alpha)$ is a strong bundle uniformizer at $\alpha$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.toStrongBundleUniformizer⟧. *Step 2, the induced constructions.* $F(\alpha)$ consists of the underlying uniformizers $\Psi$ of the members of $\bar F(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.base⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.baseUnif⟧. For an object $e$ of $E_\mu$, the set of $e$ in the construction of $E_\mu$ consists of the $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$ with $|\alpha| = |P(e)|$ and $\bar\Psi$ at $e$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.toE⟧. Every member of $\bar F(\alpha)$ belongs to the set of the object $\bar\Psi(0_{\bar o})$ with $\Psi(\bar o) = \alpha$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.mem_toE⟧, so the members of the two constructions are the same functors ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.toEUnif⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.ofE⟧. *Step 3, classes* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.hasClassUniformizers_toE_iff⟧. "Every class meets the construction" is the property of Theorem 19.2.2 ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.HasClassUniformizers⟧.
- $\Rightarrow$: given $z \in |\mathcal S|$, take the class $0(z)$ of $|E_\mu|$. It contains an $e$ with a member $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$, $|\alpha| = |P(e)|$. Then $|\alpha| = |P|(|e|) = |P|(0(z)) = z$, and $\Psi \in F(\alpha)$.
- $\Leftarrow$: given a class $|e|$, choose $\alpha$ with $|\alpha| = |P(e)|$ and $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$. Move $e$ to $e'$ in the fibre over $\alpha$ (Step 5 of the first proof), take $o$ with $\Psi(o) = \alpha$, and let $v \in K_o$ be the preimage of $e'$ under the fibre isomorphism. Then $\bar\Psi(v) = e'$. So $\bar\Psi$ is at $e'$ with $|\alpha| = |P(e')|$, and $|e'| = |e|$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The functor $\mu$ is presented by the text's first description: total space, fibres and lifts. The formalization takes $E_\mu$ to have the elements of $E$ as objects, as the text says there is no difference. The fibres are Banachable, as in the text's footnote. A strong bundle uniformizer "is a functor"; the formalization takes a uniformizer of $E_\mu$, which is (2), with the structure (1) as data, and (3) becomes a consequence of "$\Psi$ at $\alpha$" and fibre linearity. "$G$ acts by strong bundle isomorphisms" is read as: every $g \ast \cdot$ is a strong bundle map covering $g \ast \cdot$; its inverse $g^{-1} \ast \cdot$ is one as well. Remark 19.6.2 is not stated in general: its general variant with $W \to E$ models is the shape of Definition 19.8.10 (3), and only its zero-section consequence is a theorem (point G.269).

### XIX.17 The structure maps of $P : E_\mu \to \mathcal S$, the lifts $\bar F_h$ and (19.10), Definitions 19.6.4–19.6.6 (2017: §19.6, Definitions 19.11, 19.12), pp. 892–896

**Statement.** *The transition sets and the map $p$* (pp. 892–894, same) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.proj⟧: for strong bundle uniformizers $\bar\Psi$, $\bar\Psi'$, the transition set $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ is the transition set of the two functors into $E_\mu$, with elements $(a, (\varphi, e), a')$, $e = \bar\Psi(a)$. The map $p : M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi') \to M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is $p(a, \varphi, a') = (p(a), \varphi, p'(a'))$. It is compatible with the source and target maps ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.src_proj⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.tgt_proj⟧, the unit ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.proj_unit⟧, the inversion ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.proj_inv⟧, the multiplication ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.proj_comp⟧ and the map $\hat\Psi$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.proj_hat⟧. Its fibre over $(o, \varphi, o')$ is in bijection with $K_o$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.projFiberEquiv⟧. *The lifts $\bar F_h$ and (19.10)* (pp. 894–895, same) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.mapBar⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.fmapBar⟧: for transition data $F$ of the underlying construction and $h \in M(\Psi, \Psi')$, $\bar F_h(a) = (a, \varphi_{p(a)}, a'(a))$ with $\bar\Psi'(a'(a)) = \hat\varphi_{p(a)}(\bar\Psi(a))$, and $\bar f_h = t \circ \bar F_h$. They lie over $F_h$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.proj_mapBar⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.p_fmapBar⟧, and on $K|V(o)$ the point $\bar F_h(a)$ is the element of $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ with source $a$ over $F_h(p(a))$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.mapBar_eq_iff⟧. *Definition 19.6.4* (p. 895, restated) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.BundleTransitionData⟧: bundle transition data are transition data $F$ of the underlying construction such that (A) every $\bar f_h : K|V(o) \to K'$ is a strong bundle isomorphism onto $K'|f_h(V(o))$ covering $f_h$. The rest of (A) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_6_4_A_src⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_6_4_A_hat⟧ and (B)–(F) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_6_4_B⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_6_4_C⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_6_4_D⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_6_4_E⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_6_4_F⟧ hold for the lift of any transition data. The restriction and equivalence of p. 896 are given, and equivalence of bundle transition data is equivalence of the underlying data ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BundleTransitionData.equiv_iff⟧. The lift is a transition datum for $E_\mu$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BundleTransitionData.toETD⟧. *Definition 19.6.5* (p. 896, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.StrongBundleTransitionConstruction⟧: a strong bundle transition construction is an equivalence class of bundle transition data. It induces a transition construction of $\mathcal S$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleTransitionConstruction.toBaseTC⟧ and one of $E_\mu$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleTransitionConstruction.toETC⟧. *Definition 19.6.6* (p. 896, same) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · GroupoidalCategory.BasicStrongBundleConstruction⟧: a basic strong bundle construction is a pair $(\bar F, \bar{\mathcal F})$. It has an underlying basic construction of $\mathcal S$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.base⟧, a basic construction of $E_\mu$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.toE⟧ and a chosen representative ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.rep⟧.

**Proof of the facts about $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ and $p$.** *Step 1, the components.* For $\bar h = (a, (\varphi, e), a')$ in the transition set, $e = \bar\Psi(a)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.trans_mor_snd⟧ and $\hat\varphi(e) = \bar\Psi'(a')$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.trans_act_mor⟧. *Step 2, $p$ is well defined* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.proj⟧: $s(\varphi) = P(e) = P(\bar\Psi(a)) = \Psi(p(a))$ and $t(\varphi) = P(\hat\varphi(e)) = P(\bar\Psi'(a')) = \Psi'(p'(a'))$. *Step 3, the lift over a base element.* For $k = (o, \varphi, o')$ and $a \in K_o$, the point $\bar\Psi'^{-1}(\hat\varphi(\bar\Psi(a)))$ of $K'_{o'}$ is defined by the inverse of the fibre isomorphism of $\bar\Psi'$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.liftTgt⟧. With it, $(a, (\varphi, \bar\Psi(a)), \bar\Psi'^{-1}\hat\varphi\bar\Psi(a))$ is an element of $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.lift⟧ over $k$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.proj_lift⟧. *Step 4, uniqueness* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.eq_of_proj⟧: two elements with the same source and the same image under $p$ are equal. The morphisms agree, since the first components are the base morphism and the second components are $\bar\Psi$ of the common source. The targets then have the same image $\hat\varphi(e)$ under $\bar\Psi'$, which is injective on objects. So every element is the lift of its image at its source ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.lift_proj⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.eq_lift_iff⟧, and the fibre of $p$ over $k$ is in bijection with $K_o$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.projFiberEquiv⟧. *Step 5, the structure maps.* For the unit, $p(1_a) = 1_{p(a)}$ because $P(\bar\Psi(a)) = \Psi(p(a))$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.proj_unit⟧. Inversion commutes with $p$ by definition ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.proj_inv⟧. Composable elements have composable images ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.proj_composable⟧, and the first component of a composite in $E_\mu$ is the composite in $\mathcal S$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · BanFunctor.cat_comp_fst⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.proj_comp⟧. For $\hat\Psi$, $p(a, \bar\Psi(g, a), g \ast a) = (p(a), \Psi(g, p(a)), g \ast p(a))$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.proj_hat⟧. An element is also determined by its target and its image under $p$, applying Step 4 to the inverses ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleStr.eq_of_proj_tgt⟧.

**Proof of the facts about $\bar F_h$ and (19.10).** *Step 1, the lift of a map* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleStr.liftMap⟧: for a map $u \mapsto F_h(u)$ into $M(\Psi, \Psi')$, the lift at $a$ is the lift (Step 3 above) of $F_h(p(a))$ at $a$ when $s(F_h(p(a))) = p(a)$, and otherwise a lift at the zero over the source, a value of no meaning. *Step 2, $\bar F_h$* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.mapBar⟧ is this lift of $F_h$, and $\bar f_h$ is its target ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.fmapBar⟧. In both cases of Step 1 the lift lies over $F_h(p(a))$, so $p \circ \bar F_h = F_h \circ p$ everywhere ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleStr.proj_liftMap⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.proj_mapBar⟧, and $p' \circ \bar f_h = f_h \circ p$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.p_fmapBar⟧. *Step 3, on $K|V(o)$.* If $p(a) \in V(o)$, then $s(F_h(p(a))) = p(a)$ by (A) for $F$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.src_map_p⟧. So the first case applies, and $s(\bar F_h(a)) = a$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_6_4_A_src⟧ and $\bar\Psi'(\bar f_h(a)) = \hat\varphi_{p(a)}(\bar\Psi(a))$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.obj_fmapBar⟧. With Step 4 above, this gives (19.10): $\bar F_h(a)$ is the unique element with source $a$ over $F_h(p(a))$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.mapBar_eq_iff⟧. The lift depends only on the value $F_h(p(a))$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.mapBar_congr⟧.

**Proof of Definition 19.6.4 (A)–(F) for the lift.** Each identity is proved by (19.10): both sides have the same source and the same image under $p$, and the image identity is the corresponding property of $F$ ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionData⟧.
- *(A), the special form* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_6_4_A_hat⟧: for $h = (o, \Psi(g, o), g \ast o)$, $\bar F_h(a) = (a, \bar\Psi(g, a), g \ast a)$. The right side has source $a$ and image $(p(a), \Psi(g, p(a)), g \ast p(a))$, which is $F_h(p(a))$ by (A) for $F$. In particular $\bar f_h(a) = g \ast a$, covering $f_h(p(a)) = g \ast p(a) = p(g \ast a)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_6_4_A_hat_fmap⟧.
- *(B)* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_6_4_B⟧: stability of $F$ gives $F_{F_h(q)}(p(a)) = F_h(p(a))$, and the lift depends only on this value.
- *(C)* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_6_4_C⟧: $p(1_a) = 1_{p(a)} = F_{1_o}(p(a))$.
- *(D)* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_6_4_D⟧: $\bar f_h(a)$ lies over $f_h(p(a)) \in V(o')$. The element $\iota(\bar F_h(a))$ has source $\bar f_h(a)$, and its image is $\iota(F_h(p(a))) = F_{\iota(h)}(f_h(p(a)))$ by inversion for $F$.
- *(E)* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_6_4_E⟧: the composite $m(\bar F_{h'}(\bar f_h(a)), \bar F_h(a))$ has source $a$. Its image is $m(F_{h'}(f_h(p(a))), F_h(p(a)))$ by the compatibility of $p$ with $m$, and this is $F_{m(h', h)}(p(a))$ by multiplication for $F$. The composability of the two lifted elements is a hypothesis of the formal statement; it is a consequence of the others.
- *(F)* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_6_4_F⟧: for $h_1 \ne h_2$ with the same source, (F) for $F$ gives $W$ with $F_{h_1}(W)$, $F_{h_2}(W)$ disjoint. A common point of $\bar F_{h_1}(K|W)$ and $\bar F_{h_2}(K|W)$ would project to a common point of $F_{h_1}(W)$ and $F_{h_2}(W)$.

**Proof of Definitions 19.6.4–19.6.6 as structures.** *Step 1, bundle transition data* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.BundleTransitionData⟧: transition data $F$ and, for every $h$, an inverse pair making $\bar f_h$ a strong bundle isomorphism $K|V(o) \to K'|f_h(V(o))$ covering $f_h$ in the sense of Chapter 10 ⟦Chapter10/StrongBundleLocalIso · StrongBundle.IsLocalIso₂⟧. That is: an sc-diffeomorphism on the base, linear on the fibres both ways, mutually inverse sc-diffeomorphisms at level $0$ and at level $1$. At level $0$ this makes $\bar f_h$ an sc-diffeomorphism of the M-polyfold $K$ onto an open image ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BundleTransitionData.isScDiffeoOn_fmapBar⟧, using $K[0] = K$. *Step 2, restriction and equivalence.* Replacing every $V(o)$ by an open $W(o) \ni o$ restricts both $F$ and the local isomorphisms ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BundleTransitionData.restrict⟧. Two bundle transition data are equivalent, as printed, if for every $h$ there is an open $W(o)$ in both domains on which $F_h = F'_h$ and $\bar F_h = \bar F'_h$ on $K|W(o)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BundleTransitionData.Equiv⟧. The second identity follows from the first, since the lift depends only on $F_h(p(a))$; so equivalence of bundle transition data is equivalence of the underlying data ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BundleTransitionData.equiv_iff⟧. It is therefore an equivalence relation ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BundleTransitionData.setoid⟧, and a restriction is equivalent to the original ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BundleTransitionData.equiv_restrict⟧. *Step 3, the lift as transition data of $E_\mu$* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BundleTransitionData.toETD⟧: for $\bar h \in M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$, the domain is $K|V(p(s(\bar h)))$, open since $p$ is continuous, and the map is $\bar F_{p(\bar h)}$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BundleTransitionData.toETD_map⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BundleTransitionData.toETD_dom⟧. Its fields are the facts above:
- pointedness, from (19.10);
- the source property and the sc-diffeomorphism property (A), from Step 3 of the previous proof and Step 1;
- the special form, stability, identity, inversion and multiplication, by (A)–(E), after rewriting $p(\hat\Psi(g, a))$, $p(\bar F_{p(\bar h)}(q))$, $p(1_o)$ and $p(m(\bar h', \bar h))$ by the compatibilities of $p$;
- (F): for $\bar h_1 \ne \bar h_2$ with the same source, $p(\bar h_1) \ne p(\bar h_2)$ by the uniqueness of Step 4, and (F) for the lift applies.

Equivalent underlying data give equivalent data of $E_\mu$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BundleTransitionData.toETD_equiv⟧. *Step 4, Definitions 19.6.5 and 19.6.6.* The strong bundle transition construction is the quotient by Step 2 ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.StrongBundleTransitionConstruction⟧. Taking the underlying data, resp. the lift, respects the relation by Step 2, resp. Step 3, which gives the two induced transition constructions ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleTransitionConstruction.toBaseTC⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · StrongBundleTransitionConstruction.toETC⟧. A basic strong bundle construction ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · GroupoidalCategory.BasicStrongBundleConstruction⟧ has the underlying basic construction $(F, \mathcal F)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.base⟧ and the basic construction of $E_\mu$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.toE⟧. A chosen representative $\bar F$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.rep⟧ has underlying data representing $\mathcal F$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.base_rep⟧ and a lift representing the transition construction of $E_\mu$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.toE_rep⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text defines the lift $\bar F_h$ from $F$ (p. 894) and then lists (A)–(F) as requirements on it. Since the lift is determined by $F$, the formalization makes the bundle transition data a structure on $F$ with the one requirement that is not automatic: (A), $\bar f_h$ a strong bundle isomorphism onto $K'|f_h(V(o))$ covering $f_h$. The rest of (A) and (B)–(F) are proved for every lift. "Onto an open image covering $f_h$" is read as Chapter 10's local strong bundle isomorphism over the sc-diffeomorphism $f_h : V(o) \to f_h(V(o))$. Equivalence keeps the printed form with both identities, and the second identity is shown to follow from the first. The 2017 text used germs in Definition 19.6.5. The bundle transition data are indexed by $h \in M(\Psi, \Psi')$, as in the text; the transition data of $E_\mu$ are indexed by $\bar h$ and use $\bar F_{p(\bar h)}$, which lets §§19.2–19.5 apply to $E_\mu$.

### XIX.18 Definition 19.6.7, Theorem 19.6.8, Proposition 19.6.9 (2017: Definition 19.13, Theorem 19.6, Proposition 19.7), pp. 897–898

**Statement.** *Definition 19.6.7* (p. 897, same) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.BarOpen⟧: a subset $\hat U$ of $|E_\mu|$ belongs to $\bar T$ if for every $\bar z \in \hat U$ there are an object $\alpha$ with $|\alpha| = |P|(\bar z)$, a $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$, a point $a \in K$ with $|\bar\Psi(a)| = \bar z$ and an open $\hat V \ni a$ in $K$ with $|\bar\Psi(\hat V)| \subset \hat U$. This is the natural openness of the construction of $E_\mu$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.definition_19_6_7⟧. $\bar T$ is the natural topology of the basic construction of $E_\mu$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.topE⟧, $T$ is that of $\mathcal S$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.topS⟧, and the open sets of $\bar T$ are exactly those of Definition 19.6.7 ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.isOpen_topE_iff⟧. *Theorem 19.6.8* (p. 897, explicit) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_8_one⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_8_two⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_8_three⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_8_four⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_8_five⟧: assume that every isomorphism class of $\mathcal S$ contains an object $\alpha$ with $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.HasClassUniformizers⟧. This is the standing reading for Theorem 19.2.2; its witness is the reflection bundle ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanBasic_hB⟧ (§XIX.22). Then:
- (1) the sets of Definition 19.6.7 contain $|E_\mu|$ and are closed under finite intersections and arbitrary unions;
- (2) for $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$ and $\hat W \subset K$ open, $|\bar\Psi(\hat W)|$ is open;
- (3) $|\bar\Psi| : |G \ltimes K| \to |E_\mu|$ is a homeomorphism onto an open set;
- (4) every $z \in |\mathcal S|$ has an open $V \ni z$ with $|P|^{-1}(V)$ metrizable;
- (5) $|P|$ is continuous for $\bar T$ and $T$.

The text leaves the proof to the reader; it is written out here (point G.270). *Proposition 19.6.9* (p. 898, explicit) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.proposition_19_6_9⟧: under the same hypothesis, $T$ is metrizable iff $\bar T$ is. The 2017 statement had only $\Rightarrow$. The direction $\Leftarrow$ is not argued in the text; it goes through the zero section, which is a continuous embedding ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.continuous_zeroOrbit⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.isEmbedding_zeroOrbit⟧ (point G.271). The direction $\Rightarrow$ takes a route different from the text's, which goes through $X_\Psi$ and Proposition 8.4.1.

**Proof of Definition 19.6.7.** *Step 1* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.definition_19_6_7⟧. The natural openness of Definition 19.2.1 for the construction of $E_\mu$ ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · UniformizerConstruction.NatOpen⟧ asks, for $\bar z \in \hat U$: an object $x$ with $|x| = \bar z$, a member $\bar\Psi$ of the set of $x$ (that is, $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$ with $|\alpha| = |P(x)|$ and $\bar\Psi$ at $x$), a point $a$ with $|\bar\Psi(a)| = \bar z$, and $\hat V$ as printed. From the printed form, take $x = \bar\Psi(a)$; then $|\alpha| = |P|(\bar z) = |P(x)|$. Conversely, $|\alpha| = |P(x)| = |P|(\bar z)$. *Step 2.* Since every class of $E_\mu$ meets the construction of $E_\mu$ when every class of $\mathcal S$ meets $F$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.hBE⟧ (Definition 19.6.3, §XIX.16), Theorem 19.2.2 (1) gives the topology $\bar T$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.topE⟧, and Step 1 identifies its open sets ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.isOpen_topE_iff⟧.

**Proof of Theorem 19.6.8 (point G.270).** The proof applies §19.2 to the basic construction of $E_\mu$ formed by the functors $\bar\Psi$ and the lifts $\bar F$.
- *Steps 1–3, (1)–(3).* By Step 2 above, Theorem 19.2.2 (1) for $E_\mu$ ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_2_2_one⟧, rewritten with Definition 19.6.7, is (1) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_8_one⟧. For $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$, choose $\bar o$ with $\Psi(\bar o) = \alpha$. Then $\bar\Psi$ is a member of the construction of $E_\mu$ at $\bar\Psi(0_{\bar o})$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.mem_toE⟧. Theorem 19.2.2 (2) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_2_2_two⟧ and (3) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_2_2_three⟧ for $E_\mu$ then give (2) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_8_two⟧ and (3) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_8_three⟧.
- *Step 4, (4)* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_8_four⟧. Given $z$, the hypothesis gives $\alpha$ with $|\alpha| = z$ and $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$. Take $V = |\Psi(O)|$, open in $T$ by Theorem 19.2.2 (2) for $\mathcal S$ and containing $z$.
  - The preimage $|P|^{-1}(V)$ is $\{|\bar\Psi(a)|\}$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · StrongBundleStr.orbitProj_preimage_range⟧. An object $e$ with $|P(e)| = |\Psi(o)|$ can be moved into the fibre over $\Psi(o)$ (§XIX.16), and the fibre isomorphism $K_o \cong \mu(\Psi(o))$ provides $v$ with $\bar\Psi(v)$ equal to the moved object. Conversely $|P|(|\bar\Psi(a)|) = |\Psi(p(a))|$.
  - This set is the range of $|\bar\Psi|$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · StrongBundleStr.range_orbitMap⟧. By (3), $|\bar\Psi|$ is an embedding, so $|P|^{-1}(V)$ is homeomorphic to $|G \ltimes K| = G\backslash K$.
  - $G\backslash K$ is metrizable by Definition 17.1.2 for the M-polyfold $K$ with its sc-smooth action ⟦Chapter17/GroupoidalCategory · metrizableSpace_translation_orbitSpace⟧.
- *Step 5, (5)* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_8_five⟧. Let $W$ be open in $T$; we show $|P|^{-1}(W)$ is naturally open for $E_\mu$. Given $\bar z$ in it, choose an object $x$, $|x| = \bar z$, a member $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$ of its set, and $a$ with $\bar\Psi(a) = x$. The map $o \mapsto |\Psi(o)|$ is continuous into $T$ ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.continuous_π_obj⟧, and $p$ is continuous ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.continuous_p⟧. So $\hat V = p^{-1}\{o : |\Psi(o)| \in W\}$ is open. It contains $a$, since $|\Psi(p(a))| = |P(\bar\Psi(a))| = |P|(\bar z) \in W$. Its image lies in $|P|^{-1}(W)$, since $|P|(|\bar\Psi(q)|) = |\Psi(p(q))|$.

**Proof of Proposition 19.6.9.** *Step 1, the zero section is continuous* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.continuous_zeroOrbit⟧. Let $W$ be open in $\bar T$, and $z$ with $0(z) \in W$. Choose $\alpha$, $|\alpha| = z$, $\bar\Psi \in \bar F(\alpha)$, and $\bar o$ with $\Psi(\bar o) = \alpha$. The zero section $o \mapsto 0_o$ of $K \to O$ is sc-smooth, hence continuous ⟦Chapter8/EpStrongBundle · StrongBundle.isScSmoothSection_fiberZero⟧, and $q \mapsto |\bar\Psi(q)|$ is continuous into $\bar T$ (Theorem 19.2.2 for $E_\mu$). So $V = \{o : |\bar\Psi(0_o)| \in W\}$ is open in $O$. It contains $\bar o$, since $\bar\Psi(0_{\bar o}) = (\alpha, 0)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.obj_fiberZero⟧. For $o \in V$, $0(|\Psi(o)|) = |(\Psi(o), 0)| = |\bar\Psi(0_o)| \in W$. So the preimage of $W$ is naturally open in $|\mathcal S|$. *Step 2, an embedding* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.isEmbedding_zeroOrbit⟧: the zero section has the continuous left inverse $|P|$ (Theorem 19.6.8 (5) and $|P| \circ 0 = \mathrm{id}$), and a continuous map with a continuous left inverse is an embedding. *Step 3, $\Rightarrow$* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.proposition_19_6_9⟧. If $T$ is metrizable, $|\mathcal S|$ is paracompact and Hausdorff. $|P|$ is continuous (Theorem 19.6.8 (5)), and every point of $|\mathcal S|$ has an open $V$ with $|P|^{-1}(V)$ metrizable (Theorem 19.6.8 (4)). Smirnov's theorem in the pulled-back form of Chapter 2 ⟦Chapter2/Metrizable · metrizableSpace_of_pullback⟧ then makes $|E_\mu|$ metrizable: a partition of unity on $|\mathcal S|$ subordinate to the $V$'s, pulled back by $|P|$, glues the local metrics. *Step 4, $\Leftarrow$* (point G.271): $|\mathcal S|$ is homeomorphic to its image under the zero section (Step 2), a subspace of the metrizable $|E_\mu|$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* Theorem 19.6.8 and Proposition 19.6.9 carry the hypothesis that every class of $\mathcal S$ meets $F$, the standing reading of Theorem 19.2.2. Without it Theorem 19.2.2 (1) does not hold as we read it for an empty family ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · theorem_19_2_2_empty_family⟧. The text leaves the proof of Theorem 19.6.8 to the reader; the formalization reduces (1)–(3) to Theorem 19.2.2 for $E_\mu$ and proves (4) and (5) directly (point G.270). For Proposition 19.6.9 the text's route to $\Rightarrow$ goes through $X_\Psi$ and Proposition 8.4.1; the formalization uses Smirnov's theorem along $|P|$ with (4) and (5). The direction $\Leftarrow$, new in the 2021 statement and not argued there, is the zero section (point G.271).

### XIX.19 Definition 19.6.10, Theorem 19.6.11, Lemma 19.6.12 (2017: Definition 19.14, Theorem 19.7), pp. 898–899

**Statement.** *Definition 19.6.10* (p. 898, restated) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.BarOpenTr⟧: for the representative $\bar F$ of $\bar{\mathcal F}$, a subset $\hat W \subset M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ is open if every $\bar h \in \hat W$ has an open neighbourhood $\hat V$ of $s(\bar h)$ in $K$, inside $K|V(p(s(\bar h)))$, with $\bar F_{p(\bar h)}(\hat V) \subset \hat W$. This is the openness of Lemma 19.3.1 for the lift as transition data of $E_\mu$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.definition_19_6_10⟧. $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ with this topology ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.MBar⟧ has the topology of the transition construction of $E_\mu$, which does not depend on the representative ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.topology_MBar⟧. *Theorem 19.6.11* (p. 898, restated) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_11⟧: with no hypothesis, these sets form a topology, and $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ is regular, Hausdorff and locally metrizable. Moreover:
- (1) $s$ is a local homeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_11_one⟧;
- (2) $t$ is a local homeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_11_two⟧;
- (3) the unit map $K \to M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi)$ is continuous ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_11_three⟧;
- (4) the inversion is a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_11_four⟧;
- (5) the multiplication is continuous ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_11_five⟧;
- (6) the projection $p : M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi') \to M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is continuous ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_11_six⟧.

In (6) we read the map named $m$ as the projection $p$, as the sentence after (6) does; the multiplication is (5). *Lemma 19.6.12* (p. 899, new in the 2021 edition; stronger) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.lemma_19_6_12⟧: $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ is metrizable, with no hypothesis. The printed form, with every class of $\mathcal S$ meeting $F$ and the properness condition (V2) ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.PropernessV2⟧, is kept and does not use (V2) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.lemma_19_6_12_book⟧.

**Proof of Definition 19.6.10.** *Step 1* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.definition_19_6_10⟧. The lift as transition data of $E_\mu$ (§XIX.17) has domain $K|V(p(s(\bar h)))$ and map $\bar F_{p(\bar h)}$ at $\bar h$. So the openness of Lemma 19.3.1 for it ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · TransitionData.IsOpenTr⟧ is Definition 19.6.10 word for word; the equivalence holds by definition. *Step 2, independence* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.topology_MBar⟧: the lift of the representative represents the transition construction of $E_\mu$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.toE_rep⟧, and by Definition 19.3.2 ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.definition_19_3_2⟧ the topology of a transition construction is that of any representative.

**Proof of Theorem 19.6.11.** Every assertion except (6) is the corresponding result of §19.3 for the transition data of $E_\mu$, which is legitimate because the lift satisfies (A)–(F) (§XIX.17).
- *Step 1, the first assertions* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_11⟧:
  - the sets form a topology, Proposition 19.3.4 ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · TransitionData.isOpenTr_topology⟧;
  - regularity, Lemma 19.3.8 ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · TransitionData.regularSpace⟧: $s$ is a local homeomorphism into the regular $K$, and the space is Hausdorff;
  - Hausdorff, Proposition 19.3.4 (3), by (F) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · TransitionData.t2Space⟧;
  - local metrizability ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · TransitionData.exists_isOpen_metrizableSpace⟧: $s$ is a local homeomorphism into the metrizable $K$.
- *Step 2, (1)–(5).* Proposition 19.3.5 (1)–(5) for $E_\mu$: $s$ ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · TransitionData.isLocalHomeomorph_src⟧, $t = s \circ \iota$ ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · TransitionData.isLocalHomeomorph_tgt⟧, the unit by (C) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · TransitionData.continuous_unit⟧, the inversion by (D) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · TransitionData.invHomeomorph⟧ (its value is $\iota(\bar h)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_11_four_apply⟧), and the multiplication by (E) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · TransitionData.continuous_mulMap⟧.
- *Step 3, (6)* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_11_six⟧. Let $\bar h$ be given and put $k = p(\bar h)$. The sheet $\bar F_k(K|V(p(s(\bar h))))$ is an open neighbourhood of $\bar h$: it is the target of the open partial homeomorphism of Proposition 19.3.4 (1), (2) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · TransitionData.sheet⟧ ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · TransitionData.mem_sheet_target⟧. For $\bar g = \bar F_k(q)$ on the sheet, $p(\bar g) = F_k(p(q))$ and $q = s(\bar g)$ (§XIX.17). So near $\bar h$, $p = F_k \circ p \circ s$. This composite is continuous at $\bar h$: $s$ is continuous ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · TransitionData.continuous_src⟧, $p$ is continuous, and $F_k$ is continuous at every point of $V(o)$ by Proposition 19.3.4 (2) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · TransitionData.continuousAt_map⟧. The topology on $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is that of the underlying data $F$, the natural one by the representative property ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransition · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.base_rep⟧.

**Proof of Lemma 19.6.12.** *Step 1* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.lemma_19_6_12⟧: the transition sets of any transition data are metrizable ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · TransitionData.metrizableSpace⟧, applied to the lift. That proof runs as follows:
- $K$ is metrizable, and the transition set is Hausdorff.
- $s$ is a local homeomorphism onto the open set $s(M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi'))$.
- Over a neighbourhood of every point of that set there are only finitely many sheets: a limit argument for sequences whose sources converge ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · TransitionData.exists_tendsto_subseq_of_tendsto_src⟧, using (F) and the finiteness of morphism sets.
- A Hausdorff space with a local homeomorphism with finitely many sheets onto a metrizable space is metrizable ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · metrizableSpace_of_isLocalHomeomorph_of_finiteSheets⟧ (Smirnov).

*Step 2* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.lemma_19_6_12_book⟧: the printed form takes the class hypothesis and (V2) and returns Step 1.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* Definition 19.6.10 speaks of a neighbourhood $\hat V$ "of $\bar h$ in $K$" and of $\bar F_h$; we read $\hat V$ as a neighbourhood of $s(\bar h)$ in $K$, inside $K|V(p(s(\bar h)))$, and $\bar F_h$ as $\bar F_{p(\bar h)}$, the lift of the transition of the underlying base morphism. In Theorem 19.6.11 (6) the map named $m$ is read as the projection $p$, following the sentence after (6). Lemma 19.6.12 is proved without the properness condition: in the formalization the transition sets of any transition data are metrizable, by Smirnov's theorem along the local homeomorphism $s$ with finitely many sheets. The printed form is kept.

### XIX.20 Theorem 19.6.13 and Remark 19.6.14 (2017: Theorem 19.8, Remark 19.9), p. 900

**Statement.** *Theorem 19.6.13* (p. 900; the strong bundle structure stronger, the multiplication same) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.Theorem_19_6_13_Statement⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_13⟧: for a basic strong bundle construction with representative $\bar F$ and any $\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi'$, with no hypothesis, there is a strong bundle $p : M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi') \to M(\Psi, \Psi')$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.bundleBar⟧ over the natural M-polyfold structure of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ (Theorem 19.5.1) such that:
- $H$ of (19.16) is a strong bundle isomorphism $s^*K \to M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ covering the identity ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_H⟧;
- the M-polyfold of the total space is the natural M-polyfold structure of $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_total⟧;
- $\bar s$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_s⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_s_local⟧ and $\bar t$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_t⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_t_local⟧ are strong bundle maps covering $s$ and $t$ which near every fibre are strong bundle isomorphisms onto open sets;
- the unit map is a strong bundle map covering $u$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_u⟧;
- the inversion is a strong bundle isomorphism covering $\iota$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_inv⟧;
- the multiplication, defined on the pull-back of the bundle to $M(\Psi', \Psi'') \,{}_s\!\times_t M(\Psi, \Psi')$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleMultiplication · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.mulBundle⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleMultiplication · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.mBar⟧, is a strong bundle map covering $m$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleMultiplication · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_m⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleMultiplication · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_m⟧.

$H$ is a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altHHomeo⟧ and $p$ is sc-smooth ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isScSmoothMap_projBar⟧. The same holds for any bundle transition data ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_of_bundleTransitionData⟧. The printed hypotheses, every class of $\mathcal S$ meeting $F$ and $T$ metrizable (new in 2021), are not needed; the printed form takes them and does not use the second ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_book⟧. The witness is the reflection bundle ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · reflBan_theorem_19_6_13⟧. *Remark 19.6.14* (p. 900, nothing): an outlook on how the construction will be used; nothing to formalize (point G.272).

**Proof of Theorem 19.6.13, the construction.** The proof follows the alternative approach of pp. 901–904: the structure is the one that makes $H$ a strong bundle isomorphism.
- *Step 1, the spaces.* $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ is the transition set of the lift as transition data of $E_\mu$, with its natural topology ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.MB⟧. Its natural M-polyfold structure is Theorem 19.5.1 for $E_\mu$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.MBpoly⟧ ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · TransitionData.transPolyfold⟧, the structure descended from the sheets $(\bar h, q) \mapsto \bar F_{p(\bar h)}(q)$. The base is $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.MS⟧ with the structure of Theorem 19.5.1 for $F$, and $p$ is the map of §XIX.17 ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.projBar⟧. The fibred product $s^*K = M(\Psi, \Psi') \,{}_s\!\times_p K$ is a strong bundle over $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ (§XIX.21). $H((q, \varphi, q'), k) = (k, \varphi, \bar\Psi'^{-1}\hat\varphi\bar\Psi(k))$ maps into $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altHE⟧. Its inverse is $\bar h \mapsto (p(\bar h), s(\bar h))$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altHinv⟧, by the uniqueness of §XIX.17 ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altHE_altHinv⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altHinv_altHE⟧.
- *Step 2, $p$ is sc-smooth* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isScSmoothMap_projBar⟧. Smoothness is local. Near $\bar g_0$, put $k = p(\bar g_0)$ and use the sheet $\bar F_k(K|V)$, the open image of the sc-diffeomorphism $\bar F_k$ (Theorem 19.5.1 for $E_\mu$, ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · TransitionData.isScDiffeoOn_map⟧). On it, $p = F_k \circ p \circ s$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.projBar_eq_of_mem⟧ (§XIX.17). The three maps are sc-smooth: $s$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · TransitionData.isLocalScDiffeo_src⟧; $p : K \to O$ is the projection of a strong bundle, read on $K[0] = K$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.toMPolyfold0_strU⟧; and $F_k$ is an sc-diffeomorphism on $V$ (Theorem 19.5.1 for $F$).
- *Step 3, $H$ is sc-smooth* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isScSmoothMap_altHE⟧. Near $x_0 = (k, a_0)$, the open set $\pi^{-1}(F_k(V))$ of $s^*K$ contains $x_0$. On it, $H = \bar F_k \circ \pi_K$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altH_eq_mapBar⟧: a point $(h, a)$ with $h = F_k(q)$ has $q = s(h) = p(a)$, so $H(h, a)$ has source $a$ and lies over $F_k(p(a))$, which is (19.10) for $\bar F_k(a)$. Here $\pi_K$ is a strong bundle map ⟦Chapter5/StrongBundleMap · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleMap_pullbackProj'⟧ and $\bar F_k$ is sc-smooth on its domain (Theorem 19.5.1 for $E_\mu$, at $H(x_0)$, which lies over $k$).
- *Step 4, $H^{-1}$ is sc-smooth* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isScSmoothMap_altHinv⟧: its components $p$ (Step 2) and $s$ (a local sc-diffeomorphism) are sc-smooth, and a map into a pull-back is sc-smooth when its components are ⟦Chapter5/StrongBundleMap · StrongBundle.isScSmoothOn_to_pullback⟧. So $H$ is an sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isScDiffeoOn_altH⟧ and in particular a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altHHomeo⟧.
- *Step 5, the fibres.* $H$ identifies $p^{-1}(h)$ with $\pi^{-1}(h) \cong K_{s(h)}$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.fiberEquivBar⟧. The fibres of $p$ receive the linear structure transported by this bijection ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.instAddCommGroupFiberBar⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.instModuleFiberBar⟧, so that $H$ is linear on fibres ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.fiberLinBar⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.fiberLinBar_apply⟧.
- *Step 6, the transport.* For a strong bundle $Y \to X$, a homeomorphism $e : Y \to Y'$ and fibre-linear isomorphisms given by $e$ over the identity of $X$, the transported strong bundle $Y' \to X$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.transport⟧ has the charts $\Phi \circ e^{-1}$, for $\Phi$ in the atlas of $Y$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundleChart.transport⟧:
  - transported charts have the same overlaps and transition maps as the original ones, hence are compatible ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundleChart.transport_overlap⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundleChart.transport_transition⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundleChart.transport_compatible⟧;
  - the fibres are Banachable through $e$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.transportFiberCLE⟧;
  - the level-$(0,1)$ part is $e(Y_{0,1})$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.mem_oneSet_transport_iff⟧;
  - in the charts $\Phi$ and $\Phi \circ e^{-1}$, both $e$ and $e^{-1}$ are the identity of the local model, at level $0$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.isScSmoothMap_transport⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.isScSmoothMap_transport_symm⟧ and at level $1$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.isScSmoothMap_one_transport⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.isScSmoothMap_one_transport_symm⟧.

  So $e$ and $e^{-1}$ are strong bundle maps covering the identity ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleMap_transport⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleMap_transport_symm⟧, and $e$ is a strong bundle isomorphism ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleIso_transport⟧ ⟦Chapter5/StrongBundleMap · StrongBundle.IsStrongBundleIso⟧. The strong bundle of the theorem is the transport of $s^*K$ along $H$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.bundleBar⟧.

**Proof of Theorem 19.6.13, the assertions.**
- *Step 1, $H$* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_H⟧: Step 6 for $e = H$. An isomorphism is also one over the whole base in the sense of Chapter 8 ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.IsStrongBundleIso.isStrongBundleIsoOn_univ⟧ ⟦Chapter8/EpStrongBundle · StrongBundle.IsStrongBundleIsoOn⟧, in particular $H^{-1}$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIsoOn_altHinv⟧.
- *Step 2, the total space* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_total⟧: the identity from the transported level-$0$ structure to the natural M-polyfold $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ is $H \circ H^{-1}$, with $H^{-1}$ sc-smooth for the transported structure (Step 1) and $H$ sc-smooth into the natural one (Step 3 of the construction). The reverse direction uses the two other halves.
- *Step 3, $\bar s$* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_s⟧: $\bar s = \pi_K \circ H^{-1}$, (19.20). $\pi_K : s^*K \to K$ is a strong bundle map covering $s$, and $H^{-1}$ is a strong bundle map covering the identity.
- *Step 4, $\bar s$ is local* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_s_local⟧. Over the sheet $F_x(V(o)) \ni x$, $s$ is an sc-diffeomorphism onto $V(o)$, the inverse of $F_x$ (Theorem 19.5.1). Over such a sheet, $\pi_K : s^*K \to K$ is a strong bundle isomorphism covering $s$ ⟦Chapter10/StrongBundleWeakFibredProductPullback · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleIsoOn_pullbackProj'⟧. Composing with $H^{-1}$, an isomorphism over the whole base, gives an isomorphism over a neighbourhood of $x$ ⟦Chapter10/StrongBundleEquivalence · StrongBundle.IsStrongBundleIsoOn.exists_comp⟧.
- *Step 5, $\bar t$* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_t⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_t_local⟧: $\bar t = \Theta \circ H^{-1}$ with $\Theta = t \circ H : s^*K \to K'$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.tgt_eq_altTheta_comp⟧. Over every sheet $F_k(V(o))$, $\Theta = \bar f_k \circ \pi_K$. This composite is a local strong bundle isomorphism covering $t$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isLocalIso₂_altTheta⟧: (A) for $\bar f_k$ composed with the local isomorphism $\pi_K$ over the sheet ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.exists_isLocalIso₂_pullbackProj'_src⟧ ⟦Chapter10/StrongBundleLocalIso · StrongBundle.IsStrongBundleIsoOn.exists_isLocalIso₂⟧. So $\Theta$ is a strong bundle map ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_altTheta⟧ by the local criterion ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.IsStrongBundleMap.of_isLocalIso₂⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.IsStrongBundleMap.of_local⟧, and the local isomorphism becomes one in the sense of Chapter 8 ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.IsLocalIso₂.isStrongBundleIsoOn⟧. Composition with $H^{-1}$ gives both assertions, as in Steps 3 and 4.
- *Step 6, the unit* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_u⟧: $\bar u = H \circ (u \circ p, \mathrm{Id})$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.unitBar_eq⟧. Both sides of $\bar u(a) = H(u(p(a)), a)$ have source $a$ and lie over $u(p(a))$. The pair $a \mapsto (u(p(a)), a)$ into $s^*K$ is a strong bundle map covering $u$ ⟦Chapter10/StrongBundleWeakFibredProductPullback · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleMap_pullbackPair⟧, since $u$ is sc-smooth (Corollary 19.5.2 ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · TransitionData.isScSmoothMap_unit⟧) and the identity of $K$ is a strong bundle map.
- *Step 7, the inversion* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_inv⟧. $\bar\iota$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.invBar⟧ equals $H_{\bar\Psi'\bar\Psi} \circ (A \circ C) \circ H^{-1}$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.invBar_eq⟧, with $C$ and $A$ of (19.17), (19.18) (§XIX.21): the text's $\bar\iota((q, \varphi, q'), k) = (\iota(q, \varphi, q'), C(k))$. Both sides of the identity have the same target and the same image under $p$. $C$ is a strong bundle map (§XIX.21, ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_altC⟧) and $A$ is a strong bundle isomorphism covering $\iota$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_altA⟧. So $\bar\iota$ is a strong bundle map covering $\iota$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_invBar⟧. Its inverse is the inversion of $M(\bar\Psi', \bar\Psi)$, since $\iota \circ \iota = \mathrm{id}$.
