- *Step 8, the multiplication* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleMultiplication · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_m⟧. The base is the fibred product $M(\Psi', \Psi'') \,{}_s\!\times_t M(\Psi, \Psi')$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleMultiplication · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.BFP⟧. It is a sub-M-polyfold of the product since $s$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleMultiplication · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isSubMPolyfold_BFP⟧ ⟦Chapter2/FibredProduct · isSubMPolyfold_fibredProduct⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleMultiplication · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.BFPpoly⟧. The projection $\mathrm{pr}_2$ and the multiplication $m$ are sc-smooth ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleMultiplication · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isScSmoothMap_pr₂BFP⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleMultiplication · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isScSmoothMap_mulBase⟧ (Corollary 19.5.2 ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · TransitionData.isScSmoothMap_mul⟧).
  - The domain of $\bar m$ is the pull-back of $p : M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi') \to M(\Psi, \Psi')$ by $\mathrm{pr}_2$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleMultiplication · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.mulBundle⟧. A point $((h', h), \bar h)$ stands for the pair $(\bar h', \bar h)$, where $\bar h'$ is the element of $M(\bar\Psi', \bar\Psi'')$ over $h'$ with source $t(\bar h)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleMultiplication · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.mulLift⟧, and $\bar m((h', h), \bar h) = m(\bar h', \bar h)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleMultiplication · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.mBar⟧.
  - $\bar s \circ \pi'$ is a strong bundle map into $K$ covering $s \circ \mathrm{pr}_2$, by Step 3 and the pull-back projection. So the pair $w \mapsto (m(\pi(w)), \bar s(\pi'(w)))$ into $s^*K$ over $M(\Psi, \Psi'')$ is a strong bundle map covering $m$ ⟦Chapter10/StrongBundleWeakFibredProductPullback · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleMap_pullbackPair⟧. Composing with $H$ for $(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi'')$ gives a strong bundle map.
  - This composite is $\bar m$: both sides have source $s(\bar h)$ and lie over $m(h', h)$, by the compatibility of $p$ with $m$ and with the lifts (§XIX.17).

  For a basic construction the representative supplies $\bar F$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleMultiplication · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_m⟧.
- *Step 9, the statement* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_of_bundleTransitionData⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_13⟧: Steps 1–7 for the representative, whose $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ is the space of Lemma 19.6.12 ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.MB_eq_MBar⟧. The printed form ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_book⟧ ignores its two hypotheses. The witness applies the printed form to the reflection bundle, with both hypotheses verified ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · reflBan_theorem_19_6_13⟧ (§XIX.22).

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text asks for $T$ metrizable (new in 2021); in the formalization the M-polyfold structures of Theorem 19.5.1 are defined from the transition data alone and the transition sets are always metrizable (Lemma 19.6.12), so the strong bundle structure needs no hypothesis, and the printed form is kept. "A natural structure of an M-polyfold bundle" is read as a strong bundle structure over the natural M-polyfold $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ whose total space is the natural M-polyfold $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$, natural in the sense that $H$ is a strong bundle isomorphism (p. 901). "Locally diffeomorphic strong bundle maps" is read as: strong bundle maps which near every fibre are strong bundle isomorphisms onto open sets. The proof is the one the text gives in the alternative approach, with the transport of a strong bundle along a homeomorphism as the tool the text uses without naming it. The domain of the multiplication printed on p. 904 is read as the pull-back of $p$ by $\mathrm{pr}_2$, through the identification by $H$.

### XIX.21 The alternative approach (19.14)–(19.18), Definition 19.6.15, and the agreement of the structures I and II (2017: §19.6), pp. 900–905

**Statement.** *The fibred products and the maps $H$, $H'$, $C$* (pp. 900–902, restated) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.SrcPB⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.TgtPB⟧: for transition data $F$ of the underlying construction, the fibred products $M(\Psi, \Psi') \,{}_s\!\times_p K$ and $M(\Psi, \Psi') \,{}_t\!\times_{p'} K'$ of (19.14) are strong bundles over $M(\Psi, \Psi')$, the pull-backs of Proposition 2.6.8 ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.srcBundle⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.tgtBundle⟧. The maps of (19.16), $H((q, \varphi, q'), k) = (k, \varphi, \bar\Psi'^{-1}\hat\varphi\bar\Psi(k))$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altH⟧ and $H'((q, \varphi, q'), k') = (\bar\Psi^{-1}\widehat{\varphi^{-1}}\bar\Psi'(k'), \varphi, k')$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altH'⟧, are bijections ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altH_bijective⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altH'_bijective⟧ covering $p$. $C = H'^{-1} \circ H$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altC⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altC_eq⟧ is a bijection ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altC_bijective⟧ over the identity ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.pullbackProj_altC⟧. In (19.15) we read the map named source map $t$ as the target map $t$. *Definition 19.6.15* (p. 902, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.IsCoherent⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_6_15⟧: $(\bar F, F)$ is coherent if for all $\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi'$ the map $C$ is a strong bundle map covering the identity and a strong bundle isomorphism over all of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$. The map $A((q, \varphi, q'), k') = ((q', \varphi^{-1}, q), k')$ of (19.18) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altA⟧ is a strong bundle isomorphism covering $\iota$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_altA⟧. $C$ is a strong bundle isomorphism covering the identity iff $A \circ C$ is one covering $\iota$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altC_iso_iff_altA_comp⟧, so coherence is equivalent to the form in terms of $A \circ C$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.IsCoherentA⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isCoherent_iff_isCoherentA⟧. The underlying data of bundle transition data are always coherent ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isCoherent_of_bundleTransitionData⟧, with the witness ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · reflBan_isCoherent⟧. *The structures I and II agree* (pp. 901–903, explicit) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.comparison_I_II⟧. Structure II is the transport of $s^*K$ along $H$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.transport⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleIso_transport⟧, and its topology is the one coinduced by $H$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.coinduced_altHE⟧. A structure for which $H$ is a strong bundle isomorphism agrees with it ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_id_of_altH⟧. For it, $C$ and $H'$ are strong bundle isomorphisms ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_altC⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_altH'⟧, so the $H$- and $H'$-structures agree ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_id_of_altH_altH'⟧. For bundle transition data $\bar F$, a strong bundle structure I on $p : M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi') \to M(\Psi, \Psi')$ agrees with II (the identity is a strong bundle isomorphism) under the added hypotheses that the source map $\bar s : M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi') \to K$ is a strong bundle map covering $s$ for I, and near every fibre a strong bundle isomorphism onto an open set. These hypotheses are the property of the first approach that the text uses. Their witnesses are II itself ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.comparison_I_II_hyp_witness⟧ and the reflection bundle ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · reflBan_comparison_I_II⟧, with non-trivial isotropy and a non-trivial fibre action. The printed form, with the coherence hypothesis of the second approach, is kept ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.comparison_I_II_book⟧.

**Proof of (19.14)–(19.17).** *Step 1, the pull-backs* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.srcBundle⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.tgtBundle⟧. Over $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ with its M-polyfold structure of Theorem 19.5.1 ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · TransitionData.transPolyfold⟧, the source and target maps are sc-smooth, being local sc-diffeomorphisms ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isScSmoothMap_trSrc⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isScSmoothMap_trTgt⟧. So the pull-back atlas of Proposition 2.6.8 ⟦Chapter2/StrongBundlePullback · pullbackAtlas⟧ applies. The projection is continuous and surjective, the fibres are those of $K$ (resp. $K'$) and hence Banachable ⟦Chapter2/StrongBundlePullback · pullbackFiberCLE⟧, and the total spaces are paracompact ⟦Chapter2/StrongBundlePullback · paracompactSpace_pullbackSpace⟧. *Step 2, $H$ and $H'$.* $H$ is the lift of §XIX.17 at $(q, \varphi, q')$ and $k$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altH⟧, with source $k$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.src_altH⟧ over $(q, \varphi, q')$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.proj_altH⟧. $H'$ is the inverse of the lift from $\bar\Psi'$ to $\bar\Psi$ at $\iota(q, \varphi, q')$ and $k'$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altH'⟧. It has target $k'$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.tgt_altH'⟧ and lies over $\iota(\iota(q, \varphi, q')) = (q, \varphi, q')$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.proj_altH'⟧. *Step 3, bijections.* $H$ is injective because its image determines the base point (by $p$) and $k$ (by $s$); it is surjective because $\bar h = H(p(\bar h), s(\bar h))$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altH_bijective⟧. $H'$ likewise, with $t$ in place of $s$ and the uniqueness by target and image ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altH'_bijective⟧. *Step 4, $C$.* $C((q, \varphi, q'), k) = ((q, \varphi, q'), t(H((q, \varphi, q'), k)))$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altC⟧. Then $H' \circ C = H$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altH'_altC⟧, since both sides have the same target and image. So $C = H'^{-1} \circ H$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altC_eq⟧, it covers the identity by construction ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.pullbackProj_altC⟧, and it is a bijection ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altC_bijective⟧. Its inverse is $C^{-1}((q, \varphi, q'), k') = ((q, \varphi, q'), s(H'((q, \varphi, q'), k')))$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altCinv⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altCinv_altC⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altC_altCinv⟧, with $H \circ C^{-1} = H'$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altH_altCinv⟧.

**Proof of Definition 19.6.15 and the facts about $A$.** *Step 1, the definition* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleAlternative · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_6_15⟧: coherence holds by definition, in the form above ⟦Chapter8/EpStrongBundle · StrongBundle.IsStrongBundleIsoOn⟧. *Step 2, $A$* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altA⟧. $A$ covers the inversion $\iota$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.trInv⟧, which is sc-smooth (Corollary 19.5.2 ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · TransitionData.isScSmoothMap_inv⟧). Its fibre component is the pull-back projection $\pi_{K'}$, a strong bundle map ⟦Chapter5/StrongBundleMap · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleMap_pullbackProj'⟧. So $A$ is a strong bundle map into the pull-back ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_altA⟧ by the pairing lemma ⟦Chapter10/StrongBundleWeakFibredProductPullback · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleMap_pullbackPair⟧. Likewise $A^{-1}((q', \varphi^{-1}, q), k') = ((q, \varphi, q'), k')$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altAinv⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_altAinv⟧. The two are inverse since $\iota \circ \iota = \mathrm{id}$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.trInv_trInv⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altAinv_altA⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altA_altAinv⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_altA⟧. *Step 3, $C$ versus $A \circ C$* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altC_iso_iff_altA_comp⟧. $\Rightarrow$: compose with $A$, an isomorphism over the whole base. $\Leftarrow$: compose $A \circ C$ with $A^{-1}$, which covers $\iota$; then $\iota \circ \iota = \mathrm{id}$ and $A^{-1} \circ A \circ C = C$ turn the composite into $C$ covering the identity. Applied to every pair, this gives the $A \circ C$ form of coherence ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isCoherent_iff_isCoherentA⟧.

**Proof that bundle transition data are coherent.** *Step 1, $C$ is a strong bundle map* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_altC⟧: $C = (\pi, \Theta)$ with $\Theta = t \circ H$ a strong bundle map covering $t$ (Step 5 of the assertions of Theorem 19.6.13, §XIX.20, ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_altTheta⟧). The pairing lemma for the pull-back by $t$ applies with the identity on the base. *Step 2, $C$ is a local isomorphism over every sheet* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isLocalIso₂_altC⟧. Over the sheet $F_k(V(o))$, $t$ is an sc-diffeomorphism onto $f_k(V(o))$, so the projection $t^*K' \to K'$ is a local strong bundle isomorphism covering $t$ there ⟦Chapter10/StrongBundleWeakFibredProductPullback · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleIsoOn_pullbackProj'⟧. $\Theta$ is one over the same sheet ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isLocalIso₂_altTheta⟧, and $C$ is the composite of $\Theta$ with the inverse of that projection. The sheets cover $M(\Psi, \Psi')$, since $h$ lies on its own sheet ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.mem_image_map_self⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.mem_image_map⟧. *Step 3, globally* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isCoherent_of_bundleTransitionData⟧. A bijective strong bundle map covering the identity that is near every fibre a strong bundle isomorphism is one over the whole base ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleIsoOn_univ_of_local⟧. The inverse is $C^{-1}$, sc-smooth at levels $0$ and $1$ because it agrees locally with the local inverses. Steps 1, 2 and Step 4 of the previous proof supply the hypotheses.

**Proof that the structures agree.**
- *Step 1, the topology* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.coinduced_altHE⟧: $H$ is a homeomorphism onto $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ with $\bar T$ (§XIX.20), so the topology coinduced by $H$, which the second approach uses, is $\bar T$.
- *Step 2, the abstract comparison* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleMap_id_of_isStrongBundleIsoOn⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleIso_id_of_isStrongBundleIsoOn⟧. Let $B_1$, $B_2$ be two strong bundle structures on $P : Y \to X$, and $S : Y \to Y''$ a strong bundle map covering $f$ for both, which near every point is a strong bundle isomorphism for $B_2$ (resp. $B_1$). Then the identity is a strong bundle map $B_1 \to B_2$: near $x$, with the local inverse $\Psi$ of $S$ for $B_2$, the identity equals $\Psi \circ S$ on $P^{-1}(U)$, and $S$ is sc-smooth for $B_1$. The same holds at level $1$ with the level-$1$ maps. By symmetry the identity is a strong bundle isomorphism. This is the argument the text gives at the end of the section: since the maps to $K$ are local strong bundle isomorphisms, the identity is a local strong bundle isomorphism, hence a strong bundle isomorphism.
- *Step 3, uniqueness of the $H$-structure* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_id_of_altH⟧: Step 2 with $S = H^{-1}$ into $s^*K$, an isomorphism over the whole base for both structures.
- *Step 4, $C$ and $H'$.* $C^{-1} = (\pi', \Theta_{\bar\Psi'\bar\Psi} \circ A)$ is a strong bundle map covering the identity ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_altCinv⟧, since $s(H'(y)) = t(H_{\bar\Psi'\bar\Psi}(A(y)))$. So $C$ is a strong bundle isomorphism ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_altC⟧. $H'$ into $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altH'E⟧ is $H \circ C^{-1}$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altH'E_eq⟧, and its inverse $\bar h \mapsto (p(\bar h), t(\bar h))$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altH'inv⟧ is $C \circ H^{-1}$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.altH'inv_eq⟧. Composites of strong bundle isomorphisms make $H'$ one onto structure II ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_altH'⟧. Step 2 with $S = H'^{-1}$ gives the uniqueness of the $H'$-structure ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_id_of_altH'⟧. Composing the two identities shows that the $H$- and $H'$-structures are the same ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_id_of_altH_altH'⟧.
- *Step 5, I = II* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.comparison_I_II⟧: Step 2 with $S = \bar s$. For I, $\bar s$ is a strong bundle map and locally an isomorphism by hypothesis. For II, by Theorem 19.6.13 ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_s⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_6_13_s_local⟧. The total space of I is then the natural M-polyfold $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi')$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.comparison_I_II_total⟧, composing with Theorem 19.6.13 (total space). The printed form takes the coherence hypothesis and does not use it ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.comparison_I_II_book⟧, since coherence follows from $\bar F$.
- *Step 6, the witnesses.* Structure II satisfies the added hypotheses ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · StrongBundleUniformizerConstruction.comparison_I_II_hyp_witness⟧. For the reflection bundle, coherence and the full statement of Theorem 19.6.13 hold ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · reflBan_comparison_I_II⟧, including the $A \circ C$ form ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · reflBan_isCoherentA⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The fibred products (19.14) are the pull-backs of Definition 2.6.7; in (19.15) the map named source map $t$ is read as the target map $t$. The M-polyfold structure of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is defined from the transition data alone, so the properness condition of the text is not a hypothesis of these statements. "$C$ is a strong bundle isomorphism" is read as a strong bundle map covering the identity which is a strong bundle isomorphism over all of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$, and "$A$ is a strong bundle isomorphism covering $\iota$" is read globally. The text treats coherence as an assumption of the second approach; the formalization shows that it holds whenever $F$ underlies bundle transition data. The comparison is stated for a structure I with the local property the text uses ("by construction"), and the printed coherence hypothesis is kept in a separate form. In the comparison diagram the vertical arrows are the source maps $\bar s$; the text's phrase "target maps" there is read as "source maps".

### XIX.22 The witness: the reflection on $\mathbb R$ with fibre action $-1$

**Statement.** *The reflection bundle* (no counterpart in the text; extra) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBan⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanU⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanBTD⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanBasic⟧: $\mathcal S = \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$, with $\mathbb Z/2 = \{\pm 1\}$ acting by $x \mapsto \pm x$, and $\mu(x) = \{x\} \times \mathbb R$, $\mu(g, x)(x, v) = (gx, gv)$. Then:
- the group acts non-trivially on the fibres ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBan_lift_ne⟧;
- a strong bundle uniformizer construction, bundle transition data and a basic strong bundle construction exist;
- the lift of the transition of $(0, -1, 0)$ is $-\mathrm{Id}$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBan_fmapBar_neg⟧;
- every class of $\mathcal S$ meets $F$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanBasic_hB⟧;
- $T$ and $\bar T$ are metrizable ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanBasic_metrizable⟧, and so is $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanBasic_witness⟧.

It is a non-degenerate instance, with non-trivial isotropy acting non-trivially on the fibre, for the hypotheses used in §19.6. It is the witness for Theorem 19.6.8, Proposition 19.6.9, Theorem 19.6.13 and the comparison (§§XIX.18, XIX.20, XIX.21).

**Proof.** *Step 1, the functor $\mu$* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBan⟧. The base is the translation category $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · ReflCat⟧, and $E = \mathbb R \times \mathbb R$ is the trivial bundle of Chapter 3 ⟦Chapter3/NonVacuity · TrivialBundle.bundle⟧, with fibres $\{x\} \times \mathbb R \cong \mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflFibCLE⟧. The lift of $(g, x)$ is $(x, v) \mapsto (gx, gv)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflLift⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflLift_apply⟧, bounded through $v \mapsto gv$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · unitsCLM⟧. It satisfies (1) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflLift_unit⟧ since $1 \cdot e = e$, and (2) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflLift_comp⟧ since $(g'g) \cdot e = g' \cdot (g \cdot e)$. The action on $E$ is the diagonal action ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBan_act⟧. *Step 2, non-triviality* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBan_lift_neg⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBan_lift_ne⟧: $\mu(-1, 0)(0, v) = (0, -v)$, and $(0, -1) \ne (0, 1)$. *Step 3, the uniformizer.* $\bar\Psi$ is the identity of $K = \mathbb R \times \mathbb R$ with the diagonal action, mapping $(g, a)$ to $((g, a_1), a)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanFunctor⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanUnif⟧. It is injective on objects; it is full, because a morphism $((g, a_1), a)$ of $E_\mu$ from $a$ is the image of $(g, a)$; and it is faithful, because the image determines $g$ and $a$. Each $a \mapsto ga$ is a strong bundle map covering $x \mapsto gx$, being a smooth base map times a linear fibre map ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflHmap⟧ ⟦Chapter16/FibreNegationStructure · isStrongBundleMap_prodLinMap⟧. The structure (1) ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanStr⟧ has $O = \mathbb R$, $\Psi$ the identity functor, $p$ the trivial bundle projection and the identities as fibre isomorphisms; every field holds by definition. The construction is $\bar F(x) = \{\bar\Psi\}$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanU⟧, with $\bar\Psi$ at every $x$, and every member is this one ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanU_unif_eq⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanU_base_eq⟧. *Step 4, the transition data* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanTD⟧: $F_h(q) = (q, g, gq)$ for $h = (o, g, go)$, with $V(o) = \mathbb R$. They are built by the keyed construction of §19.4 ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · ofKey⟧: an element of $M(\Psi, \Psi)$ is determined by its source and group element ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBase_trans_ext⟧, $q \mapsto gq$ is an sc-diffeomorphism with inverse $q \mapsto g^{-1}q$, and the keys multiply. So $F_h(q) = \hat\Psi(g, q)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanTD_map⟧ and $f_h(q) = gq$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanTD_fmap⟧. *Step 5, the lifts and (A)* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBan_fmapBar⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanBTD⟧. By the formula $\bar\Psi(\bar f_h(a)) = \hat\varphi(\bar\Psi(a))$ (§XIX.17), $\bar f_h(x, v) = (gx, gv)$. The maps $a \mapsto ga$ and $a \mapsto g^{-1}a$ are mutually inverse strong bundle maps over mutually inverse sc-diffeomorphisms, hence a strong bundle isomorphism over all of $\mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter10/StrongBundleEquivalenceNat · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleIsoOn_univ_of_inverse⟧. That is a local strong bundle isomorphism onto $K|f_h(\mathbb R) = K$ ⟦Chapter10/StrongBundleLocalIso · StrongBundle.IsStrongBundleIsoOn.exists_isLocalIso₂⟧, which is (A). For $h = (0, -1, 0)$, $\bar f_h = -\mathrm{Id}$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBan_fmapBar_neg⟧. *Step 6, the hypotheses* ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanBasic⟧. Every class $|x|$ contains $x$ with $\Psi \in F(x)$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanBasic_hB⟧. $|\Psi|$ is onto $|\mathcal S|$, so $T$ is metrizable ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.metrizableSpace_natTop_of_surjective⟧: by Theorem 19.2.2 (3), $|\Psi|$ is then a homeomorphism from the metrizable $\mathbb Z/2 \backslash \mathbb R$. Proposition 19.6.9 gives $\bar T$ metrizable ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanBasic_metrizable⟧, and Lemma 19.6.12 gives $M(\bar\Psi, \bar\Psi)$ metrizable. All of this is collected in one statement ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleExamples · reflBanBasic_witness⟧. The witnesses of the later sections apply the theorems to this construction: Theorem 19.6.13 ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTheorem · reflBan_theorem_19_6_13⟧, coherence ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleCoherence · reflBan_isCoherent⟧ and the comparison ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleComparison · reflBan_comparison_I_II⟧.

### XIX.23 Definition 19.7.1, Remark 19.7.2, Definitions 19.7.3, 19.7.4, Lemma 19.7.5, Remark 19.7.6 (2017: Definitions 19.15–19.17, Remark 19.10, Lemma 19.1, Remark 19.11), pp. 906–908

**Statement.** *Definition 19.7.1* (p. 906, same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · GroupoidalCategory.IsFiniteToOneCovering⟧: a functor $P : \mathcal A \to \mathcal B$ of groupoidal categories is a finite-to-one covering functor if (1) it is surjective on objects and every preimage $P^{-1}(\beta)$ is finite, and (2) the map $\hat\varphi \mapsto (P(\hat\varphi), s(\hat\varphi))$ is a bijection from the morphisms of $\mathcal A$ onto the pairs $(\varphi, \alpha)$ with $s(\varphi) = P(\alpha)$. Equivalently, every $\varphi$ with $s(\varphi) = P(\alpha)$ has a unique lift $\hat\varphi$ with $P(\hat\varphi) = \varphi$ and $s(\hat\varphi) = \alpha$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · IsFiniteToOneCovering.lift⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · IsFiniteToOneCovering.existsUnique_lift⟧. *Remark 19.7.2* (p. 906, same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · GroupoidalCategory.remark_19_7_2⟧: the definition involves no topology; the identity functor of any groupoidal category is one. *Definition 19.7.3* (p. 906, same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · GroupoidalCategory.CoveringUniformizer⟧: a proper covering uniformizer for $P$ at $\beta$ consists of:
- a uniformizer $\Psi : G \ltimes O_\beta \to \mathcal B$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · Uniformizer⟧;
- an ep-groupoid $E$ with Hausdorff, paracompact object and morphism spaces;
- a functor $\hat\tau : E \to G \ltimes O_\beta$ which is a proper ep-groupoid covering model in the sense of Definition 17.8.4 ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringModel · ProperCoveringModel⟧;
- a functor $\hat\Psi : E \to \mathcal A$.

These satisfy (1) $\Psi(o_\beta) = \beta$ for the distinguished point $o_\beta$ of the model; (2) $\hat\Psi$ is injective on objects, full and faithful, and $P \circ \hat\Psi = \Psi \circ \hat\tau$; (3) the square (19.22) is cartesian. *Definition 19.7.4* (p. 907, same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.definition_19_7_4⟧: cartesian means that on objects the image of $e \mapsto (\hat\tau(e), \hat\Psi(e))$ is the set of pairs $(o, a)$ with $P(a) = \Psi(o)$, each pair with a unique $e$, and that every $\hat\varphi$ with $P(\hat\varphi) = \Psi(g, o)$ is $\hat\Psi(\hat g)$ for some $\hat g$ with $\hat\tau(\hat g) = (g, o)$. The 2021 Definition 19.7.4 differs from the 2017 Definition 19.17. *Lemma 19.7.5* (p. 907, same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · GroupoidalCategory.lemma_19_7_5⟧: for a proper covering model $\hat\tau$ and a functor $\hat\Psi$, at most one functor $\Psi : G \ltimes O \to \mathcal B$ satisfies $P \circ \hat\Psi = \Psi \circ \hat\tau$. With the printed formulas, $\Psi(o) = P(\hat\Psi(\sigma_a(o)))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.obj_eq⟧ and $\Psi(g, o) = P(\hat\Psi(\hat g))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.mor_eq⟧. *Remark 19.7.6* (p. 908, same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.remark_19_7_6⟧: so $\Psi$ is determined by $\hat\Psi$, and one may speak of the uniformizer $\hat\Psi$.

**Proof of Definition 19.7.1 and Remark 19.7.2.** *Step 1, the lift* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · IsFiniteToOneCovering.lift⟧. For $s(\varphi) = P(\alpha)$, the pair $(\varphi, \alpha)$ is in the target of the bijection (2). Its preimage $\hat\varphi$ satisfies $P(\hat\varphi) = \varphi$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · IsFiniteToOneCovering.P_lift⟧ and $s(\hat\varphi) = \alpha$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · IsFiniteToOneCovering.src_lift⟧. *Step 2, uniqueness.* Injectivity of (2) says that a morphism of $\mathcal A$ is determined by its image and its source ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · IsFiniteToOneCovering.mor_ext⟧. So every morphism with these two properties is the lift ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · IsFiniteToOneCovering.eq_lift⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · IsFiniteToOneCovering.existsUnique_lift⟧. *Step 3, Remark 19.7.2* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · GroupoidalCategory.remark_19_7_2⟧. For the identity functor, (1) holds since the preimage of $\beta$ is $\{\beta\}$. The map of (2) is $\varphi \mapsto (\varphi, s(\varphi))$, injective by its first component and onto, since a pair $(\varphi, \alpha)$ with $s(\varphi) = \alpha$ is the image of $\varphi$.

**Proof of Definitions 19.7.3 and 19.7.4.** *Step 1, the data* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · GroupoidalCategory.CoveringUniformizer⟧. The uniformizer $\Psi$ carries the conditions of (2) for $\Psi$ as its own fields. The model of Definition 17.8.4 provides:
- the point $\bar o$, invariant under $G$;
- the surjectivity of $\hat\tau$ on objects and its local sc-diffeomorphism property;
- the disjoint open sets $U(y)$, $y \in \hat\tau^{-1}(\bar o)$, covering $E$, on each of which $\hat\tau$ is an sc-diffeomorphism onto $O$ with inverse $\sigma_y$;
- the sc-diffeomorphism $\hat g \mapsto (\hat\tau(\hat g), s(\hat g))$ of (3).

The distinguished point $o_\beta$ of (1) is this $\bar o$. The fields of the structure are (1), the three properties of $\hat\Psi$ in (2), the commutativity on objects and on morphisms, and (3) in the form of Definition 19.7.4 without the uniqueness of $e$. *Step 2, "with isotropy $G$"* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.isCentredAt⟧: $\Psi(\bar o) = \beta$ and $\bar o$ is invariant under $G$, so $\Psi$ is centred at $\beta$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · Uniformizer.IsCentredAt⟧. Then $G \cong \mathrm{Aut}(\beta)$ ⟦Chapter17/LocalUniformizer · Uniformizer.isotropyEquiv⟧. *Step 3, Definition 19.7.4 as printed* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.definition_19_7_4⟧. The image of $e \mapsto (\hat\tau(e), \hat\Psi(e))$ lies in the set of pairs with $P(a) = \Psi(o)$ by the commutativity $P(\hat\Psi(e)) = \Psi(\hat\tau(e))$. It is all of it by the cartesian field on objects. The $e$ is unique because $\hat\Psi$ is injective on objects. The part on morphisms is the field itself.

**Proof of Lemma 19.7.5.** *Step 1, objects* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · GroupoidalCategory.lemma_19_7_5⟧. Let $\Psi_1, \Psi_2$ both satisfy $P \circ \hat\Psi = \Psi_i \circ \hat\tau$. Every $o$ is $\hat\tau(e)$ for some $e$, since $\hat\tau$ is surjective on objects. Then $\Psi_1(o) = P(\hat\Psi(e)) = \Psi_2(o)$. *Step 2, morphisms.* For $(g, o)$, choose $e$ over $o$. By Definition 17.8.4 (3) there is the morphism $\hat g$ of $E$ with source $e$ and group index $g$ ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringModel · ProperCoveringModel.liftMor⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringModel · ProperCoveringModel.src_liftMor⟧ ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringModel · ProperCoveringModel.groupIndex_liftMor⟧. Since $\hat\tau(\hat g)$ is (group index, $\hat\tau$ of the source) ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringModel · ProperCoveringModel.mor_eq⟧, $\hat\tau(\hat g) = (g, o)$, and $\Psi_1(g, o) = P(\hat\Psi(\hat g)) = \Psi_2(g, o)$. Only the commutativity and Definition 17.8.4 are used. *Step 3, the printed formulas.* For $a \in \hat\tau^{-1}(\bar o)$, the point $\sigma_a(o) \in U(a)$ lies over $o$ ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringModel · ProperCoveringModel.obj_σ⟧. This gives $\Psi(o) = P(\hat\Psi(\sigma_a(o)))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.obj_eq⟧, and with $\hat g$ the lift of $g$ at $\sigma_a(o)$, $\Psi(g, o) = P(\hat\Psi(\hat g))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.mor_eq⟧.

**Proof of Remark 19.7.6** ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.remark_19_7_6⟧. A functor $\Psi'$ with $P \circ \hat\Psi = \Psi' \circ \hat\tau$ for the same model and the same $\hat\Psi$ equals $\Psi$, by Lemma 19.7.5 applied to $\Psi'$ and $\Psi$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* In Definition 19.7.3, $E$ is an ep-groupoid and $\hat\Psi$ a functor of the underlying groupoids. The distinguished point $o_\beta$ is the point of the model of Definition 17.8.4, as in Corollary 19.7.20, which speaks of the distinguished element with $\Psi(\bar o) = \beta$; "with isotropy $G$" then follows. In Definition 19.7.4 the inclusion of the image in the set of pairs is the commutativity, and the uniqueness of $e$ follows from the injectivity of $\hat\Psi$. Lemma 19.7.5 is stated for any two functors $G \ltimes O \to \mathcal B$, not only for uniformizers.

### XIX.24 Definition 19.7.7, Remark 19.7.8, Lemmas 19.7.9, 19.7.10, Definition 19.7.11 and the compatibility reading (2017: Definitions 19.18, 19.19, Remark 19.12, Lemma 19.2), pp. 908–910

**Statement.** *Definition 19.7.7* (p. 908, same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · GroupoidalCategory.CoveringUniformizerConstruction⟧: a proper covering uniformizer construction $\hat F$ gives for every object $\beta$ of $\mathcal B$ a set $\hat F(\beta)$ of proper covering uniformizers for $P$ at $\beta$. It induces the uniformizer construction $F(\beta) = \{\Psi \mid (\hat\Psi, \Psi) \in \hat F(\beta)\}$ of (19.24) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizerConstruction.toUniformizerConstruction⟧. *Remark 19.7.8* (p. 908, same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizerConstruction.remark_19_7_8⟧: for $\hat\Psi \in \hat F(\beta)$ the associated $\Psi$ lies in $F(\beta)$ and is centred at $\beta$, and every element of $F(\beta)$ arises so. *The transition sets and the map $p$* (pp. 908–910, same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.Trans⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.transMap⟧: $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ is the set of triples $(e, \hat\varphi, e')$ with $\hat\varphi : \hat\Psi(e) \to \hat\Psi'(e')$, and (19.23) $p(e, \hat\varphi, e') = (\hat\tau(e), P(\hat\varphi), \hat\tau'(e'))$. The map $p$ commutes with the source, target, unit, inversion and multiplication ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.transMap_src_tgt⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.transMap_unit⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.transMap_inv⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.transMap_comp⟧. *Lemma 19.7.9* (p. 908, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.lemma_19_7_9⟧: if $P$ is a finite-to-one covering functor, then $p$ is surjective, and for $h = (o, \varphi, o')$ the map $x \mapsto s(x)$ is a bijection from $p^{-1}(h)$ onto $\hat\tau^{-1}(o)$; in particular $p^{-1}(h)$ is finite. *Lemma 19.7.10* (p. 909, same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.lemma_19_7_10⟧: under the same hypothesis, $(e, \hat\varphi, e') \mapsto (p(e, \hat\varphi, e'), e)$ is a bijection $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi') \to M(\Psi, \Psi') \,{}_s\!\times_{\hat\tau} E$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.FibTrans⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.transEquiv⟧. *Definition 19.7.11* (p. 910, explicit) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · GroupoidalCategory.CoveringBasicConstruction⟧: a basic construction for $P$ is a pair $(\hat F, \mathcal F)$, $\mathcal F$ a transition construction for the induced $F$, so that $(F, \mathcal F)$ is a basic construction for $\mathcal B$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringBasicConstruction.toBasic⟧. The standing reading that every class of $\mathcal B$ has a representative $\beta$ with $\hat F(\beta) \ne \emptyset$ is a named property ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringBasicConstruction.HasClassUniformizers⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringBasicConstruction.HasClassUniformizers.toBasic⟧. *The compatibility reading* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.IsCompatible⟧. We read Definition 19.7.11 as asking that the uniformizers $\hat\Psi$ be compatible with the transitions: for all members $\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi'$ the top target map $\hat t : M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi') \to E'$ is continuous. The opening of §19.7 calls the uniformizers compatible, and Definition 19.6.4 (A) asks the analogous property of the lifted maps. The literal definition does not ask it. The hypothesis is weak: its witness is the absolute-value construction, where $|\mathcal A| \cong \mathbb R$ covers $|\mathcal B| \cong [0, \infty)$ by the absolute value ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic_isCompatible⟧. Without it, Proposition 19.7.15 (2) and Lemma 19.7.17 do not hold as we read them (§XIX.26).

**Proof of Definition 19.7.7 and Remark 19.7.8.** *Step 1* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizerConstruction.toUniformizerConstruction⟧. $F(\beta)$ is the image of $\hat F(\beta)$ under $\hat\Psi \mapsto \Psi$. A member $\Psi$ is at $\beta$ because $\Psi(\bar o) = \beta$. A member of $\hat F$, with its $\beta$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizerConstruction.Unif⟧, gives the member $\Psi$ of $F$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizerConstruction.Unif.toUnif⟧. *Step 2, Remark 19.7.8* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizerConstruction.remark_19_7_8⟧: membership is by construction, centring is Step 2 of the proof of Definition 19.7.3 (§XIX.23), and every $\Psi \in F(\beta)$ is in the image by definition.

**Proof of the structural identities of $p$.** *Step 1, $p$ is well defined* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.transMap⟧: $s(P(\hat\varphi)) = P(s(\hat\varphi)) = P(\hat\Psi(e)) = \Psi(\hat\tau(e))$, and likewise at the target. *Step 2, the diagrams (19.30)–(19.32).* The source and target identities hold by definition ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.transMap_src_tgt⟧. For the unit, $P$ maps units to units and $P(\hat\Psi(e)) = \Psi(\hat\tau(e))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.transMap_unit⟧. For inversion, $P$ commutes with inverses ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.transMap_inv⟧. For multiplication, $P$ commutes with composition, and composable elements have composable images ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.transMap_comp⟧.

**Proof of Lemma 19.7.9.** *Step 1, finiteness of $\hat\tau^{-1}(o)$* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.finite_fiber_τ⟧. $\hat\Psi$ is injective on objects and maps $\hat\tau^{-1}(o)$ into $P^{-1}(\Psi(o))$, which is finite by Definition 19.7.1 (1). *Step 2, the lift over $h$* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.liftTrans⟧. Let $h = (o, \varphi, o')$ and $e$ with $\hat\tau(e) = o$. Then $s(\varphi) = \Psi(o) = P(\hat\Psi(e))$, so $\varphi$ has the lift $\hat\varphi$ with source $\hat\Psi(e)$ (Definition 19.7.1 (2)). Its target lies over $t(\varphi) = \Psi'(o')$. So the cartesian property of $\hat\Psi'$ gives $e'$ with $\hat\tau'(e') = o'$ and $\hat\Psi'(e') = t(\hat\varphi)$. The triple $(e, \hat\varphi, e')$ is an element with source $e$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.liftTrans_src⟧ over $h$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.transMap_liftTrans⟧. *Step 3, uniqueness* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.trans_ext⟧. Two elements with the same image under $p$ and the same source have the same morphism, since a morphism of $\mathcal A$ is determined by its image and its source. Then their targets have the same image under the injective $\hat\Psi'$. So every element is the lift of its image at its source ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.eq_liftTrans⟧. *Step 4, the lemma* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.lemma_19_7_9⟧. Surjectivity: given $h$, choose $e$ over $s(h)$ (Definition 17.8.4 (1)) and apply Step 2. On $p^{-1}(h)$ the source map lands in $\hat\tau^{-1}(s(h))$, since $\hat\tau(s(x)) = s(p(x))$. It is injective by Step 3 and onto by Step 2. Finiteness follows from Step 1.

**Proof of Lemma 19.7.10** ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.lemma_19_7_10⟧. The map $x \mapsto (p(x), s(x))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.fibredTransMap⟧ lands in the fibred product of the pairs $(h, e)$ with $s(h) = \hat\tau(e)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.FibTrans⟧. It is injective by Step 3 and onto by Step 2 of the previous proof, with inverse $(h, e) \mapsto$ the lift of $h$ at $e$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.transEquiv⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.transEquiv_apply⟧.

**Proof of Definition 19.7.11 and the class hypothesis.** *Step 1* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · GroupoidalCategory.CoveringBasicConstruction⟧: the data are $\hat F$ and a transition construction $\mathcal F$ for $F$ ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.TransitionConstruction⟧. The pair $(F, \mathcal F)$ is a basic construction for $\mathcal B$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringBasicConstruction.toBasic⟧ ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · BasicConstruction⟧. *Step 2* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringBasicConstruction.HasClassUniformizers.toBasic⟧: if every class has a $\beta$ with $\hat F(\beta) \ne \emptyset$, then $F(\beta) \ne \emptyset$ by Step 1 of the proof of Definition 19.7.7. That is the standing reading of Theorem 19.2.2 for $(F, \mathcal F)$ ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstruction · UniformizerConstruction.HasClassUniformizers⟧. *Step 3, the compatibility* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.IsCompatible⟧. With the topology on $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ of Proposition 19.7.13 (§XIX.25), $\hat t$, the target of an element ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.tgtHat⟧, is continuous for all pairs of members. Its consequences carry the suffix "hyp" in their names.

**Proof that the absolute-value construction is compatible.** *Step 1, the functor* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absCovP⟧. $\mathcal B = \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$ with $\mathbb Z/2 = \{\pm 1\}$ acting by reflection ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absCatB⟧, and $\mathcal A = \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes (\{\pm 1\} \times \mathbb R)$ with $k(i, x) = (ki, kx)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absCatA⟧. $P(i, x) = x$ and $P(k, (i, x)) = (k, x)$. It is a finite-to-one covering functor ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absCovP_isFiniteToOneCovering⟧: $P^{-1}(x) = \{\pm 1\} \times \{x\}$, and $(k, x)$ lifts uniquely at $(i, x)$ to $(k, (i, x))$. *Step 2, the uniformizers* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absCU⟧. For a slice $D$ of $\mathcal B$ (an open set $V$ with a subgroup $H$) and a point $\beta \in V$ invariant under $H$, the sheet model $H \ltimes (\{\pm 1\} \times V) \to H \ltimes V$ of Definition 17.8.4 ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringModel · sheetModel⟧ with $\hat\Psi(i, v) = (i, v)$ is a proper covering uniformizer at $\beta$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · sliceCU⟧:
- $\hat\Psi$ is injective;
- it is full, since a morphism $(k, (i, v))$ of $\mathcal A$ between points of the image has $k \in H$ by the slice property;
- it is faithful;
- it is cartesian, the point over $(o, a)$ being $(a_1, o)$.

$\hat F(0)$ is the global model with $H = \mathbb Z/2$, $V = \mathbb R$. $\hat F(\beta)$ for $\beta > 0$ (resp. $\beta < 0$) is the model over the slice $(0, \infty)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · posSlice⟧ (resp. $(-\infty, 0)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · negSlice⟧) with trivial group. *Step 3, the transitions* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · sliceData⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic⟧: $F_h(q) = (q, k, kq)$ for $h = (o, k, ko)$, the keyed construction of §19.4 ⟦Chapter19/BasicConstructionExamples · ofKey⟧. Every class has a uniformizer ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic_hasClassUniformizers⟧. *Step 4, compatibility* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic_isCompatible⟧. For an element $(e, \hat\varphi, e')$ the equations $s(\hat\varphi) = \hat\Psi(e)$ and $t(\hat\varphi) = \hat\Psi'(e')$ give $\hat t = (k(p(\cdot)) \cdot i(\hat s(\cdot)), t(p(\cdot)))$, with $k$ the group element. This is continuous: the group element is locally constant on $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · continuous_absM_key⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · sliceData_isOpenTr_key⟧, and $p$, $\hat s$ and $t$ are continuous (Propositions 19.7.14, 19.7.15 (2), §XIX.25).

*Where Lean departs from the text.* $\hat F : \mathcal B \to \mathrm{SET}$ is read on objects, as in Definition 19.1.3. The finiteness in Lemma 19.7.9 comes from Definition 19.7.1 (1); Definition 17.8.4 does not ask for finite fibres. Definition 19.7.11 is read with the compatibility of the uniformizers with the transitions, the continuity of every top target map $\hat t$. The literal definition asks for no relation between members of $\hat F(\beta)$. Two members over the same $\Psi$ may differ by a permutation of the sheets of $\hat\tau$ that is not locally constant (§XIX.26).

### XIX.25 Lemma 19.7.12, Propositions 19.7.13–19.7.15, pp. 911–915

**Statement.** *The topologies* (p. 910, same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.MB⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.Fib⟧: $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ carries its natural topology of Theorem 19.3.3, (19.25), and $M(\Psi, \Psi') \,{}_s\!\times_{\hat\tau} E$ the subspace topology of $M(\Psi, \Psi') \times E$. *Lemma 19.7.12* (p. 911, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.lemma_19_7_12⟧: $M(\Psi, \Psi') \,{}_s\!\times_{\hat\tau} E$ is locally metrizable, regular and Hausdorff; it is even metrizable ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.metrizableSpace_fib⟧. *Proposition 19.7.13* (p. 913, new in the 2021 edition; stronger) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.proposition_19_7_13⟧: for a finite-to-one covering functor $P$, $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ has a topology for which the bijection (19.26) is a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.fibHomeomorph⟧, and it is the only one. It is regular, Hausdorff, locally metrizable, and metrizable ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.metrizableSpace_MHat⟧. The text has metrizability under the properness condition; it holds with no hypothesis beyond $P$ being a finite-to-one covering functor. *Proposition 19.7.14* (p. 914, new in the 2021 edition; restated) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.proposition_19_7_14⟧: $p : M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi') \to M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is a surjective local homeomorphism. The tool is that projections of a fibred product along a local homeomorphism are local homeomorphisms ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · Chapter19.isLocalHomeomorph_fibredFst⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · Chapter19.isLocalHomeomorph_fibredSnd⟧. We read "local covering functor" as a finite-to-one covering functor (Definition 19.7.1). *Proposition 19.7.15* (p. 915, new in the 2021 edition; explicit) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.proposition_19_7_15_hyp⟧: for a finite-to-one covering functor $P$:
- (1) $p$ is a surjective local homeomorphism and (19.29) is a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.proposition_19_7_15_one⟧;
- (2) the top source map $\hat s$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalHomeomorph_srcHat⟧ and, under the compatibility, the top target map $\hat t$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalHomeomorph_tgtHat⟧ are local homeomorphisms;
- (3) the top unit is continuous ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.continuous_unitHat⟧ and, under the compatibility, the top inversion is a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.invHatHomeomorph⟧;
- (4) under the compatibility, the top multiplication is a local homeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalHomeomorph_mulHat⟧.

Also $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ and $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ are metrizable. The added hypothesis is the compatibility of Definition 19.7.11 (§XIX.24), with the witness of the absolute-value construction ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic_isCompatible⟧. (1), the source map and the unit need no hypothesis.

**Proof of the two point-set lemmas.** *Step 1, the first projection* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · Chapter19.isLocalHomeomorph_fibredFst⟧. Let $s : X \to Z$ be continuous, $\tau : Y \to Z$ a local homeomorphism, and $(x_0, y_0)$ a point with $s(x_0) = \tau(y_0)$. Take a partial homeomorphism $e$ of $\tau$ from an open $U \ni y_0$ onto an open $e(U)$. On the open set of pairs $(x, y)$ with $y \in U$, the first projection has the continuous inverse $x \mapsto (x, e^{-1}(s(x)))$ on the open set $s^{-1}(e(U))$, and the two maps are inverse to each other because $\tau = e$ on $U$. *Step 2, the second projection* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · Chapter19.isLocalHomeomorph_fibredSnd⟧: swap the factors, a homeomorphism of fibred products, and apply Step 1.

**Proof of Lemma 19.7.12** ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.lemma_19_7_12⟧. $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is metrizable for every basic construction, without the properness condition ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.metrizableSpace_M⟧ (Proposition 19.4.4). The object space of the ep-groupoid $E$ is an M-polyfold, hence metrizable. So the product is metrizable, and so is the subspace $M(\Psi, \Psi') \,{}_s\!\times_{\hat\tau} E$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.metrizableSpace_fib⟧. A metrizable space is regular, Hausdorff and locally metrizable, with the whole space as neighbourhood.

**Proof of Proposition 19.7.13.** *Step 1, the topology* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.topologicalSpaceMHat⟧: the topology on $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.MHat⟧ is the one induced by the map (19.26) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.toFib⟧, which is then continuous ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.continuous_toFib⟧. *Step 2, the homeomorphism* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.fibHomeomorph⟧. The inverse is $(h, e) \mapsto$ the lift of $h$ at $e$ (Lemma 19.7.10, §XIX.24). It is continuous into the induced topology because its composite with (19.26) is the identity. *Step 3, uniqueness.* A topology for which (19.26) is a homeomorphism makes (19.26) an inducing map, so it is the induced topology. *Step 4, the properties* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.metrizableSpace_MHat⟧: through the homeomorphism of Step 2, $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ is metrizable by Lemma 19.7.12, hence regular, Hausdorff and locally metrizable ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.proposition_19_7_13⟧.

**Proof of Proposition 19.7.14** ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.proposition_19_7_14⟧. $\hat\tau$ is a local homeomorphism, being a local sc-diffeomorphism on objects (Definition 17.8.4 (1)) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalHomeomorph_τ⟧. The source map of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is continuous (Proposition 19.3.5) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalHomeomorph_srcB⟧. So the first projection of $M(\Psi, \Psi') \,{}_s\!\times_{\hat\tau} E$ is a local homeomorphism (Step 1 of the point-set lemmas). $p$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.pB⟧ is this projection composed with the homeomorphism (19.26). Surjectivity is Lemma 19.7.9.

**Proof of Proposition 19.7.15.**
- *Step 1, (1)* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.proposition_19_7_15_one⟧: Propositions 19.7.14 and 19.7.13.
- *Step 2, (2), the source map* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalHomeomorph_srcHat⟧: $\hat s$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.srcHat⟧ is the second projection of the fibred product composed with (19.26). The second projection is a local homeomorphism because the source map of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is a local homeomorphism and $\hat\tau$ is continuous (Step 2 of the point-set lemmas).
- *Step 3, (2), the target map* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalHomeomorph_tgtHat⟧. The diagram (19.30) gives $\hat\tau' \circ \hat t = t \circ p$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.τ_tgtHat⟧ (and $\hat\tau \circ \hat s = s \circ p$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.τ_srcHat⟧). The right side is a local homeomorphism, since $t$ is one on $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalHomeomorph_tgtB⟧ and $p$ is one. If $g \circ f$ and $g$ are local homeomorphisms and $f$ is continuous, then $f$ is a local homeomorphism. Here $g = \hat\tau'$ and $f = \hat t$, continuous by the compatibility.
- *Step 4, (3), the unit* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.continuous_unitHat⟧. Into the induced topology it suffices that $p \circ \hat u$ and $\hat s \circ \hat u$ are continuous. The second is the identity of $E$. The first is $u \circ \hat\tau$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.transMap_unit⟧, with $u$ continuous (Proposition 19.3.5 (3), ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · TransitionData.continuous_unit⟧).
- *Step 5, (3), the inversion* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.continuous_invHat⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.invHatHomeomorph⟧. For $\hat\iota$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.invHat⟧, $p \circ \hat\iota = \iota \circ p$ is continuous, $\iota$ being a homeomorphism (Proposition 19.3.5 (4), ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · TransitionData.invHomeomorph⟧), and $\hat s \circ \hat\iota = \hat t$ is continuous by the compatibility. $\hat\iota$ is its own inverse ⟦Chapter17/TransitionSet · TransitionSet.inv_inv⟧, so it is a homeomorphism.
- *Step 6, (4)* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalHomeomorph_mulHat⟧. The top multiplication $\hat m$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.mulHat⟧ is defined on the pairs with $\hat s(\bar h') = \hat t(\bar h)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.FibProdHat⟧.
  - $\hat m$ is continuous ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.continuous_mulHat⟧: $p \circ \hat m = m \circ (p \times p)$ is continuous by Proposition 19.3.5 (5) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · TransitionData.continuous_mulMap⟧, and $\hat s \circ \hat m = \hat s \circ \mathrm{pr}_2$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.srcHat_mulHat⟧.
  - The fibred product is taken along the local homeomorphism $\hat s$ and the map $\hat t$, continuous by the compatibility. So $\mathrm{pr}_2$ is a local homeomorphism, and so is $\hat s \circ \hat m = \hat s \circ \mathrm{pr}_2$.
  - Since $\hat s$ is a local homeomorphism and $\hat m$ is continuous, $\hat m$ is a local homeomorphism.
- *Step 7, the collection* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.proposition_19_7_15_hyp⟧: Steps 1–6 with the metrizability of Proposition 19.7.13 and Lemma 19.7.12.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The properness condition of Propositions 19.7.13 and 19.7.15 is not needed for metrizability. In the formalization $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is metrizable without it, hence so are $M(\Psi, \Psi') \,{}_s\!\times_{\hat\tau} E$ and $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$. "Uniquely characterized" in Proposition 19.7.13 is read as: any topology making (19.26) a homeomorphism is the induced one. "Finite-to-one proper covering functor" (Proposition 19.7.13) and "local covering functor" (Proposition 19.7.14) are read as Definition 19.7.1. In the proof of Proposition 19.7.15 we read ${}_s\!\times_p E$ as ${}_s\!\times_{\hat\tau} E$. For the top target map the printed argument gives $\hat\tau' \circ \hat t = t \circ p$, a local homeomorphism. That does not give the continuity of $\hat t$, which may pass between the sheets of $\hat\tau'$. So Proposition 19.7.15 (2) for $\hat t$, the inversion in (3), and (4) are stated under the compatibility of Definition 19.7.11; §XIX.26 shows it is needed.

### XIX.26 Compatibility is needed: the twisted and doubled examples

**Statement.** *The twisted construction* (no counterpart in the text; extra) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · twistCU⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · twistBasic⟧: in the absolute-value example (§XIX.24), let $\sigma(x) = -1$ for $|x| > 1$ and $\sigma(x) = 1$ otherwise ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · twistSign⟧. Then $\hat\Psi(i, x) = (\sigma(x) i, x)$ over the same $\Psi$ and the same sheet model is a second proper covering uniformizer at $0$. With $\hat F(0) = \{\hat\Psi_{\mathrm{abs}}, \hat\Psi\}$ and the other members unchanged, this is a basic construction for $P$ in the sense of the literal Definition 19.7.11 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · twistHatU⟧. Every class has a uniformizer ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · twistBasic_hasClassUniformizers⟧. Moreover:
- it is not compatible ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · not_isCompatible_twistBasic⟧: its top target map is not continuous ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · not_continuous_tgtHat_twistBasic⟧;
- so Proposition 19.7.15 (2) does not hold for it ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · proposition_19_7_15_two_without_compatible⟧;
- Lemma 19.7.17 (1) still holds for it ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · lemma_19_7_17_one_twistBasic⟧, but (2) does not ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · lemma_19_7_17_two_without_compatible⟧.

*The doubled construction* (no counterpart in the text; extra) ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twoBasic⟧: the members of the absolute-value construction and, at $\beta = 2$, a second member over the slice $(0, \infty)$ twisted by $\sigma_2(x) = -1$ for $x > 2$ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twoCU⟧. For it, the collection of Definition 19.7.16 is not closed under intersections ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · lemma_19_7_17_one_without_compatible⟧, so no topology has these sets as its open sets ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twoBasic_no_topology⟧. These examples satisfy every printed hypothesis of Definition 19.7.11. They are the reason for the compatibility reading (§XIX.24).

**Proof for the twisted construction.**
- *Step 1, $\hat\Psi$ is a proper covering uniformizer* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · twistCU⟧. $\sigma$ is even ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · twistSign_smul⟧. Hence for a morphism $(g, (i, x))$ the target of its image $(g, (\sigma(x) i, x))$ is $(g\sigma(x) i, gx) = (\sigma(gx)\, g i, gx)$, which is $\hat\Psi(g(i, x))$; so $\hat\Psi$ is a functor. $\sigma^2 = 1$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · twistSign_mul_self⟧ gives injectivity, fullness and the cartesian property, the point over $(o, a)$ being $(\sigma(o) a_1, o)$. Faithfulness and the commutativity with $P$ are as for the untwisted member. Definition 19.7.3 asks for no continuity of $\hat\Psi$, and $\hat\Psi$ is not continuous at $|x| = 1$.
- *Step 2, the construction* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · twistBasic⟧. All underlying $\Psi$ are slice uniformizers ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · twistHatU_slice⟧, so the keyed transitions of §XIX.24 apply. The two members of $\hat F(0)$ have the same $\Psi$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · twistX_Ψ_eq⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · twistX⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · twistY⟧.
- *Step 3, $\hat t$ is not continuous* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · not_continuous_tgtHat_twistBasic⟧. Consider the path $t \mapsto ((1, t), 1, (\sigma(t), t))$ in $M(\hat\Psi_{\mathrm{abs}}, \hat\Psi)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · twistPath⟧. Its image under $p$ is the path of units over $t$, continuous through the homeomorphism of Proposition 19.3.7 ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_3_7⟧. Its source $(1, t)$ is continuous, so the path is continuous in the topology of Proposition 19.7.13. If $\hat t$ were continuous, the first component $\sigma(t)$ of $\hat t$ along the path would be a continuous map $\mathbb R \to \{\pm 1\}$, hence constant. But $\sigma(0) = 1$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · twistSign_zero⟧ and $\sigma(2) = -1$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · twistSign_two⟧.
- *Step 4, consequences* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · proposition_19_7_15_two_without_compatible⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · not_isCompatible_twistBasic⟧. A local homeomorphism is continuous, so $\hat t$ is not one, and the compatibility does not hold.
- *Step 5, Lemma 19.7.17 (1) holds* ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twistBasic_natOpenA_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · lemma_19_7_17_one_twistBasic⟧. Definition 19.7.16 uses a member of $\hat F(\beta)$ only for $\beta$ a representative of $|P|(\bar z)$. So the twisted member is used only at the points over $|0|$, where it agrees with the untwisted one on $\{|x| < 1\}$. Hence the collection of Definition 19.7.16 is that of the absolute-value construction ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · natOpenA_mono⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · absHatU_subset_twistHatU⟧. That collection is the orbit-space topology of $|\mathcal A|$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · natOpenA_abs_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · isOpen_absTopA_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twistTopA_eq⟧, metrizable ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · metrizableSpace_absTopA⟧ through the injective invariant $|(i, x)| \mapsto ix$ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · absInv⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · absInv_injective⟧, which identifies $|\mathcal A|$ with $\mathbb R$.
- *Step 6, Lemma 19.7.17 (2) does not hold* ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · lemma_19_7_17_two_without_compatible⟧. Take $V = \{1\} \times (1/2, 3/2)$ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twistV⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · isOpen_twistV⟧ in the object space of the twisted member. Its image $|\hat\Psi(V)|$ has invariants in $(1/2, 1] \cup (-3/2, -1)$ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · absInv_twist_image⟧: $x$ for $x \le 1$ and $-x$ for $x > 1$. It contains the point with invariant $1$ and no point with invariant slightly above $1$, so it is not open in the topology of Step 5.

**Proof for the doubled construction.**
- *Step 1, the member at $2$* ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twistSliceCU⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twoCU⟧: the twist of Step 1 above, for any slice and any $\sigma$ invariant under its group, applied to $(0, \infty)$ with trivial group and $\sigma_2$ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twistSignTwo⟧, with $\sigma_2(2) = 1$ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twistSignTwo_twoPt⟧. The construction keeps every class supplied ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twoBasic_hasClassUniformizers⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · absHatU_subset_twoHatU⟧.
- *Step 2, two open sets* ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · natOpenA_twoW₁⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · natOpenA_twoW₂⟧. Let $W_1$ consist of the points with invariant in $(3/2, 2] \cup (-5/2, -2)$ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twoW₁⟧, and $W_2$ of those with invariant in $(3/2, 5/2)$ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twoW₂⟧. $W_2$ is open through the untwisted members. $W_1$ is open: at the point $|(1, 2)|$ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twoOrbit⟧ use the twisted member with $V = \{1\} \times (3/2, 5/2)$ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twoV⟧, whose image has exactly these invariants; elsewhere use the untwisted members.
- *Step 3, the intersection* ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twoBasic_inter_not_natOpenA⟧. $W_1 \cap W_2$ has invariants in $(3/2, 2]$ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · absInv_mem_of_mem_inter⟧. At $|(1, 2)|$ the representative is $\beta = \pm 2$. Every open neighbourhood of a lift reaches invariants $2 + t$ for small $t > 0$ under an untwisted member, and $-(2 + t)$ under the twisted member ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · exists_pos_mem_line⟧. Neither lies in $W_1 \cap W_2$.
- *Step 4* ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · lemma_19_7_17_one_without_compatible⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twoBasic_no_topology⟧. Under the compatibility the collection would be closed under intersections (§XIX.27), so the doubled construction is not compatible. No topology has these sets as its open sets, since a topology contains $W_1 \cap W_2$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* Not in the book. The twisted example shows that two members over the same $\Psi$ and the same model may differ by a permutation of the sheets of $\hat\tau$ that is not locally constant. The doubled example shows that the same happens at a point with trivial isotropy. At a point invariant under $\mathbb Z/2$ the twist has to be even, and there it does not affect Lemma 19.7.17 (1). Together they show that Proposition 19.7.15 (2) and both parts of Lemma 19.7.17 use the compatibility reading of Definition 19.7.11.

### XIX.27 Definition 19.7.16 and Lemma 19.7.17, p. 916

**Statement.** *Definition 19.7.16* (p. 916, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.NatOpenA⟧: a subset $W$ of $|\mathcal A|$ belongs to $T_{|\mathcal A|}$ if every $\bar z \in W$ has a representative $\beta$ of $|P|(\bar z)$, a member $\hat\Psi : E \to \mathcal A$ of $\hat F(\beta)$, a point $e \in E$ with $|\hat\Psi(e)| = \bar z$, and an open $V \ni e$ of $E$ with $|\hat\Psi(V)| \subset W$. *Lemma 19.7.17* (p. 916, new in the 2021 edition; explicit) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.lemma_19_7_17_one_hyp⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.lemma_19_7_17_two_hyp⟧: let $P$ be a finite-to-one covering functor. Assume the compatibility of Definition 19.7.11 (§XIX.24), with the witness of the absolute-value construction ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic_isCompatible⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic_natTopA_eq⟧. Assume also the standing reading that every class of $\mathcal B$ has a representative with $\hat F(\beta) \ne \emptyset$, with the same witness ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic_hasClassUniformizers⟧. Then:
- (1) $T_{|\mathcal A|}$ contains $\emptyset$ and $|\mathcal A|$ and is closed under finite intersections and arbitrary unions; this is the topology ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.natTopA⟧. Every point lies in the image of a uniformizer whose orbit map is an open embedding.
- (2) For $\hat\Psi \in \hat F(\beta)$ and $V \subset E$ open, $|\hat\Psi(V)|$ is open ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.isOpen_image_hat⟧. The map $e \mapsto |\hat\Psi(e)|$ is continuous ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.continuous_π_hat⟧, and $|\hat\Psi| : |E| \to |\mathcal A|$ is a homeomorphism onto an open subset.

The key step is the neighbourhood lemma ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.exists_nbhd_hat⟧. The examples of §XIX.26 show that the compatibility is needed.

**Proof of the lifting facts.** *Step 1, points over $|\Psi(o)|$* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.exists_hat_of_orbitMap_eq⟧. Let $|P|(\bar z) = |\Psi(o)|$ and $\bar z = |\alpha|$. A morphism $\varphi : P(\alpha) \to \Psi(o)$ has a lift $\hat\varphi$ at $\alpha$ (Definition 19.7.1). Its target lies over $\Psi(o)$, so the cartesian property gives $e$ with $\hat\tau(e) = o$ and $\hat\Psi(e) = t(\hat\varphi)$. Then $|\hat\Psi(e)| = \bar z$. In particular, for $\hat\Psi \in \hat F(\beta)$ and $|\beta| = |P|(\bar z)$, $\bar z$ is $|\hat\Psi(e)|$ for some $e$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.exists_hat_of_orbitMap_eq_pt⟧, by Step 1 at $o = \bar o$. We also use $|P|(|\hat\Psi(e)|) = |\Psi(\hat\tau(e))|$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.orbitMap_π_hat⟧. *Step 2, elements from isomorphisms* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.exists_trans_of_π_eq⟧: if $|\hat\Psi(e)| = |\hat\Psi'(e')|$, a morphism $\hat\Psi(e) \to \hat\Psi'(e')$ gives an element of $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ from $e$ to $e'$.

**Proof of the neighbourhood lemma** ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.exists_nbhd_hat⟧. Let $\hat h \in M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ and $V' \ni t(\hat h)$ open. Put $N = \hat s(\hat t^{-1}(V'))$. By the compatibility $\hat t^{-1}(V')$ is open, and $\hat s$ is a local homeomorphism, hence open (Proposition 19.7.15 (2), §XIX.25). So $N$ is an open neighbourhood of $s(\hat h)$. For $d = \hat s(\bar b) \in N$, the element $\bar b$ is a morphism $\hat\Psi(d) \to \hat\Psi'(\hat t(\bar b))$ with $\hat t(\bar b) \in V'$, so $|\hat\Psi(N)| \subset |\hat\Psi'(V')|$.

**Proof of Lemma 19.7.17 (1).**
- *Step 1, empty set and unions* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.natOpenA_empty⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.natOpenA_sUnion⟧: the empty set has no points to check, and a representation for $\bar z \in W \subset \bigcup S$ serves for the union.
- *Step 2, the whole space* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.natOpenA_univ⟧: for $\bar z$, the class hypothesis gives $\beta$ with $|\beta| = |P|(\bar z)$ and $\hat\Psi \in \hat F(\beta)$. The lifting facts give $e$ with $|\hat\Psi(e)| = \bar z$; take $V = E$.
- *Step 3, intersections* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.natOpenA_inter⟧. Let $\bar z \in W_1 \cap W_2$ be represented through $(\hat\Psi_1, e_1, V_1)$ and $(\hat\Psi_2, e_2, V_2)$. Since $|\hat\Psi_1(e_1)| = \bar z = |\hat\Psi_2(e_2)|$, Step 2 of the lifting facts gives $\hat h$ from $e_1$ to $e_2$. The neighbourhood lemma gives $N \ni e_1$ with $|\hat\Psi_1(N)| \subset |\hat\Psi_2(V_2)| \subset W_2$. Then $(\hat\Psi_1, e_1, N \cap V_1)$ represents $\bar z$ in $W_1 \cap W_2$.
- *Step 4, the open embeddings* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.lemma_19_7_17_one_hyp⟧: for $\bar z$, Step 2 gives a member $\hat\Psi$ and $e$ with $\bar z = |\hat\Psi(e)|$, and (2) makes $|\hat\Psi|$ an open embedding.

Local metrizability is read through Step 4: every point has the open neighbourhood $|\hat\Psi(E)| \cong |E|$, and $|E|$ is locally metrizable by Theorem 7.3.1 ⟦Chapter7/OrbitTopology · theorem_7_3_1⟧.

**Proof of Lemma 19.7.17 (2).** *Step 1, openness* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.isOpen_image_hat⟧. Let $d \in V$. The class hypothesis gives $\beta'$ with $|\beta'| = |P|(|\hat\Psi(d)|)$ and $\hat\Psi' \in \hat F(\beta')$. The lifting facts give $e'$ with $|\hat\Psi'(e')| = |\hat\Psi(d)|$ and an element from $e'$ to $d$. The neighbourhood lemma gives $N \ni e'$ with $|\hat\Psi'(N)| \subset |\hat\Psi(V)|$, which is the required representation at $|\hat\Psi(d)|$ through the member $\hat\Psi'$. *Step 2, continuity* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.continuous_π_hat⟧. Let $W$ be open and $|\hat\Psi(d)| \in W$, represented through $(\hat\Psi', e', V')$. An element from $d$ to $e'$ and the neighbourhood lemma give $N \ni d$ with $|\hat\Psi(N)| \subset |\hat\Psi'(V')| \subset W$. *Step 3, injectivity* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.injective_orbitMap_hat⟧: if $|\hat\Psi(e)| = |\hat\Psi(e')|$, a morphism $\hat\Psi(e) \to \hat\Psi(e')$ is the image of a morphism $e \to e'$ of $E$, since $\hat\Psi$ is full. *Step 4, open embedding* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.lemma_19_7_17_two_hyp⟧. $|\hat\Psi|$ is continuous by Step 2 through the quotient $|E|$, and injective by Step 3. It is open: the image of an open $W \subset |E|$ is $|\hat\Psi(\pi^{-1}(W))|$, open by Step 1, as $\pi : E \to |E|$ is continuous. A continuous, injective, open map is an open embedding.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* Definition 19.7.16 is formalized literally, with "$\bar z \in |\hat\Psi(V)|$" read as a point $e \in V$ with $|\hat\Psi(e)| = \bar z$. In the proof of closure under intersections, Lemma 19.7.17 (1) uses that the top target maps $\hat t$ are continuous, through Proposition 19.7.15 (2); we assume the compatibility of Definition 19.7.11. "$\emptyset$ and $|\mathcal A|$ belong to $T$" needs that every class of $\mathcal B$ has a representative with $\hat F(\beta) \ne \emptyset$, as in Theorem 19.2.2. "Locally metrizable" is read through (2). Both hypotheses hold in the absolute-value construction, where $T_{|\mathcal A|}$ is the orbit-space topology of $|\mathcal A| \cong \mathbb R$. The twisted and doubled examples (§XIX.26) show that (2) and (1) do not hold as we read them without the compatibility.

### XIX.28 Proposition 19.7.18 (2017: Lemma 19.3), p. 919

**Statement.** *Proposition 19.7.18* (p. 919, narrower) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.proposition_19_7_18_hyp⟧: let $P$ be a finite-to-one covering functor and $(\hat F, \mathcal F)$ a basic construction for $P$. Assume:
- the compatibility of Definition 19.7.11 (§XIX.24), every top target map $\hat t$ continuous, with the witness of the absolute-value construction ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic_isCompatible⟧;
- the standing reading that every class of $\mathcal B$ has a representative $\beta$ with $\hat F(\beta) \ne \emptyset$.

Then $|P| : (|\mathcal A|, T_{|\mathcal A|}) \to (|\mathcal B|, T_{|\mathcal B|})$ is continuous ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.continuous_orbitMap⟧, open ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.isOpenMap_orbitMap⟧ and surjective ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.surjective_orbitMap⟧, with finite fibres ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.finite_fiber_orbitMap⟧. For every $\hat\Psi \in \hat F(\beta)$, $P^{-1}(\Psi(O_\beta)) = \hat\Psi(E)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.preimage_range_obj⟧ and (19.34) $|P|^{-1}(|\Psi(O_\beta)|) = |\hat\Psi(E)|$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.preimage_range_orbitMap⟧. The printed conclusion that $|P|$ is a local homeomorphism does not hold as we read it ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · not_proposition_19_7_18⟧. In the absolute-value construction every hypothesis holds, the compatibility included, and $|P|$ is the absolute value $\mathbb R \to [0, \infty)$, which is not a local homeomorphism at $0$:
- with the natural topology of Lemma 19.7.17 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · not_proposition_19_7_18_natTopA⟧;
- and for every topology on $|\mathcal A|$ making $e \mapsto |\hat\Psi(e)|$ continuous for the member of $\hat F(0)$, with any topology on $|\mathcal B|$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · not_proposition_19_7_18_of_continuous⟧;
- the same at the level of the ep-groupoid functor ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absCovering_orbitMap_not_isLocalHomeomorph⟧.

The local homeomorphism property holds wherever $|\hat\tau|$ is a local homeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalHomeomorphOn_orbitMap_of_τ_hyp⟧, through the square (19.35) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.orbitMap_hat_comm⟧. *The isotropy criterion* (no counterpart in the text) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.isLocalHomeomorphOn_orbitMap_of_isotropy_surjective⟧: for an ep-functor $F : K \ltimes U \to L \ltimes V$ of translation groupoids which is a local homeomorphism on objects, $|F|$ is a local homeomorphism at $|a|$ whenever $F$ maps $\mathrm{Aut}(a)$ onto $\mathrm{Aut}(F(a))$. In the absolute-value example this is exact ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absP_isLocalHomeomorphOn_iff⟧, but it is not necessary in general ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.isotropy_surjective_not_necessary⟧. The 2017 form, continuous and open, is proved.

**Proof of the proved form.**
- *Step 1, $P^{-1}(\Psi(O_\beta)) = \hat\Psi(E)$* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.preimage_range_obj⟧: if $P(a) = \Psi(o)$, the cartesian property (Definition 19.7.4) gives $e$ with $\hat\Psi(e) = a$; conversely $P(\hat\Psi(e)) = \Psi(\hat\tau(e))$.
