- *Step 2, (19.34)* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.preimage_range_orbitMap⟧: if $|P|(\bar z) = |\Psi(o)|$, then $\bar z = |\hat\Psi(e)|$ for some $e$ over $o$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.exists_hat_of_orbitMap_eq⟧ (§XIX.27), and conversely $|P|(|\hat\Psi(e)|) = |\Psi(\hat\tau(e))|$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.orbitMap_π_hat⟧.
- *Step 3, surjective with finite fibres.* $P$ is surjective on objects, so $|P|$ is surjective ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.surjective_orbitMap⟧. If $|P(\alpha)| = |\beta|$, the lift of a morphism $P(\alpha) \to \beta$ at $\alpha$ moves $\alpha$ into $P^{-1}(\beta)$. So $|P|^{-1}(|\beta|)$ lies in the image of the finite set $P^{-1}(\beta)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.finite_fiber_orbitMap⟧.
- *Step 4, continuity* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.continuous_orbitMap⟧. Let $W$ be open in $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ and $|P|(\bar z) \in W$. Choose $\beta$, $\hat\Psi \in \hat F(\beta)$ and $e$ with $|\hat\Psi(e)| = \bar z$. The map $d \mapsto |\Psi(\hat\tau(d))|$ is continuous, by Theorem 19.2.2 for $\Psi$ ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.continuous_π_obj⟧ and the continuity of $\hat\tau$. So the preimage of $W$ under it is an open $V \ni e$, and $|\hat\Psi(V)| \subset |P|^{-1}(W)$ by Step 2. This is Definition 19.7.16 for $|P|^{-1}(W)$.
- *Step 5, openness* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.isOpenMap_orbitMap⟧. If $W$ is open and $\bar z \in W$ is represented by $(\hat\Psi, e, V)$, then $|P|(W)$ contains $|\Psi(\hat\tau(V))|$. This set is open: $\hat\tau$ is a local homeomorphism, hence open, and Theorem 19.2.2 (2) applies ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_2_2_two⟧. It contains $|P|(\bar z)$.
- *Step 6, where $|\hat\tau|$ is a local homeomorphism* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalHomeomorphOn_orbitMap_of_τ_hyp⟧. The square (19.35) $|P| \circ |\hat\Psi| = |\Psi| \circ |\hat\tau|$ commutes ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.orbitMap_hat_comm⟧. Near a point where $|\hat\tau|$ is injective on an open $N'$, the set $|\hat\Psi|(N')$ is open (Lemma 19.7.17 (2), ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.lemma_19_7_17_two_hyp⟧). $|P|$ is injective there, because $|\Psi|$ is injective (Theorem 19.2.2 (3), ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_2_2_three⟧) and $|\hat\tau|$ is injective on $N'$. A continuous, injective, open map on an open set is an open embedding there.

**Proof that the local homeomorphism property does not hold as we read it.**
- *Step 1, the example* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absP⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absP_isProperCovering⟧. At the level of ep-groupoids, $\mathcal A = \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes (\{\pm 1\} \times \mathbb R)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absA⟧ and $\mathcal B = \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$, the reflection ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absB⟧. $P(i, x) = x$ is the sheet projection ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.sheetProj⟧. It is a proper covering functor in the sense of Definition 8.5.1 ⟦Chapter8/Basic · EpFunctor.IsProperCovering⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.sheetProj_isProperCovering⟧, the strongest covering notion of Parts I–III.
- *Step 2, the orbit spaces* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absAOrbitHomeomorph⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absBOrbitHomeomorph⟧. $|(i, x)| \mapsto ix$ is a homeomorphism $|\mathcal A| \cong \mathbb R$, and $|x| \mapsto |x|$ a homeomorphism $|\mathcal B| \cong [0, \infty)$. Under them $|P|$ is $t \mapsto |t|$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absBOrbitHomeomorph_orbitMap⟧. $|P|$ is continuous, open and surjective with finite fibres ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absP_orbitMap_continuous⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absP_orbitMap_isOpenMap⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absP_orbitMap_surjective⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absP_orbitMap_finite_fiber⟧.
- *Step 3, no local injectivity at $0$* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absCovering_orbitMap_not_isLocalHomeomorphOn_zero⟧. For $t \ne 0$, $|(i, t)| \ne |(i, -t)|$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absA_π_ne⟧, since the only group element moving $i$ to $i$ is $1$. But $|P|$ identifies them ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absP_orbitMap_neg⟧. Every neighbourhood of $|(i, 0)|$ contains such a pair for small $t$, so $|P|$ is not injective near $|(i, 0)|$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absCovering_orbitMap_not_isLocalHomeomorph⟧.
- *Step 4, as a basic construction for $P$* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · not_proposition_19_7_18⟧. The construction of §XIX.24 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic⟧ satisfies Definition 19.7.11, the class hypothesis and the compatibility. Its collection $T_{|\mathcal A|}$ is the orbit-space topology ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · isOpen_absTopA_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic_natTopA_eq⟧, and $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ is that of $|\mathcal B|$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absNatTopB_eq⟧. The functor is the one of Step 1 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absCovP_orbitMap_eq⟧. For any topology on $|\mathcal A|$ in which $t \mapsto |(1, t)|$ is continuous, $|P|$ is not injective on any open neighbourhood of $|(1, 0)|$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · not_injOn_of_continuous⟧, by Step 3 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · not_proposition_19_7_18_natTopA⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · not_proposition_19_7_18_of_continuous⟧. Lemma 19.7.17 (2) makes $t \mapsto |(1, t)|$ continuous.

**Proof of the isotropy criterion** ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.isLocalHomeomorphOn_orbitMap_of_isotropy_surjective⟧.
- *Step 1, a slice at $F(a)$* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.exists_slice⟧: for a finite group acting continuously on a Hausdorff space there is an open $S \ni b$ such that $y, ly \in S$ implies $lb = b$.
- *Step 2, a small neighbourhood $W$.* Take $W$ open around $a$ inside the source of a local homeomorphism chart of $F$, with $F(W) \subset S$, on which the group component of $F(g, w)$ equals that of $F(g, a)$ for every $g$ (finitely many open conditions). Replace $W$ by its intersection with its translates under $K_a$.
- *Step 3, injectivity on $\pi(W)$.* Suppose $|F|(|w|) = |F|(|w'|)$ for $w, w' \in W$. Then $l F(w) = F(w')$ for some $l$. By the slice $l F(a) = F(a)$, so $l \in \mathrm{Aut}(F(a))$. By hypothesis $l$ is the image of some $g \in \mathrm{Aut}(a)$. By Step 2, $F(g w) = l F(w) = F(w')$, and $F$ is injective on $W$, so $gw = w'$ and $|w| = |w'|$.
- *Step 4.* $|F|$ is continuous ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.orbitMap_continuous⟧ and open ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.orbitMap_isOpenMap⟧, so it is an open embedding on the open set $\pi(W)$.
- *Step 5, in the example* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absP_isotropy_surjective_iff⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.absP_isLocalHomeomorphOn_iff⟧. $\mathrm{Aut}(i, x)$ is trivial. $\mathrm{Aut}(x)$ is trivial for $x \ne 0$ and contains $-1$ for $x = 0$. So the criterion holds exactly at $x \ne 0$, which by Step 3 of the previous proof is exactly where $|P|$ is a local homeomorphism.
- *Step 6, not necessary* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.isotropy_surjective_not_necessary⟧. For $\mathbb Z/3$ acting trivially on $\mathbb R$, the sheet projection $\mathbb Z/3 \ltimes (\mathbb Z/3 \times \mathbb R) \to \mathbb Z/3 \ltimes \mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.trivialSheetProj⟧ is a proper covering functor. $|P|$ is injective ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitCounterexample · Chapter19.trivialSheetProj_orbitMap_injective⟧, hence a homeomorphism. But $\mathrm{Aut}(a) = 1$ does not map onto $\mathrm{Aut}(P(a)) = \mathbb Z/3$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The proof infers from "$|\hat\tau|$ is a surjective local homeomorphism" that $|P|$ is one. The map $|\hat\tau| : |E| \to |G \ltimes O|$ is continuous, open and surjective with finite fibres. It is a local homeomorphism only where the isotropy of $E$ maps onto that of $G \ltimes O$. At the point $0$ of the absolute-value example, $\mathrm{Aut}(1, 0) = 1$ and $\mathrm{Aut}(0) = \mathbb Z/2$. The formalization proves the properties the argument gives, the 2017 form with finite fibres and (19.34), and the local homeomorphism property where the isotropy map is onto.

### XIX.29 Lemma 19.7.19, the divisibility remark after it, Corollary 19.7.20 (2017: Lemma 19.4), pp. 920–922

**Statement.** *Lemma 19.7.19* (p. 920, new in the 2021 edition; restated) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · ProperCoveringModel.lemma_19_7_19⟧: let $\hat\tau : E \to G \ltimes O$ be a connected proper covering model (Definition 17.8.4 with $O$ connected ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringModel · ProperCoveringModel.Connected⟧). For $y, y' \in \hat\tau^{-1}(\bar o)$, there is a morphism $y \to y'$ iff $|U(y)| = |U(y')|$ in $|E|$. We read the connected local ep-groupoid covering model as a connected proper covering model, as in Remark 17.8.11 (2). *The divisibility remark* (p. 920, new in the 2021 edition; narrower) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · not_remark_19_7_19_divides⟧: the remark that the number $k$ of classes divides $\ell = \#\hat\tau^{-1}(\bar o)$ does not hold as we read it. For a connected sheet model with three sheets ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · divModel⟧, $\ell = 3$ and $k = 2$. Each class has $|G : \mathrm{im}\, G_y|$ elements, so the counts need not divide one another. The remark is not used later. *Corollary 19.7.20* (p. 921, narrower) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · CoveringBasicConstruction.corollary_19_7_20_hyp⟧: let $P$ be a finite-to-one covering functor, and assume the compatibility of Definition 19.7.11 with its witness ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic_isCompatible⟧ and the class hypothesis. Let $z = |\beta|$, $\hat\Psi \in \hat F(\beta)$, $U(z)$ an open neighbourhood of $z$ and $\hat U$ an open neighbourhood of $|P|^{-1}(z)$. There is a connected $G$-invariant open neighbourhood $O'$ of $\bar o$ such that:
- the restriction to $\hat\tau^{-1}(O')$ is a connected proper covering model;
- $\hat\tau^{-1}(O')$ is the disjoint union of the connected open sets $V(y) = U(y) \cap \hat\tau^{-1}(O') \ni y$;
- (1) $W(z) = |\Psi(O')|$ is an open neighbourhood of $z$ in $U(z)$;
- (2) there are mutually inequivalent $a_1, \dots, a_k \in \hat\tau^{-1}(\bar o)$ whose classes exhaust $\hat\tau^{-1}(\bar o)$, each $V(a_i)$ is $G_{a_i}$-invariant, and the sets $W(\bar z_i) = |\hat\Psi(V(a_i))|$ are mutually disjoint open neighbourhoods of $\bar z_i = |\hat\Psi(a_i)|$ with (19.38) $|P|^{-1}(W(z)) = \bigsqcup W(\bar z_i) \subset \hat U$, and $a_i \mapsto \bar z_i$ is a bijection onto $|P|^{-1}(z)$.

Consequences: every open neighbourhood of $|P|^{-1}(z)$ contains some $|P|^{-1}(W)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · CoveringBasicConstruction.exists_isOpen_preimage_subset⟧, $|P|$ is closed ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · CoveringBasicConstruction.isClosedMap_orbitMap⟧, and the fibres are evenly covered ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · CoveringBasicConstruction.evenlyCovered_orbitMap⟧. Part (3), that $|P| : W(\bar z_i) \to W(z)$ is a homeomorphism, does not hold as we read it ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · not_corollary_19_7_20_three⟧. Its proof identifies $|G_{a_i} \ltimes V(a_i)|$ with $|G \ltimes O'|$, which needs $G_{a_i} = G$.

**Proof of Lemma 19.7.19.**
- *Step 1, the key step* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · ProperCoveringModel.sheetIndex_tgt_liftMor_σ⟧. Let $S \ni \bar o$ be preconnected, $y \in \hat\tau^{-1}(\bar o)$ and $g \in G$. For $o \in S$, take the morphism with source $\sigma_y(o)$ and group element $g$ ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringModel · ProperCoveringModel.liftMor⟧, and look at the sheet of its target. This sheet does not depend on $o$:
  - the morphism depends continuously on $o$ ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringModel · ProperCoveringModel.continuous_liftMor_σ⟧;
  - the sheet index of the target is locally constant, Lemma 17.8.7 ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringModel · ProperCoveringModel.lemma_17_8_7⟧;
  - a locally constant function on a preconnected set is constant.

  Its value at $\bar o$ is the sheet of $t(\hat g)$ for $\hat g$ the morphism from $y$ with group element $g$, which ends in $\hat\tau^{-1}(\bar o)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · ProperCoveringModel.tgt_liftMor_mem_fiber⟧.
- *Step 2, morphisms move sheets* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · ProperCoveringModel.liftMor_mem_cutSheet⟧. Write $V(y) = U(y) \cap \hat\tau^{-1}(S)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · ProperCoveringModel.cutSheet⟧. If $S$ is moreover $G$-invariant and $\varphi : y \to y'$, then the morphism with source $e \in V(y)$ and the group element of $\varphi$ ends in $V(y')$, by Step 1. Hence $|V(y)| \subset |V(y')|$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · ProperCoveringModel.image_π_cutSheet_subset⟧, and equality for $y \sim y'$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · ProperCoveringModel.image_π_cutSheet_eq⟧.
- *Step 3, the converse* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · ProperCoveringModel.liftMor_of_mem_cutSheet⟧. A morphism $\psi$ from $V(y)$ to $V(y')$ with group element $g$ gives, by Step 1, the morphism from $y$ with group element $g$, ending in the sheet of $y'$ and over $\bar o$, hence at $y'$. So if $|V(y)|$ and $|V(y')|$ meet, $y \sim y'$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · ProperCoveringModel.isomorphic_of_mem_cutSheet⟧.
- *Step 4, the lemma* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · ProperCoveringModel.lemma_19_7_19⟧: with $S = O$ connected and invariant, $V(y) = U(y)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · ProperCoveringModel.cutSheet_univ⟧, and Steps 2 and 3 give both directions.

**Proof that the divisibility does not hold as we read it** ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · not_remark_19_7_19_divides⟧. Take the sheet model of Definition 17.8.4 ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringModel · sheetModel⟧ over $\mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$ with three sheets $\{\pm 1\} \sqcup \{\ast\}$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · divS⟧: $\mathbb Z/2$ swaps $\pm 1$, leaves $\ast$ in place, and acts on $\mathbb R$ by reflection ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · divModel⟧. It is connected, and $\hat\tau^{-1}(0)$ has three points ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · divModel_fiber⟧. The two points on the swapped sheets are isomorphic ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · divModel_π_inl⟧, and not isomorphic to the third ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · divModel_π_ne⟧, since no group element maps a swapped sheet to $\ast$. So $k = 2$, $\ell = 3$, and $2 \nmid 3$.

**Proof of Corollary 19.7.20 (1), (2).**
- *Step 1, shrinking* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · CoveringBasicConstruction.exists_open_pt_subset⟧. Put $O_0 = \bigcap_{a} \hat\tau(U(a) \cap |\hat\Psi|^{-1}(\hat U)) \cap |\Psi|^{-1}(U(z))$, $a \in \hat\tau^{-1}(\bar o)$. It is a finite intersection: $\hat\tau^{-1}(\bar o)$ is finite (§XIX.24). Each term is open, since $\hat\tau$ is open and injective on $U(a)$, and $e \mapsto |\hat\Psi(e)|$ and $o \mapsto |\Psi(o)|$ are continuous. Points $e$ over $O_0$ have $|\hat\Psi(e)| \in \hat U$: such an $e$ lies in some $U(a)$, where $\hat\tau$ is injective.
- *Step 2, $O'$.* Proposition 17.8.6 ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringModelRestrict · proposition_17_8_6⟧ gives a connected $G$-invariant open $O' \subset O_0$ containing $\bar o$ whose restricted model ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringModelRestrict · ProperCoveringModel.restrictFunctor⟧ is a connected proper covering model. The sets $V(y)$ are open ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · ProperCoveringModel.isOpen_cutSheet⟧, connected as homeomorphic images of $O'$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · ProperCoveringModel.cutSheet_eq_image⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · ProperCoveringModel.isConnected_cutSheet⟧, contain $y$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · ProperCoveringModel.mem_cutSheet_self⟧, are disjoint ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · ProperCoveringModel.pairwiseDisjoint_cutSheet⟧, and cover $\hat\tau^{-1}(O')$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · ProperCoveringModel.iUnion_cutSheet⟧.
- *Step 3, (1).* $W(z) = |\Psi(O')|$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · CoveringUniformizer.imageB⟧ is open by Theorem 19.2.2 (2), contains $z = |\Psi(\bar o)|$, and lies in $U(z)$ by Step 1.
- *Step 4, the representatives* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · GroupoidStr.exists_reprs⟧: every set of objects contains a set $S$ of mutually inequivalent objects meeting the class of each member. Apply this to $\hat\tau^{-1}(\bar o)$. $G_{a}$-invariance of $V(a)$ is Step 2 of the previous proof with $\varphi : a \to a$.
- *Step 5, the sets $W(\bar z_a)$.* $W(\bar z_a) = |\hat\Psi(V(a))|$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · CoveringUniformizer.imageA⟧ is open by Lemma 19.7.17 (2) and contains $|\hat\Psi(a)|$. Two of them meet only if $a \sim a'$, by Step 3 of the previous proof and $|\hat\Psi(e)| = |\hat\Psi(e')| \iff |e| = |e'|$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · CoveringUniformizer.π_hat_eq_iff⟧. So they are disjoint for $a \ne a'$ in $S$.
- *Step 6, (19.38).* If $|P|(\bar z) \in |\Psi(O')|$, then $\bar z = |\hat\Psi(e)|$ with $\hat\tau(e) \in O'$ (§XIX.27). The point $e$ lies in the $V(y)$ of its sheet, $y \sim a$ for some $a \in S$, and $|V(y)| = |V(a)|$; so $\bar z \in W(\bar z_a)$. The converse is $|P|(|\hat\Psi(e)|) = |\Psi(\hat\tau(e))|$. The union lies in $\hat U$ by Step 1. Finally $a \mapsto \bar z_a$ maps $S$ into $|P|^{-1}(z)$, is injective by inequivalence, and is onto by the same argument at $o = \bar o$.
- *Step 7, consequences.* With $U(z) = |\mathcal B|$, (19.38) gives the tube property ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · CoveringBasicConstruction.exists_isOpen_preimage_subset⟧. For closedness ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · CoveringBasicConstruction.isClosedMap_orbitMap⟧: if $z \notin |P|(C)$ with $C$ closed, apply the tube property to $\hat U$, the complement of $C$. With also $\hat U = |\mathcal A|$, (2) gives the even covering ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalStructure · CoveringBasicConstruction.evenlyCovered_orbitMap⟧.

**Proof that (3) does not hold as we read it** ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · not_corollary_19_7_20_three⟧. In the absolute-value construction, take $z = |0|$, the member of $\hat F(0)$, any $a \in \hat\tau^{-1}(\bar o)$ and any open $V \ni a$. The image $|\hat\Psi(V)|$ is the image of an open neighbourhood of $(a_1, 0)$ in $\{\pm 1\} \times \mathbb R$. It contains $|(a_1, t)|$ and $|(a_1, -t)|$ for small $t \ne 0$, which are distinct and have the same image under $|P|$ (§XIX.28). So $|P|$ is not injective on $W(\bar z_1)$ for any choice in the corollary.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* In Lemma 19.7.19, $|U(y)|$ is the image of $U(y)$ in $|E|$. The text's path inside the set of morphisms with group element $g$ is replaced by the connected family $o \mapsto$ (the morphism with source $\sigma_y(o)$ and group element $g$), as in Lemma 17.8.8, on which the sheet index is constant. In Corollary 19.7.20, "$V(a_i)$ invariant under $G_{a_i}$" is read as: for every $\varphi : a_i \to a_i$ and $e \in V(a_i)$, the morphism from $e$ with the group element of $\varphi$ ends in $V(a_i)$. "Mutually not equivalent" means that there is no morphism $a_i \to a_j$ for $a_i \ne a_j$. The neighbourhoods $U(z)$, $\hat U$ are open in $T_{|\mathcal B|}$, $T_{|\mathcal A|}$. The text's proof needs only $O'$ small enough; we take it inside the set of Step 1. The divisibility "$k$ divides $\ell$" holds when all classes have the same size.

### XIX.30 Lemma 19.7.21, Remark 19.7.22, Propositions 19.7.23, 19.7.24, Theorem 19.7.25 (2017: Propositions 19.9, 19.10), pp. 922–926

**Statement.** *Lemma 19.7.21* (p. 922, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.lemma_19_7_21⟧: for a finite-to-one covering functor $P$, under the class hypothesis:
- (1) if $(F, \mathcal F)$ satisfies the properness condition (V2) ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.PropernessV2⟧, then $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ is regular, Hausdorff and locally metrizable;
- (2) every $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ and $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ is metrizable, with no hypothesis;
- (3) under (V2), if $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ is paracompact it is metrizable.

*Remark 19.7.22* (p. 923, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.remark_19_7_22⟧: $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ is metrizable iff (V2) holds and $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ is paracompact, and then the transition sets are metrizable. *Proposition 19.7.23* (p. 923, explicit) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.proposition_19_7_23_hyp⟧: under the compatibility of Definition 19.7.11 with its witness ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic_isCompatible⟧ and the class hypothesis, $T_{|\mathcal A|}$ satisfies the properness condition ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.PropernessV2A⟧ iff $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ does. *Proposition 19.7.24* (p. 924, explicit) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.proposition_19_7_24_hyp⟧: under the same hypotheses, $T_{|\mathcal A|}$ is paracompact iff $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ is. The proofs use that $|P|$ is a perfect map ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · Chapter19.paracompactSpace_of_tube⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · Chapter19.paracompactSpace_of_tube_of_isOpenMap⟧, and do not use Corollary 19.7.20 (3). *Theorem 19.7.25* (p. 925, new in the 2021 edition; narrower): under the same hypotheses and the printed one that $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ is metrizable:
- (1) $T_{|\mathcal A|}$ is metrizable and $|P|$ is a continuous surjection ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.theorem_19_7_25_one_hyp⟧;
- (2) $\mathcal A$ and $\mathcal B$ with their natural topologies are groupoidal categories with topology ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.toGCTA⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.toGCTB⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.theorem_19_7_25_two_hyp⟧;
- (3) $P$ is a proper covering functor of such categories, Definition 17.8.1 ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringStructure · GroupoidFunctor.IsProperCoveringGCT⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.theorem_19_7_25_three_hyp⟧;
- (4a) every fibre $|P|^{-1}(z)$ is finite, and there are an open $U(z)$ and mutually disjoint open $U(\bar z_i)$ with $|P|^{-1}(U(z)) = \bigsqcup U(\bar z_i)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.theorem_19_7_25_four_a_hyp⟧.

(4b), that $|P| : U(\bar z_i) \to U(z)$ is a homeomorphism, does not hold as we read it ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · not_theorem_19_7_25_four_b⟧; this is the point of Proposition 19.7.18. The hypotheses of the theorem hold for the absolute-value construction ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · theorem_19_7_25_absBasic⟧.

**Proof of Lemma 19.7.21 and Remark 19.7.22.** *Step 1, (1)* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.lemma_19_7_21⟧: under (V2), Proposition 19.4.5 ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_4_5⟧ gives Hausdorff and completely regular, hence regular, and Theorem 19.2.2 (5) ⟦Chapter19/NaturalTopology · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_2_2_five⟧ gives local metrizability. *Step 2, (2)*: $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is metrizable for every basic construction ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.metrizableSpace_M⟧, and $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ by Proposition 19.7.13 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.metrizableSpace_MHat⟧. *Step 3, (3)*: Theorem 19.4.6 (2) ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.theorem_19_4_6_two⟧. *Step 4, Remark 19.7.22* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.remark_19_7_22⟧: the equivalence is Proposition 19.4.7 ⟦Chapter19/Metrizability · BasicConstruction.proposition_19_4_7⟧, and the metrizability of the transition sets is Step 2.

**Proof of the perfect-map lemmas.** Let $f : X \to Y$ be continuous with finite fibres and the tube property: every open $U \supset f^{-1}(y)$ contains some $f^{-1}(V)$, $V \ni y$ open.
- *Step 1, upward* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · Chapter19.paracompactSpace_of_tube⟧. Let $Y$ be paracompact and $\mathcal U$ an open cover of $X$. Every fibre is covered by finitely many members, and their union contains a tube $f^{-1}(V_y)$. A locally finite refinement of the cover $\{V_y\}$ of $Y$, pulled back and intersected with the finitely many members chosen for each $y$, is a locally finite refinement of $\mathcal U$.
- *Step 2, downward* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · Chapter19.paracompactSpace_of_tube_of_isOpenMap⟧. Let $X$ be paracompact and $f$ open and surjective. The images of a locally finite refinement of the pulled-back cover form an open cover of $Y$. It is locally finite: a point $y$ has a tube around its finite fibre meeting only finitely many members.
- *Step 3, Hausdorff* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · Chapter19.t2Space_of_disjoint_fibre_nhds⟧: if $Y$ is Hausdorff and the points of each fibre have mutually disjoint open neighbourhoods, then $X$ is Hausdorff. Points in different fibres are separated by preimages; points in one fibre by hypothesis.

**Proof of Propositions 19.7.23 and 19.7.24.**
- *Step 1, the properness condition for $|\mathcal A|$* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.PropernessV2A⟧. $\mathcal A$ has no translation-groupoid uniformizers. We read Definition 19.4.1 (2) through the uniformizers $\hat\Psi$ of $\hat F$: every $e \in E$ has an open $U(e)$ with $\overline{|\hat\Psi(U(e))|} \subset |\hat\Psi(E)|$ ⟦Chapter19/TopologyLemmas · Chapter19.OrbitChartV2⟧. We use $|P|(|\hat\Psi(V)|) = |\Psi(\hat\tau(V))|$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.image_orbitMap_imageA⟧.
- *Step 2, Proposition 19.7.23, $\Rightarrow$* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.proposition_19_7_23_hyp⟧. Let $o = \hat\tau(e)$, with $U(e)$ from the condition for $|\mathcal A|$, and take the open set $\hat\tau(U(e)) \ni o$. Since $|P|$ is closed (§XIX.29), the closure of $|\Psi(\hat\tau(U(e)))| = |P|(|\hat\Psi(U(e))|)$ is contained in $|P|$ of the closure of $|\hat\Psi(U(e))|$. That lies in $|P|(|\hat\Psi(E)|) \subset |\Psi(O)|$.
- *Step 3, $\Leftarrow$.* Given $e$, take $U \ni \hat\tau(e)$ from the condition for $|\mathcal B|$ and the open set $\hat\tau^{-1}(U)$. A point $w$ of the closure of $|\hat\Psi(\hat\tau^{-1}(U))|$ has $|P|(w)$ in the closure of $|\Psi(U)|$, by the continuity of $|P|$. So $|P|(w) \in |\Psi(O)|$, and by (19.34) $w \in |\hat\Psi(E)|$.
- *Step 4, Proposition 19.7.24* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.proposition_19_7_24_hyp⟧. $|P|$ is continuous, open, surjective, with finite fibres (§XIX.28) and the tube property (§XIX.29). The two perfect-map lemmas give the two directions.

**Proof of Theorem 19.7.25.**
- *Step 1, Hausdorff* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.t2Space_natTopA⟧: the even covering of §XIX.29 gives disjoint neighbourhoods of the points of each fibre; apply Step 3 of the perfect-map lemmas.
- *Step 2, locally metrizable* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.exists_metrizable_nhds_natTopA⟧: every point lies in the image of an open embedding $|\hat\Psi|$ (Lemma 19.7.17), and $|E|$ is locally metrizable by Theorem 7.3.1 ⟦Chapter7/OrbitTopology · theorem_7_3_1⟧.
- *Step 3, (1)* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.theorem_19_7_25_one_hyp⟧. $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ is metrizable, hence paracompact, so $T_{|\mathcal A|}$ is paracompact (Proposition 19.7.24). A paracompact Hausdorff locally metrizable space is metrizable by Smirnov's theorem ⟦Chapter2/Metrizable · metrizableSpace_of_locally⟧.
- *Step 4, (2), (3).* The categories with topology carry $T_{|\mathcal A|}$ and $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ (Definition 17.1.1). Definition 17.8.1 asks:
  - that $|P|$ be continuous and surjective;
  - that $P$ have finite fibres and be surjective on objects;
  - that the fibres of $|P|$ be evenly covered;
  - that $\hat\varphi \mapsto (P(\hat\varphi), s(\hat\varphi))$ be bijective.

  These are §XIX.28, Definition 19.7.1 and §XIX.29 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.theorem_19_7_25_three_hyp⟧.
- *Step 5, (4a)* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.theorem_19_7_25_four_a_hyp⟧: the even covering of §XIX.29; the metrizability of $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ is not needed.
- *Step 6, (4b) does not hold as we read it* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · not_theorem_19_7_25_four_b⟧. In the absolute-value construction, $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ is metrizable, the orbit-space topology of $[0, \infty)$. $|P|^{-1}(|0|) = \{|(1, 0)|\}$, and $|P|$ is not injective on any open neighbourhood of $|(1, 0)|$ (§XIX.28).
- *Step 7, the witness* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · theorem_19_7_25_absBasic⟧. For the absolute-value construction both properness conditions hold (Propositions 19.4.7 and 19.7.23). $T_{|\mathcal A|}$ is metrizable and $P$ is a proper covering functor of groupoidal categories with topology.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The proofs of Propositions 19.7.23 and 19.7.24 cite Corollary 19.7.20, including its part (3). What they need is that $|P|$ is continuous, open, surjective, with finite fibres and the tube property of Corollary 19.7.20 (2), that is, a perfect map; with this the statements hold as printed. The properness condition for $T_{|\mathcal A|}$ is read through the uniformizers of $\hat F$. Theorem 19.7.25 (1) is proved from paracompactness, Hausdorffness and local metrizability. All statements carry the compatibility of Definition 19.7.11, since they rest on Lemma 19.7.17.

### XIX.31 Proposition 19.7.26, Definition 19.7.27, Theorems 19.7.28, 19.7.29 (2017: Proposition 19.8, Theorem 19.10), pp. 926–929

**Statement.** *Proposition 19.7.26* (p. 926, stronger) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.proposition_19_7_26⟧: for a finite-to-one covering functor $P$ and a basic construction for $P$ with the class hypothesis:
- (i) every $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ has its natural M-polyfold structure ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.basePolyfold⟧, with local sc-diffeomorphisms $s$ and $t$;
- (ii) every $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ has an M-polyfold structure ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.hatPolyfold⟧ for which $p$ is a surjective local sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_pB⟧;
- (iii) it is the only such structure ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isScDiffeoOn_id_of_isLocalScDiffeo_pB⟧;
- (iv) $(e, \hat\varphi, e') \mapsto (p(e, \hat\varphi, e'), e)$ is an sc-diffeomorphism onto the sub-M-polyfold $M(\Psi, \Psi') \,{}_s\!\times_{\hat\tau} E$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.fibSet⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isSubMPolyfold_fibSet⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.fibPolyfold⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isScDiffeoOn_toFibSet⟧;
- (v) if the domains $O$ are tame, then $E$, $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ and $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ are tame ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isTame_E⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isTame_basePolyfold⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isTame_hatPolyfold⟧.

The printed metrizability of $|\mathcal B|$ is kept as a hypothesis and not used. Neither it nor the compatibility is needed, and the M-polyfold structure is characterized. *Definition 19.7.27* (p. 928, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.definition_19_7_27⟧: the diagrams (19.30)–(19.32) are the structural commutative diagrams, and they commute. *Theorem 19.7.28* (p. 928, new in the 2021 edition; explicit): under the compatibility of Definition 19.7.11 with its witness ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic_isCompatible⟧, the class hypothesis, and the printed metrizability of $|\mathcal B|$:
- (1) $p$ is a surjective local sc-diffeomorphism, and the structural diagrams have the expected sc-smoothness ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.theorem_19_7_28_one_hyp⟧;
- (2) $\mathcal A$ and $\mathcal B$ are groupoidal categories with topology and $P$ is a proper covering functor ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.theorem_19_7_28_two_hyp⟧;
- (3) the members of $\hat F$ are exactly proper covering uniformizers in the sense of Definition 17.8.10 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.toProperCoveringUniformizer⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.ofProperCoveringUniformizer⟧, they form a construction of Definition 17.8.12 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.toProperCoveringConstruction⟧, and with the M-polyfolds of Proposition 19.7.26 they give a proper polyfold covering structure in the sense of Definition 17.8.13 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.toProperPolyfoldCoveringStructure⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.toProperPolyfoldCoveringFunctor⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.theorem_19_7_28_three_hyp⟧.

*Theorem 19.7.29* (p. 929, restated) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.theorem_19_7_29_hyp⟧: under the same hypotheses, $\mathcal A$ and $\mathcal B$ have natural polyfold structures. (1) The local representative of $P$ with respect to $\hat\Psi$ is $\hat\tau$. (2) The diagram (19.40) commutes, with $\langle P, s\rangle$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.fibredMorP⟧, $\hat\Gamma$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.gammaHat⟧ and $\Gamma \,{}_s\!\times_{\hat\tau} \hat\Psi$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.gammaFib⟧. So $\langle p, s\rangle = (\Gamma \,{}_s\!\times_{\hat\tau} \hat\Psi)^{-1} \circ \langle P, s\rangle \circ \hat\Gamma$, which is sc-smooth. We read the polyfold structure of $\mathcal A$ through Definition 17.8.13, since $\mathcal A$ has no translation-groupoid uniformizers of its own.

**Proof of Proposition 19.7.26.**
- *Step 1, the base* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.basePolyfold⟧: $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ carries the structure of Theorem 19.5.1 for the representative of $\mathcal F$ ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · TransitionData.transPolyfold⟧, for which $s$ and $t$ are local sc-diffeomorphisms ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · TransitionData.isLocalScDiffeo_src⟧ ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructure · TransitionData.isLocalScDiffeo_tgt⟧.
- *Step 2, the fibred product* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isSubMPolyfold_fibSet⟧. Since $s$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism and $\hat\tau$ is sc-smooth on objects, the fibred product is a sub-M-polyfold of $M(\Psi, \Psi') \times E$ (Part I, ⟦Chapter2/FibredProduct · isSubMPolyfold_fibredProduct'⟧). Its first projection is a local sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_fibFst⟧, since $\hat\tau$ is one on objects.
- *Step 3, the transport* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.hatPolyfold⟧. $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ is Hausdorff and paracompact ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.t2Space_MHat⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.paracompactSpace_MHat⟧, being metrizable. (19.26) is a homeomorphism onto the fibred product ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.fibSetHomeomorph⟧. Transport the sub-M-polyfold structure along it ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstructionExamples · MPolyfold.transport⟧; this is the descent along a homeomorphism of Chapter 9. Then (19.26) is an sc-diffeomorphism by construction ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isScDiffeoOn_toFibSet⟧, which is (iv).
- *Step 4, (ii)* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_pB⟧: $p$ is the first projection composed with (19.26), a composite of local sc-diffeomorphisms ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_of_isScDiffeoOn_univ⟧. It is surjective by Proposition 19.7.14.
- *Step 5, (iii)* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isScDiffeoOn_id_of_isLocalScDiffeo_pB⟧. If $Q$ is another structure for which $p$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism, then the identity $Q \to$ (Step 3) is, near each point, the composite of the local inverse of $p$ for one structure with $p$ for the other. So it is a bijective local sc-diffeomorphism, hence an sc-diffeomorphism.
- *Step 6, (v).* $E$ is tame because $\hat\tau$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism onto the tame $O$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isTame_E⟧. $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is tame because $s$ is one onto $O$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isTame_basePolyfold⟧. $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ is tame because $p$ is one onto $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isTame_hatPolyfold⟧.

**Proof of Definition 19.7.27** ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.definition_19_7_27⟧. The four diagrams commute by §XIX.24 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.transMap_src_tgt⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.transMap_inv⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.transMap_unit⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.transMap_comp⟧.

**Proof of Theorem 19.7.28.**
- *Step 1, the top maps.* In each case one composes with $p$ or $\hat\tau$, which are local sc-diffeomorphisms:
  - $\hat s$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism since $\hat\tau \circ \hat s = s \circ p$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_srcHat⟧;
  - $\hat t$ likewise since $\hat\tau' \circ \hat t = t \circ p$, with $\hat t$ continuous by the compatibility ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_tgtHat⟧;
  - the top unit is sc-smooth from $p \circ \hat u = u \circ \hat\tau$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isScSmoothMap_unitHat⟧;
  - the top inversion is an sc-diffeomorphism from $p \circ \hat\iota = \iota \circ p$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isScDiffeoOn_invHat⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isScSmoothMap_invHat⟧, the bottom one being an sc-diffeomorphism (Corollary 19.5.2) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isScDiffeoOn_invB⟧;
  - the top multiplication is a local sc-diffeomorphism from $\hat s \circ \hat m = \hat s \circ \mathrm{pr}_2$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_mulHat⟧ and sc-smooth in the form of Definition 17.1.8 (c) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isScSmoothMap_mulHat⟧.

  With Proposition 19.7.26 and Corollary 19.5.2 for the bottom maps this is (1) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.theorem_19_7_28_one_hyp⟧.
- *Step 2, (2)* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.theorem_19_7_28_two_hyp⟧: Theorem 19.7.25 (2), (3), with the reducible forms of the two categories ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.gctA⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.gctB⟧.
- *Step 3, (3), the uniformizers* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.toProperCoveringUniformizer⟧. A member of $\hat F(\beta)$ is a proper covering uniformizer of Definition 17.8.10 ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringUniformizer · ProperCoveringUniformizer⟧. Its conditions (1) and (3) follow from the cartesian property. Condition (4) follows from Theorem 19.2.2 (3) for $|\Psi|$, Lemma 19.7.17 (2) for $|\hat\Psi|$, and (19.34). Conversely ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.ofProperCoveringUniformizer⟧, a uniformizer of Definition 17.8.10 satisfies Definition 19.7.4: use 17.8.10 (3), the injectivity of $\Psi$, and the fact that a morphism of $\mathcal A$ is determined by its image and source. The two passages are inverse on the data ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.ofProperCoveringUniformizer_to⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.toProperCoveringUniformizer_of⟧.
- *Step 4, (3), the structure* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.toProperPolyfoldCoveringStructure⟧. The construction has exactly the members of $\hat F$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.mem_toProperCoveringConstruction_iff⟧. The base is the polyfold structure of $\mathcal B$ of Theorem 19.5.3 (2) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.polyfoldB⟧ ⟦Chapter19/PolyfoldStructureGCT · BasicConstruction.toPolyfoldConstruction⟧. The M-polyfolds of the transition sets are those of Proposition 19.7.26, with $p$ surjective. This is Definition 17.8.13 ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringPolyfold · ProperPolyfoldCoveringStructure⟧, and $P$ is a proper polyfold covering functor ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringPolyfold · ProperPolyfoldCoveringFunctor⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.theorem_19_7_28_three_hyp⟧.

**Proof of Theorem 19.7.29** ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.theorem_19_7_29_hyp⟧.
- *Step 1, the polyfold structures.* For $\mathcal B$, Theorem 19.5.3 (2): the footprints cover $|\mathcal B|$ ⟦Chapter17/PolyfoldConstruction · PolyfoldConstruction.HasCoveringUniformizers⟧. For $\mathcal A$, the proper polyfold covering structure of Theorem 19.7.28 (3), with the open embeddings $|\hat\Psi|$ (Lemma 19.7.17 (2)), whose images cover $|\mathcal A|$ (Lemma 19.7.17 (1)).
- *Step 2, (1).* $P \circ \hat\Psi = \Psi \circ \hat\tau$ on objects and morphisms. $\hat\tau$ is sc-smooth on morphisms, and it is a proper covering functor in the sense of Definition 8.5.1, because its fibre over $\bar o$ is finite (§XIX.24, Remark 17.8.5 ⟦Chapter17/ProperCoveringModel · ProperCoveringModel.isProperCovering⟧).
- *Step 3, (2).* For $a = (e, \hat\varphi, e')$, $(\Gamma \,{}_s\!\times_{\hat\tau} \hat\Psi)(p(a), e) = (P(\hat\varphi), \hat\Psi(e)) = \langle P, s\rangle(\hat\Gamma(a))$, since $s(\hat\varphi) = \hat\Psi(e)$. So the diagram commutes, and the image of $\hat\Gamma$ goes onto the image of $\Gamma \,{}_s\!\times_{\hat\tau} \hat\Psi$.
- *Step 4, injectivity.* $\Gamma \,{}_s\!\times_{\hat\tau} \hat\Psi$ is injective: $\hat\Psi$ is injective on objects, and an element of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is determined by its source, its morphism and, through the injective $\Psi'$, its target. Hence $(\Gamma \,{}_s\!\times_{\hat\tau} \hat\Psi)(q) = \langle P, s\rangle(\hat\Gamma(a))$ iff $q = \langle p, s\rangle(a)$, which is the formula of the text.
- *Step 5, smoothness.* $\langle p, s\rangle$ is sc-smooth, by Proposition 19.7.26 (iv).

*Where Lean departs from the text.* In Proposition 19.7.26, $M(\Psi, \Psi') \,{}_s\!\times_p E$ is read as $M(\Psi, \Psi') \,{}_s\!\times_{\hat\tau} E$, as in Lemma 19.7.10 and Theorem 19.7.29. "Characterized by" is read as: any M-polyfold structure for which $p$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism agrees with this one. "$(F, \mathcal F)$ is tame" means that the domains $O$ are tame, as in Theorem 19.5.3 (2). The metrizability of $|\mathcal B|$ is kept in Proposition 19.7.26 and Theorem 19.7.28 (1) as printed but is not used there. It is used in Theorem 19.7.28 (2), (3) and Theorem 19.7.29, for the groupoidal categories with topology. Statements involving $\hat t$ carry the compatibility of Definition 19.7.11: $\hat\iota$, $\hat m$, the proper polyfold covering structure and Theorem 19.7.29. "$\mathcal A$ has a natural polyfold structure" is read through Definition 17.8.13, which says $\mathcal A$ becomes a polyfold through $P$. Formula (19.40) is stated as the equivalence of Step 4.

### XIX.32 The witness: the absolute value $|\mathcal A| \cong \mathbb R \to |\mathcal B| \cong [0, \infty)$

**Statement.** *The absolute-value construction* (no counterpart in the text; extra) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic⟧: $P : \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes (\{\pm 1\} \times \mathbb R) \to \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$, $(i, x) \mapsto x$, is a finite-to-one covering functor ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absCovP_isFiniteToOneCovering⟧. Its basic construction (§XIX.24) has these properties:
- it is compatible ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic_isCompatible⟧ and has uniformizers at every class ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic_hasClassUniformizers⟧;
- its natural topologies are the orbit-space topologies ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic_natTopA_eq⟧, $|\mathcal A| \cong \mathbb R$ and $|\mathcal B| \cong [0, \infty)$, with $|P|$ the absolute value;
- it satisfies Proposition 19.7.26 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfoldExamples · absBasic_proposition_19_7_26⟧, Theorem 19.7.28 (1) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfoldExamples · absBasic_theorem_19_7_28_one⟧ and (3) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfoldExamples · absPolyfoldCoveringFunctor⟧, and Theorem 19.7.29 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfoldExamples · absBasic_theorem_19_7_29⟧;
- it is non-degenerate ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfoldExamples · absBasic_covering_polyfold_nondegenerate⟧: non-trivial isotropy, and a covering with two sheets away from $0$.

It is the witness for the compatibility reading and the hypotheses of §§19.7.18–19.7.29. The same example shows that Proposition 19.7.18, Corollary 19.7.20 (3) and Theorem 19.7.25 (4b) do not hold as we read them (§§XIX.28–XIX.30).

**Proof.**
- *Step 1, the model at $0$* ⟦Chapter19/ProperCoveringModelExample · Chapter19.absP_properCoveringModel⟧. The functor is the sheet functor of Chapter 17 for $S = G = \{\pm 1\}$, $O = \mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter19/ProperCoveringModelExample · Chapter19.absP_eq_sheetFunctor⟧. It is a proper covering model with point $0$ ⟦Chapter19/ProperCoveringModelExample · Chapter19.absP_properCoveringModel_pt⟧. It is connected ⟦Chapter19/ProperCoveringModelExample · Chapter19.absP_properCoveringModel_connected⟧, with $\hat\tau^{-1}(0) = \{\pm 1\} \times \{0\}$ ⟦Chapter19/ProperCoveringModelExample · Chapter19.absP_properCoveringModel_fiber⟧. Remark 17.8.5 recovers that it is a proper covering functor ⟦Chapter19/ProperCoveringModelExample · Chapter19.absP_isProperCovering_of_model⟧.
- *Step 2, the hypotheses* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfoldExamples · absBasic_covering_polyfold_nondegenerate⟧:
  - finite-to-one covering, compatibility and class uniformizers, from §XIX.24;
  - $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ metrizable ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfoldExamples · absBasic_metrizableSpace⟧, the orbit-space topology of $[0, \infty)$ (§XIX.30);
  - tame domains, all open subsets of $\mathbb R$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfoldExamples · absBasic_isTame⟧;
  - every $\hat F(\beta)$ non-empty;
  - two points over the distinguished point of the member at $0$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfoldExamples · absUnifZero⟧, and $-1$ acts non-trivially on $O = \mathbb R$.
- *Step 3, the conclusions.* At $\hat\Psi = \hat\Psi' = {}$ the member at $0$:
  - $p$ is a surjective local sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfoldExamples · absBasic_proposition_19_7_26⟧;
  - $\hat s$ and $\hat t$ are local sc-diffeomorphisms ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfoldExamples · absBasic_theorem_19_7_28_one⟧;
  - $\mathcal B$ has covering uniformizers and $\Gamma \,{}_s\!\times_{\hat\tau} \hat\Psi$ is injective ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfoldExamples · absBasic_theorem_19_7_29⟧;
  - the proper polyfold covering functor is that of Theorem 19.7.28 (3) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfoldExamples · absPolyfoldCoveringFunctor⟧.

  Theorem 19.7.25 (1)–(4a) hold there ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · theorem_19_7_25_absBasic⟧.

### XIX.33 Definitions 19.8.1, 19.8.2, Lemma 19.8.3 and the functor $\hat\Gamma$ of (19.55) (new in the 2021 edition), pp. 930–936

**Statement.** *The setting (19.41)–(19.46)* (pp. 930–932, same): a finite-to-one covering functor $P : \mathcal A \to \mathcal B$ (Definition 19.7.1) and a functor $\mu_B : \mathcal B \to \mathrm{Ban}$ (§19.6) form the pull-back diagram $(P, \mu_B)$; $E_B$ is the category of $\mu_B$ and $P_B : E_B \to \mathcal B$ its projection. The functor $\mu_B \circ P : \mathcal A \to \mathrm{Ban}$, whose category is $P^*E_B$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · BanFunctor.pull⟧, and the action groupoid of a groupoid action ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · GroupoidAction.groupoid⟧ are the two auxiliary constructions. *Definition 19.8.1* (p. 932, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · GroupoidalCategory.PullbackUniformizer⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.definition_19_8_1⟧: a uniformizer for $(P, \mu_B)$ at $\beta$ is a triple $(\Psi, \hat\Psi, \Gamma)$ in which $(\Psi, \hat\Psi)$ is a uniformizer for $P$ at $\beta$ (Definition 19.7.3) and $(\Psi, \Gamma)$ is a strong bundle uniformizer for $P_B$ (Definition 19.6.1) over the same $\Psi$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · GroupoidalCategory.StrongBundleOver⟧, so that (19.45) commutes and $\Gamma(w) = (\Psi(Q(w)), A(w))$, which is (19.46) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · StrongBundleOver.obj_eq⟧. *Definition 19.8.2* (p. 932, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · GroupoidalCategory.PullbackUniformizerConstruction⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_8_2⟧: a uniformizer construction $\tilde F$ assigns to $\beta$ a set $\tilde F(\beta)$ of such triples; it induces $F$ for $\mathcal B$, $\bar F$ for $P_B$ and $\hat F$ for $P$, and $\hat F$ and $\bar F$ both induce $F$. *Lemma 19.8.3* (p. 933, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.lemma_19_8_3⟧: for $(w, (\varphi, A(w)), w') \in M(\Gamma, \Gamma')$ with image $(e, \varphi, e')$ under $m$, one has $s(\varphi) = \Psi(e)$, $t(\varphi) = \Psi'(e')$, $Q(w) = e$, $Q'(w') = e'$ and $A'(w') = \mu_B(\varphi)(A(w))$, the relations (19.48). *The functor $\hat\Gamma$ of (19.55)* (p. 936, same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.gammaHat⟧: $\hat\Gamma : \tau^*W \to P^*E_B$, $\hat\Gamma(\hat e, w) = (\hat\Psi(\hat e), \Gamma(w))$, is injective on objects, full and faithful, and makes (19.61) commute.

**Proof of the construction of $\mu_B \circ P$.** *Step 1, the total space and the fibres* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · BanFunctor.pull⟧: the total space is the set of pairs $(\alpha, v)$ with $P_B(v) = P(\alpha)$, projected to $\alpha$. The fibre over $\alpha$ is identified with the fibre $\mu_B(P(\alpha))$ by $(\alpha, v) \mapsto v$, a topological linear isomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · BanFunctor.pullCLE⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · BanFunctor.pullFiberCLE⟧; the topology and linear structure of the fibre are taken through it, and Banachability is transferred from $\mu_B(P(\alpha))$ by composing with this isomorphism. *Step 2, the lifts* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · BanFunctor.pullLift⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · BanFunctor.pullLift_val⟧: the lift of $\hat\varphi$ is $\mu_B(P(\hat\varphi))$ on the second component, $(\alpha, v) \mapsto (t(\hat\varphi), \mu_B(P(\hat\varphi))(v))$. *Step 3, the functor axioms.* For the unit, $P(1_\alpha) = 1_{P(\alpha)}$ and $\mu_B$ maps units to the identity; for composites, $P$ preserves composition and $\mu_B(P(\hat\varphi')P(\hat\varphi)) = \mu_B(P(\hat\varphi')) \circ \mu_B(P(\hat\varphi))$, the composability over $\mathcal B$ coming from $s(P\hat\varphi') = P(s\hat\varphi') = P(t\hat\varphi) = t(P\hat\varphi)$. The action of a morphism of $P^*E_B$ is then $(\hat\varphi, (\alpha, v)) \mapsto (t(\hat\varphi), \mu_B(P(\hat\varphi))(v))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · BanFunctor.pull_act⟧. *Step 4, the forgetful functor* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · BanFunctor.pullToE⟧: $(\alpha, v) \mapsto v$, $(\hat\varphi, (\alpha, v)) \mapsto (P(\hat\varphi), v)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · BanFunctor.pullMorE⟧ is a functor $P^*E_B \to E_B$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · BanFunctor.act_pullMorE⟧.

**Proof of Definition 19.8.1.** *Step 1, the strong bundle part over a given $\Psi$* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · GroupoidalCategory.StrongBundleOver⟧. The diagram (19.45) has one $\Psi$, so the strong bundle part is formalized as Definition 19.6.1 (1) with the base $O$, the group $G$ and the functor $\Psi$ taken from $\Psi$. Its fields are: a Hausdorff paracompact total space $W$ with a $G$-action; a projection $Q : W \to O$ with linear fibres and a strong bundle structure; equivariance $Q(g \ast w) = g \ast Q(w)$, each $g \ast \cdot$ a strong bundle map covering $g \ast \cdot$ on $O$; a functor $\Gamma : G \ltimes W \to E_B$ injective on objects, full and faithful; $P_B \circ \Gamma = \Psi \circ (\mathrm{Id} \ltimes Q)$ on objects and morphisms; and for each $o$ a topological linear isomorphism $A : Q^{-1}(o) \to \mu_B(\Psi(o))$ which is $\Gamma$ on the fibre. From these, $\Gamma$ is a uniformizer of $E_B$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · StrongBundleOver.toUniformizer⟧ carrying the structure (1) of Definition 19.6.1 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · StrongBundleOver.toStr⟧, whose underlying uniformizer is $\Psi$ by definition ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · StrongBundleOver.toStr_toBase⟧. With $A(w)$ the fibre part of $\Gamma(w)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · StrongBundleOver.amap⟧, (19.46) is the field relating $A$ and $\Gamma$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · StrongBundleOver.obj_eq⟧. *Step 2, the triple* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · GroupoidalCategory.PullbackUniformizer⟧: a covering uniformizer $(\Psi, \hat\Psi)$ (§19.7) together with strong bundle data over its $\Psi$. It is a strong bundle uniformizer at $\beta$, the point of $O$ over $\beta$ being the base point of the covering model ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.toStrongBundleUniformizer⟧. *Step 3, (19.45) and (19.46)* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.definition_19_8_1⟧: $P \circ \hat\Psi = \Psi \circ \tau$ on objects and morphisms are the commutativity fields of the covering uniformizer (Definition 19.7.4); $P_B \circ \Gamma = \Psi \circ Q$ on objects and morphisms are fields of Step 1; and $\Gamma(w) = A(w)$ is Step 1's (19.46).

**Proof of Definition 19.8.2.** *Step 1* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · GroupoidalCategory.PullbackUniformizerConstruction⟧: $\tilde F$ is a family of sets $\tilde F(\beta)$ indexed by objects, as Definitions 19.1.3 and 19.7.7 are read. *Step 2, the induced constructions.* (3) $\hat F(\beta)$ is the image of $\tilde F(\beta)$ under $(\Psi, \hat\Psi, \Gamma) \mapsto (\Psi, \hat\Psi)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.hatU⟧; (1) $F$ is the construction for $\mathcal B$ induced by $\hat F$ as in (19.24) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.toUniformizerConstruction⟧; (2) $\bar F(\beta)$ is the set of the strong bundle uniformizers $(\Psi, \Gamma)$ of the members of $\tilde F(\beta)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.barF⟧. *Step 3* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_8_2⟧: $\hat F$ induces $F$ by definition; for $\bar F$, the base of $(\Psi, \Gamma)$ is $\Psi$ (Step 1 of Definition 19.8.1), and the two images of images coincide.

**Proof of Lemma 19.8.3.** *Step 1, the transition sets (19.47).* $M(\Gamma, \Gamma')$ is the transition set of the functors $\Gamma, \Gamma'$ into $E_B$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.TransG⟧; $m : M(\Gamma, \Gamma') \to M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is §19.6's projection $p$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.mG⟧, and $p : M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi') \to M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is §19.7's map ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.pHat⟧. *Step 2, the relations* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.lemma_19_8_3⟧. The first two relations are the source and target conditions of the element $m(h) \in M(\Psi, \Psi')$. The third and fourth hold by the definition of $m$. For the fifth, (19.46) at the target writes $A'(w')$ as $\Gamma'(w')$, which by the target condition of $h$ is the action of the morphism $(\varphi, \Gamma(w))$ of $E_B$; by the source condition of $h$ and (19.46) at the source, $\Gamma(w) = A(w)$, so this is $\mu_B(\varphi)(A(w))$.

**Proof of the construction of $\tau^*W$ and $\hat\Gamma$.** *Step 1, groupoid actions.* An action of a groupoid $X$ on a set $Y$ over an anchor $Y \to \mathrm{Obj}$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · GroupoidalCategory.GroupoidAction⟧ gives the action groupoid with objects $Y$ and morphisms the pairs $(\sigma, y)$ with $s(\sigma)$ the anchor of $y$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · GroupoidAction.groupoid⟧, with inverse $(\sigma^{-1}, \sigma \ast y)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · GroupoidAction.morInv⟧ and composite $(\sigma'\sigma, y)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · GroupoidAction.morComp⟧. *Step 2, the action of $\hat E$ on $\tau^*W$* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.tauWAction⟧. The objects of $\tau^*W$ are the pairs $(\hat e, w)$ with $\tau(\hat e) = Q(w)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.TauW⟧. A morphism $\hat\sigma$ with $\tau(\hat\sigma) = (g, o)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.tauG⟧ acts by $(s(\hat\sigma), w) \mapsto (t(\hat\sigma), g \ast w)$; the result lies in $\tau^*W$ because $Q(g \ast w) = g \ast Q(w) = g \ast \tau(s\hat\sigma) = \tau(t\hat\sigma)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.tau_obj_tgt⟧. The unit axiom holds because $\tau$ maps units to units, so $g = 1$, and the composition axiom because $\tau$ preserves composites and the $G$-action is a group action. $\tau^*W$ is the action groupoid ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.tauW⟧. *Step 3, $\hat\Gamma$* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.gammaHat⟧. On objects $\hat\Gamma(\hat e, w) = (\hat\Psi(\hat e), \Gamma(w))$, an object of $P^*E_B$ since $P_B(\Gamma(w)) = \Psi(Q(w)) = \Psi(\tau(\hat e)) = P(\hat\Psi(\hat e))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.gammaHatObj⟧; on morphisms $(\hat\sigma, (\hat e, w)) \mapsto (\hat\Psi(\hat\sigma), \hat\Gamma(\hat e, w))$. The target condition uses $\Gamma(g \ast w) = \mu_B(P(\hat\Psi(\hat\sigma)))(\Gamma(w))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.obj_tauG_smul⟧, which follows from $P_B \circ \Gamma = \Psi \circ (\mathrm{Id} \ltimes Q)$ on morphisms and $P \circ \hat\Psi = \Psi \circ \tau$, together with the action formula of $P^*E_B$. The composition and unit axioms reduce to those of $\hat\Psi$. *Step 4, injective on objects* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.injective_gammaHat⟧: equal images give $\hat\Psi(\hat e_1) = \hat\Psi(\hat e_2)$ and $\Gamma(w_1) = \Gamma(w_2)$, and both functors are injective on objects. *Step 5, faithful* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.faithful_gammaHat⟧: two morphisms with the same source, the same target and the same image have the same object component, the same source and target in $\hat E$ and the same image under $\hat\Psi$, so they agree because $\hat\Psi$ is faithful. *Step 6, full* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.full_gammaHat⟧: a morphism $(\varphi, \hat\Gamma(\hat e_1, w_1))$ from $\hat\Gamma(\hat e_1, w_1)$ to $\hat\Gamma(\hat e_2, w_2)$ has $\varphi : \hat\Psi(\hat e_1) \to \hat\Psi(\hat e_2)$, so fullness of $\hat\Psi$ gives $\hat\sigma : \hat e_1 \to \hat e_2$ with $\hat\Psi(\hat\sigma) = \varphi$. The morphism $(\hat\sigma, (\hat e_1, w_1))$ of $\tau^*W$ has target $(\hat e_2, g \ast w_1)$. By Step 3, $\Gamma(g \ast w_1) = \mu_B(P(\varphi))(\Gamma(w_1)) = \Gamma(w_2)$, and $g \ast w_1 = w_2$ by injectivity of $\Gamma$ on objects. *Step 7, (19.61)* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.gammaHat_comm⟧: the projection of $P^*E_B$ composed with $\hat\Gamma$ is $\hat\Psi$ on the first component, and the forgetful functor composed with $\hat\Gamma$ is $\Gamma \circ \hat\tau$ with $\hat\tau(\hat e, w) = w$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · PullbackUniformizer.tauWProj⟧. Both hold by definition, and on morphisms they use $P \circ \hat\Psi = \Psi \circ \tau$.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The text calls $(\Psi, \Gamma)$ a strong bundle uniformizer and draws one $\Psi$ in (19.45). The formalization builds the strong bundle data over the given $\Psi$, so the sharing of $\Psi$ holds by construction and is not a condition. $P^*E_B$ is the category of the functor $\mu_B \circ P$, whose objects $(\alpha, v)$ carry $\beta = P(\alpha)$ implicitly.

### XIX.34 Definition 19.8.4, the note after it, Definition 19.8.5 (new in the 2021 edition), pp. 934–937

**Statement.** *Definition 19.8.4* (p. 934, new in the 2021 edition; explicit) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.PullbackTransitionConstruction⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_8_4⟧: a transition construction for $\tilde F$ is a transition construction $\mathcal F$ for the underlying $F$ such that (1) $(F, \mathcal F)$ defines a metrizable natural topology on $|\mathcal B|$, and (2) the strong bundle construction $(\bar F, \mathcal F)$ is coherent in the sense of Definition 19.6.15 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.IsCoherent⟧; (3), that $(\hat F, \mathcal F)$ is a basic construction for $P$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.toCBC⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.PullbackTransitionConstruction.toCoveringBasicConstruction⟧, holds by construction. We read "defines a metrizable natural topology" in (1) as the standing reading $F(\alpha) \ne \emptyset$ on every class (needed to define $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ by Definition 19.2.1) together with the metrizability of $T_{|\mathcal B|}$; the standing reading is the field `hasClassUniformizers`. The compatibility reading of Definition 19.7.11 is not part of the definition; the results that use it take it as a hypothesis. *The note after Definition 19.8.4* (p. 934, new in the 2021 edition; restated) ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · definition_19_8_4_one_implies_three⟧: as printed, (1) and $\tilde F$ give (3). With the compatibility reading of Definition 19.7.11 the note would need that reading as well: a construction satisfying Definitions 19.8.4 and 19.8.5 need not be compatible ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · definition_19_8_4_one_without_compatible⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · definition_19_8_4_without_compatible⟧. *Definition 19.8.5* (p. 937, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.PullbackBasicConstruction⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_8_5⟧: a basic construction for $(P, \mu_B)$ is a pair $(\tilde F, \mathcal F)$ of a uniformizer construction and a transition construction for it.

**Proof of the coherence condition (2).** *Step 1, the pull-backs.* For members $(\Psi, \hat\Psi, \Gamma)$, $(\Psi', \hat\Psi', \Gamma')$ of $\tilde F$, $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ carries the natural M-polyfold structure of Theorem 19.5.1 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.basePoly⟧, for which the source and target maps are sc-smooth ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isScSmoothMap_srcB⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isScSmoothMap_tgtB⟧. The fibred products $M(\Psi, \Psi')\,{}_s\!\times_Q W$ and $M(\Psi, \Psi')\,{}_t\!\times_{Q'} W'$ of (19.14) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.SrcPB⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.TgtPB⟧ are paracompact ⟦Chapter2/StrongBundlePullback · paracompactSpace_pullbackSpace⟧ and carry the pull-back strong bundles of Proposition 2.6.8 ⟦Chapter3/ParamPerturbation · StrongBundle.pullback⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.srcBundle⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.tgtBundle⟧. *Step 2, $H$ and $H'$ of (19.16).* $H((e, \varphi, e'), w) = (w, (\varphi, \Gamma(w)), \Gamma'^{-1}(\mu_B(\varphi)(\Gamma(w))))$ is §19.6's lift of a base transition through a point of the fibre ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.altH⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.lift⟧, with inverse $h \mapsto (m(h), s(h))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.altHinv⟧. The two identities $H^{-1} H = \mathrm{id}$ and $H H^{-1} = \mathrm{id}$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.altHinv_altH⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.altH_altHinv⟧ are the facts that the lift lies over the base transition and that an element is the lift of its projection ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.proj_lift⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.lift_proj⟧. $H'$ is the inverse of the lift through the target ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.altH'⟧, with inverse $h \mapsto (m(h), t(h))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.altH'inv⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.altH'inv_altH'⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.altH'_altH'inv⟧. *Step 3, $C$ and coherence.* $C = H'^{-1} \circ H$ maps $((e, \varphi, e'), w)$ to $((e, \varphi, e'), t(H(\ldots)))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.altC⟧. Coherence asks that for all pairs of members $C$ be a strong bundle isomorphism covering the identity of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.IsCoherent⟧. In §19.6 the structure (1) of Definition 19.6.1 is determined by the functor $\bar\Psi$. Here it is part of the member of $\tilde F$, which is why coherence is restated for the members.

**Proof of Definition 19.8.4.** *Step 1, the fields* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.PullbackTransitionConstruction⟧: a transition construction $\mathcal F$ for $F$; every class of $\mathcal B$ meets an object $\beta$ with $\hat F(\beta) \ne \emptyset$; $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ is metrizable; and coherence. *Step 2, (3)* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.PullbackTransitionConstruction.toCoveringBasicConstruction⟧: the pair $(\hat F, \mathcal F)$ is a basic construction for $P$ in the sense of Definition 19.7.11, since that definition asks only for a transition construction of the induced $F$. *Step 3* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_8_4⟧: the covering basic construction has uniformizer construction $\hat F$, its basic construction for $\mathcal B$ is $(F, \mathcal F)$, and it has uniformizers on every class and is coherent; all of this holds by definition or is a field.

**Proof of the note after Definition 19.8.4, as printed.** ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · definition_19_8_4_one_implies_three⟧ For every $\tilde F$ and every transition construction $\mathcal F$ of $F$: $(\hat F, \mathcal F)$ has uniformizer construction $\hat F$ and underlying $(F, \mathcal F)$ (by definition), and if every class meets an object $\beta$ with $F(\beta) \ne \emptyset$, then it meets one with $\hat F(\beta) \ne \emptyset$: a member $\Psi \in F(\beta)$ is by (19.24) the base of some $(\Psi, \hat\Psi) \in \hat F(\beta)$.

**Proof that the compatibility is not a consequence of (1).** *Step 1, the example* ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twistFt⟧: over the absolute-value covering $P$ of §19.7 and the reflection bundle $\mu_B$ (the witness of §19.6), $\tilde F(\beta) = \{\text{absPU}(\beta)\}$ for $\beta \ne 0$ and $\tilde F(0)$ adds the triple $(\Psi, \hat\Psi_{\mathrm{tw}}, \Gamma)$ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twistPU⟧, where $\hat\Psi_{\mathrm{tw}}$ is the twisted covering uniformizer of the examples after Proposition 19.7.15 and $\Gamma$ is the strong bundle uniformizer of absPU$(0)$. The induced $\hat F$ is that of the twisted example ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twistFt_hatU⟧. *Step 2, (1) and (3)* ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · definition_19_8_4_one_without_compatible⟧: the keyed slice transition construction ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twistFt𝓕⟧ makes $(\hat F, \mathcal F)$ the twisted basic construction ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twistFt_toCBC⟧, which has uniformizers on every class and a metrizable $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twistBasic_metrizable⟧, its natural topology being that of the absolute-value example. It is not compatible ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · not_isCompatible_twistBasic⟧. *Step 3, (2)* ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twistFt_isCoherent⟧: coherence of one pair depends only on the basic construction of $\mathcal B$, the uniformizers $\Psi$, $\Psi'$ and the strong bundle data ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · cohPair_congr⟧. Every member of the example has the $\Psi$ and the strong bundle data of absPU$(\beta)$ ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twistFt_member_data⟧, and its basic construction for $\mathcal B$ is that of the absolute-value pull-back ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twistFt_toBasic_eq⟧. So coherence transfers from that pull-back (§XIX.36). This gives a transition construction in the full sense of Definition 19.8.4 ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twistFtTransition⟧ and a basic construction of Definition 19.8.5 ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · twistFtBasic⟧. *Step 4* ⟦Chapter19/CompatibilityExamples · definition_19_8_4_without_compatible⟧: its covering basic construction is the twisted one and is not compatible. So the compatibility hypothesis of Theorem 19.8.6 (5) for $|\mathcal A|$, Lemma 19.8.21 and Theorem 19.8.22 goes beyond Definitions 19.8.4 and 19.8.5.

**Proof of Definition 19.8.5.** ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_8_5⟧ The pair records $\tilde F$ and a transition construction for it; its covering basic construction has uniformizer construction $\hat F$, and it is coherent, by the fields.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* Condition (1) is split into its two parts: the uniformizers on every class (the standing reading of Theorem 19.2.2) and the metrizability. Coherence is restated for the members of $\tilde F$, because the strong bundle data are part of a member. The note "(1) implies (3)" holds as printed. Under the compatibility reading of Definition 19.7.11 it would need that reading as a further assumption, and the example shows it is not implied.

### XIX.35 Theorem 19.8.6 (new in the 2021 edition), p. 938: bundle covering pull-backs

**Statement.** *Theorem 19.8.6* (p. 938, new in the 2021 edition; explicit). Let $P$ be a finite-to-one covering functor, $\tilde F$ a uniformizer construction for $(P, \mu_B)$ and $(\Psi, \hat\Psi, \Gamma)$, $(\Psi', \hat\Psi', \Gamma')$ members of it. For every transition construction $\mathcal F$ of $F$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_8_6_one_four⟧:
- $\hat\Gamma$ is injective on objects, full and faithful;
- (1) $m : M(\Gamma, \Gamma') \to M(\Psi, \Psi')$ carries the strong bundle structure over the natural M-polyfold $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ for which $H$ is a strong bundle isomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.bundleG⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_altH⟧;
- (2) $p : M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi') \to M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is a surjective local sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.pBmap⟧;
- (3) $U : M(\hat\Gamma, \hat\Gamma') \to p^*M(\Gamma, \Gamma')$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.Umap⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.pullBundle⟧ is a bijection over $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$, and $\hat m$ carries a strong bundle structure for which $U$ is a strong bundle isomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_U⟧; it is the only such structure ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.bundleHat_unique⟧;
- (4) $m \circ \bar p = p \circ \hat m$, and $\bar p$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.pbarMap⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.mGmap_pbarMap⟧ is a surjective strong bundle map covering $p$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_pbar⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.surjective_pbar⟧ which is a local strong bundle isomorphism near every point ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isLocalStrongBundleIso_pbar⟧.

For a transition construction $T$ of Definition 19.8.4 and under the compatibility of Definition 19.7.11 (§XIX.24: every lifted target map $\hat t$ continuous, as l. 44572 and Definition 19.6.4 (A) suggest) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_8_6_hyp⟧, the same holds and in addition (5): the natural topologies of $|\mathcal B|$ and $|\mathcal A|$ are metrizable. The witness of the compatibility is the absolute-value covering ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic_isCompatible⟧, and for the pull-back ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · absFt_isCompatible⟧: non-trivial isotropy, a two-sheeted covering away from $0$ and a fibre action by $-1$ (§XIX.36). The compatibility enters only (5) for $|\mathcal A|$. For $|E_B|$, (5) is the metrizability of the topology of Definition 19.6.7 for $(\bar F, \mathcal F)$, for a coherent $\mathcal F$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.natTopEB⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.metrizableSpace_natTopEB⟧. (5) for $|P^*E_B|$ is not stated separately (point G.273). The structure maps of $M(\Gamma, \Gamma')$, which the theorem uses through §19.6, are proved for the members of $\tilde F$ with a coherent $\mathcal F$: the target map is a local strong bundle isomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isLocalStrongBundleIso_tgtGmap⟧, the inversion a strong bundle isomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_invG⟧, the unit a strong bundle map ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_unitG⟧, and the multiplication a strong bundle map on the strong bundle over the fibred product ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.mulBundleG⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_mulG⟧.

**Proof.** *Step 0, the M-polyfolds.* $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ has the natural M-polyfold structure of Theorem 19.5.1 for the basic construction $(F, \mathcal F)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.basePolyfold⟧. For a finite-to-one $P$, $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ is Hausdorff and paracompact ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · CoveringBasicConstruction.instT2SpaceMHatFact⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · CoveringBasicConstruction.instParacompactSpaceMHatFact⟧, and it carries the M-polyfold structure of Proposition 19.7.26 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.hatPoly⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.hatPolyfold⟧.

*Step 1, $\hat\Gamma$.* Injectivity on objects, fullness and faithfulness are Steps 4–6 of the construction of $\hat\Gamma$ (§XIX.33).

*Step 2, (1): the strong bundle $m$.* We read "natural strong bundle structure" as the structure of Theorem 19.6.13: $M(\Gamma, \Gamma')$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.MG⟧ is given the topology making $H$ a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.instTopologicalSpaceMG⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.altHHomeo⟧, under which it is Hausdorff and paracompact ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.instT2SpaceMG⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.instParacompactSpaceMG⟧. Since $m \circ H$ is the projection of the fibred product, $H$ maps fibres to fibres ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.fiberGEquiv⟧, and the fibres of $m$ get the linear structures making $H$ fibrewise linear ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.instModuleFiberG⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.fiberGLin⟧. The structure is the transport of the pull-back bundle $M(\Psi, \Psi')\,{}_s\!\times_Q W$ along $H$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.bundleG⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.transport⟧, and $H$ is a strong bundle isomorphism covering the identity by the transport lemma ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleIso_transport⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_altH⟧. Here $m$ is the map of (19.49) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.mGmap⟧.

*Step 3, (2).* $p$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.pBmap⟧ is a local sc-diffeomorphism for the structure of Proposition 19.7.26 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPolyfold · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_pB⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_pBmap⟧, and it is surjective by Proposition 19.7.14 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.proposition_19_7_14⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.surjective_pBmap⟧.

*Step 4, the maps $\hat m$, $\bar p$ and $U$.* $M(\hat\Gamma, \hat\Gamma')$ is the transition set of $\hat\Gamma$, $\hat\Gamma'$ in $P^*E_B$, whose elements are the triples $((\hat e, w), (\hat\varphi, \hat\Gamma(\hat e, w)), (\hat e', w'))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.MGHat⟧. Then:
- $\hat m$ forgets the $W$-components, giving $(\hat e, \hat\varphi, \hat e') \in M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.mHatMap⟧. Its source and target conditions come from those of the triple, through the action formula of $P^*E_B$.
- $\bar p$ forgets the $\hat E$-components, giving $(w, (P(\hat\varphi), A(w)), w') \in M(\Gamma, \Gamma')$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.pbarMap⟧, through the forgetful functor $P^*E_B \to E_B$ (§XIX.33).
- $m \circ \bar p = p \circ \hat m$ is an equality of transition triples: the sources agree because $(\hat e, w) \in \tau^*W$, the morphisms are both $P(\hat\varphi)$, and the targets agree likewise ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.mGmap_pbarMap⟧.
- $U = (\hat m, \bar p)$ maps into the fibred product $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi') \times_{M(\Psi, \Psi')} M(\Gamma, \Gamma')$ of (19.58) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.PB⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.Umap⟧.
