Its inverse ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.Uinv⟧ takes $(a, g)$ with $m(g) = p(a)$ to the triple with source $(s(a), s(g))$, morphism $(a_{\mathrm{mor}}, \hat\Gamma(s(a), s(g)))$ and target $(t(a), t(g))$. The pairs lie in $\tau^*W$ by the source and target components of $m(g) = p(a)$. The target condition of the triple is the action formula of $P^*E_B$ combined with three facts: the target condition of $a$; the first component of the morphism of $g$, which is $P(a_{\mathrm{mor}})$ by the middle component of $m(g) = p(a)$; and the target condition of $g$. The two composites are identities ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.Uinv_Umap⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.Umap_Uinv⟧: one way by the source condition of the triple, the other by the source condition of $g$.

*Step 5, (3).* The pull-back $p^*M(\Gamma, \Gamma')$ of the strong bundle of Step 2 along the sc-smooth $p$ (Proposition 2.6.8) is a strong bundle over $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.pullBundle⟧. $M(\hat\Gamma, \hat\Gamma')$ gets the topology making $U$ a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.instTopologicalSpaceMGHat⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.Uhomeo⟧. Since $U$ lies over the identity of $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$, its fibres get the linear structures making $U$ fibrewise linear ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.fiberHatEquiv⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.fiberHatLin⟧. This is the text's "fiber-wise linear isomorphism". The strong bundle structure of $\hat m$ is the transport of $p^*M(\Gamma, \Gamma')$ along $U^{-1}$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.bundleHat⟧, and $U$ is a strong bundle isomorphism covering the identity ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_U⟧. *Uniqueness* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.bundleHat_unique⟧: if $U$ is a strong bundle isomorphism for a second structure $B'$ as well, then the identity is a strong bundle isomorphism from $B'$ to the transported structure. This is the general fact that two strong bundle structures on the same projection are equal when one bijection is a strong bundle isomorphism from both to a third, applied with $U$ on all of $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleIso_id_of_isStrongBundleIsoOn⟧.

*Step 6, (4).*
- *A strong bundle map* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_pbar⟧: $\bar p$ is the projection $p^*M(\Gamma, \Gamma') \to M(\Gamma, \Gamma')$ composed with $U$. The projection is a strong bundle map covering $p$ ⟦Chapter5/StrongBundleMap · isStrongBundleMap_pullbackProj'⟧, and so is the composite.
- *Locally an isomorphism* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isLocalStrongBundleIso_pbar⟧: near $a$, $p$ is an sc-diffeomorphism of open sets. Over such a set the projection of the pull-back is a strong bundle isomorphism ⟦Chapter10/StrongBundleWeakFibredProductPullback · isStrongBundleIsoOn_pullbackProj'⟧, and composing with the global isomorphism $U$ gives the local isomorphism.
- *Surjective* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.surjective_pbar⟧: for $g \in M(\Gamma, \Gamma')$ choose $a$ with $p(a) = m(g)$ by (2); then $\bar p(U^{-1}(a, g)) = g$.

*Step 7, assembling (1)–(4)* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_8_6_one_four⟧: Steps 1–6 use only that $\mathcal F$ is a transition construction of $F$ and that $P$ is finite-to-one. Conditions (1) and (2) of Definition 19.8.4 are not used.

*Step 8, (5)* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_8_6_hyp⟧.
- $|\mathcal B|$ is metrizable by Definition 19.8.4 (1).
- $|\mathcal A|$ is metrizable by Theorem 19.7.25 (1) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringMetrizability · CoveringBasicConstruction.theorem_19_7_25_one_hyp⟧, applied to $(\hat F, \mathcal F)$ with the compatibility reading, the uniformizers on every class and the metrizability of $|\mathcal B|$.
- $|E_B|$: the topology of Definition 19.6.7 for the strong bundle uniformizers $\bar F$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.NatOpenEB⟧ is a topology for a coherent $\mathcal F$ with uniformizers on every class ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.natTopEB⟧; coherence enters the finite intersections. It is metrizable ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.metrizableSpace_natTopEB⟧ by the metrization criterion through a map ⟦Chapter2/Metrizable · metrizableSpace_of_pullback⟧. The criterion needs that $|P_B|$ is continuous and that every point of the paracompact $|\mathcal B|$ lies in an open set $|\Psi(O)|$ (Theorem 19.2.2 (2)) whose preimage is $|\Gamma(W)|$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · StrongBundleStr.orbitProj_preimage_range⟧, which is metrizable.

*Step 9, the structure maps of $M(\Gamma, \Gamma')$.* These are Theorem 19.6.13 for the members of $\tilde F$; they enter Theorem 19.8.22.
- *Target* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.tgtGmap_eq⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_tgtGmap⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isLocalStrongBundleIso_tgtGmap⟧: $t = \mathrm{pr} \circ C \circ H^{-1}$, with $\mathrm{pr}$ the projection of $M(\Psi, \Psi')\,{}_t\!\times_{Q'} W'$ to $W'$. Coherence makes $C$ a strong bundle isomorphism, and $\mathrm{pr}$ is a local strong bundle isomorphism over the local sc-diffeomorphism $t$ of $M(\Psi, \Psi')$.
- *Source* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_srcGmap⟧: $s = \mathrm{pr} \circ H^{-1}$.
- *Inversion* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.invG_eq⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_invG⟧: $\iota$ is $H$ composed with the exchange map $A$ of (19.18) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_altA⟧, with $C$ and with $H^{-1}$. It is a strong bundle map in both directions and an involution.
- *Unit* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.unitG_eq⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_unitG⟧: $u = H \circ (u \circ Q, \mathrm{id})$, a strong bundle map by the pairing lemma for pull-backs ⟦Chapter10/StrongBundleWeakFibredProductPullback · isStrongBundleMap_pullbackPair⟧. No coherence is needed here.
- *Multiplication* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.mulG⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_mulG⟧: the total space over $M(\Psi', \Psi'')\,{}_s\!\times_t M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is the pull-back of $m$ along the second projection ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.mulBundleG⟧. On it the multiplication is $H \circ (m \circ \pi, s \circ \mathrm{pr}_2)$: the lift through $t(h)$ of the first base factor, composed with $h$. Equality with $H(\ldots)$ follows from the compatibility of the projection with composition ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleUniformizer · StrongBundleStr.proj_comp⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* (1)–(4) hold for every transition construction of $F$, without (1)–(3) of Definition 19.8.4. The strong bundle structures on $m$ and $\hat m$ are those of the text's description ($H$, respectively $U$, a strong bundle isomorphism), with the topologies and the fibrewise linear structures carried along $H$ and $U$; uniqueness in (3) is proved. Coherence enters only the structure maps of $M(\Gamma, \Gamma')$ and the topology of $|E_B|$. (5) for $|\mathcal A|$ uses the compatibility reading of Definition 19.7.11, which Definitions 19.8.4 and 19.8.5 do not imply (§XIX.34). (5) for $|P^*E_B|$ is not stated separately (G.273).

### XIX.36 The witness: the absolute value with a fibre

**Statement.** *The absolute value with a fibre* (no counterpart in the text; extra) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · absFt⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · absFtTransition⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · absFtBasic⟧: the absolute-value covering $P : \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes (\{\pm 1\} \times \mathbb R) \to \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$ of §19.7 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absCovP⟧, with $|\mathcal A| \cong \mathbb R$, $|\mathcal B| \cong [0, \infty)$ and $|P|$ the absolute value, pulled back with the reflection bundle $\mu_B$ of §19.6 over $\mathcal B$, on which the generator acts on the fibre by $-1$. It has a transition construction in the full sense of Definition 19.8.4, coherent ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · absFt_isCoherent⟧ and compatible ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · absFt_isCompatible⟧. Theorem 19.8.6 holds for it in full ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · absFt_theorem_19_8_6_full⟧, with $|E_B|$ metrizable ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopologyExamples · absFt_metrizableSpace_natTopEB⟧. It is non-degenerate: the isotropy at $0$ is non-trivial and acts on the fibre by $-1$, the covering is two-sheeted away from $0$, and $C$ is not the identity ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · absFt_coherent_nondegenerate⟧.

**Proof.** *Step 1, the slice bundles.* Over a slice $H \ltimes V$ of $\mathcal B$, the strong bundle data are the trivial bundle $W_V = V \times_{\mathbb R} (\mathbb R \times \mathbb R) \to V$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · SliceW⟧ with $h \ast (v, a) = (hv, ha)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · SliceW.smul⟧ and $\Gamma(v, a) = a$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · sliceBunFunctor⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · sliceBun⟧. On $V = \mathbb R$ the element $-1$ acts on the fibre by $-1$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · sliceBun_smul_neg⟧. *Step 2, the members.* Over each slice covering uniformizer of §19.7 this gives a member of Definition 19.8.1 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · slicePU⟧. The member absPU$(\beta)$ is taken over the slice $\mathbb R$ with group $\mathbb Z/2$ at $\beta = 0$, and over the positive or negative half-line with trivial group otherwise ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · absPU⟧. Its covering part is the member of the absolute-value construction ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · absPU_Ψh⟧, so $\tilde F(\beta) = \{\text{absPU}(\beta)\}$ induces the $\hat F$ of §19.7 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · absFt_hatU⟧. *Step 3, the transition construction.* The keyed slice transition construction $F_h(q) = (q, g, gq)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · absFt𝓕⟧ makes $(\hat F, \mathcal F)$ the absolute-value basic construction ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · absFt_toCBC⟧. That construction has uniformizers on every class, a metrizable $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ and the compatibility ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · absBasic_conditions⟧; the metrizability of $T_{|\mathcal B|} \cong [0, \infty)$ is part of the statement about Theorem 19.7.25 (4b) for this example ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · not_theorem_19_7_25_four_b⟧. A first form of Theorem 19.8.6 (1)–(4), without coherence, follows at once ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · exists_absFt_transition⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · absFt_theorem_19_8_6⟧.

*Step 4, coherence* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · absFt_isCoherent⟧. For slice members the map $H$ of (19.16) is $H(k, (v, a)) = (v, (\varphi, a), (t(k), ga))$ for $k = (o, g, go)$, so $C(k, (v, a)) = (k, (t(k), g a))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · altC_absFt_eq⟧. The group element $g$ of $k$ is a continuous, that is locally constant, function on $M(\Psi, \Psi')$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · continuous_absFt_key⟧, and it satisfies $g \cdot s(k) = t(k)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · key_smul_src⟧. A general lemma covers this situation: for sc-smooth $f_1, f_2$ and a continuous $c$ into $\mathbb Z^\times$ with $c(x) f_1(x) = f_2(x)$, the map $(x, (v, a)) \mapsto (x, (f_2(x), c(x) a))$ between the pull-backs of slice bundles is a strong bundle map covering the identity ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · sliceFibreMap⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · isStrongBundleMap_sliceFibreMap⟧. It applies to $C$ and to its inverse $(k, (t(k), a')) \mapsto (k, (s(k), g^{-1} a'))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · altCinvAbs⟧, so $C$ is a strong bundle isomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · isStrongBundleIso_altC_absFt⟧. Every member is a slice member ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · absPU_eq⟧, which gives coherence.

*Step 5, the definitions and the theorem.*
- Definition 19.8.4 holds: the uniformizers on every class and the metrizability come from Step 3, and coherence from Step 4 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · absFtTransition⟧. Definition 19.8.5 follows ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · absFtBasic⟧.
- The compatibility is that of the absolute-value construction ⟦Chapter19/CoveringCounterexamples · absBasic_isCompatible⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · absFt_isCompatible⟧.
- Theorem 19.8.6 with (5) for $|\mathcal A|$ and $|\mathcal B|$ is the general theorem applied to this construction ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · absFt_theorem_19_8_6_full⟧. $|E_B|$ is metrizable and $|P_B|$ is continuous ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopologyExamples · absFt_metrizableSpace_natTopEB⟧.

*Step 6, non-degeneracy* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · absFt_coherent_nondegenerate⟧. Take the member at $\beta = 0$ and $k = (0, -1, 0)$: then $C$ maps $(k, (0, (0, 1)))$ to $(k, (0, (0, -1)))$, so $C$ is not the identity on the fibre. On the pulled-back functor $\mu_A = \mu_B \circ P$, the morphism $(-1, (1, 0))$ maps the fibre over $(1, 0)$ to the fibre over $(-1, 0)$ by $-1$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · reflAbsSquare_act_neg⟧.

### XIX.37 Definitions 19.8.7, 19.8.8, Remark 19.8.9, Definition 19.8.10, Remark 19.8.11, Lemma 19.8.12 (2017: Definitions 19.20–19.22), pp. 941–946

**Statement.** *Definition 19.8.7* (p. 941, 2017: Definition 19.20; same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · GroupoidalCategory.BundleCoveringSquare⟧: a bundle covering square $\mathfrak E$ consists of:
- a finite-to-one covering functor $P : \mathcal A \to \mathcal B$ (Definition 19.7.1);
- functors $\mu_A : \mathcal A \to \mathrm{Ban}$ and $\mu_B : \mathcal B \to \mathrm{Ban}$;
- a natural isomorphism $\Theta : \mu_A \to \mu_B \circ P$, that is, topological linear isomorphisms $\Theta_\alpha : \mu_A(\alpha) \to \mu_B(P(\alpha))$ with $\Theta_{\alpha'} \circ \mu_A(\hat\varphi) = \mu_B(P(\hat\varphi)) \circ \Theta_\alpha$ for $\hat\varphi : \alpha \to \alpha'$ (19.64).

It gives the bundle categories $E_A$, $E_B$, the projections $P_A$, $P_B$ and the functor $\hat P : E_A \to E_B$ of (19.69) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · BundleCoveringSquare.Phat⟧, and the square (19.70), (19.71) commutes ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · BundleCoveringSquare.definition_19_8_7⟧. The pull-back $(P, \mu_B)$ of Definition 19.8.1 is the case $\mu_A = \mu_B \circ P$, $\Theta = \mathrm{id}$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · BundleCoveringSquare.ofPull⟧. The witness is the absolute-value covering with the reflection bundle ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · reflAbsSquare⟧, on which $\mu_A$ acts on the fibre by $-1$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · reflAbsSquare_act_neg⟧.

*Definition 19.8.8* (p. 943, 2017: Definition 19.21; restated) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · GroupoidalCategory.StrongEpBundleCoveringModel⟧: a strong ep-bundle covering model $K$ (19.72) over a proper covering model $\hat\tau : \hat E \to G \ltimes O_\beta$ (Definition 17.8.4, point $o_\beta$) consists of:
- strong bundles $Q : W \to G \ltimes O_\beta$ and $\hat Q : \hat W \to \hat E$ over the ep-groupoids, in the sense of Definition 8.3.1;
- a proper strong bundle covering $\Xi : \hat W \to W$ over $\hat\tau$ in the sense of Definition 8.5.9 ⟦Chapter8/Book · IsProperStrongBundleCovering2021⟧.

Then (19.75) $\Xi(\hat\mu(\hat\sigma, \hat w)) = \mu(\hat\tau(\hat\sigma), \Xi(\hat w))$ holds ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.xi_mu⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.definition_19_8_8⟧. We read the covering model of the base as one of Definition 17.8.4 throughout, as in Definition 19.8.1; the text cites Definition 8.5.1 with $E = G \ltimes O_\beta$. For a uniformizer of a pull-back, $\hat\tau^* W$ is a model ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · PullbackUniformizer.coveringModel⟧.

*Remark 19.8.9* (p. 943, new in the 2021 edition; restated) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.remark_19_8_9⟧: a model is a proper strong bundle covering whose base groupoid has the special form $G \ltimes O_\beta$.

*Definition 19.8.10* (p. 943, 2017: Definition 19.22; same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · GroupoidalCategory.SquareUniformizer⟧: a uniformizer $\hat\Gamma : K \to \mathfrak E$ at $\beta$ is a model $K$ over the proper covering model of $(\Psi, \hat\Psi)$, together with functors $\Psi : G \ltimes O \to \mathcal B$, $\hat\Psi : \hat E \to \mathcal A$, $\Gamma : G \ltimes W \to E_B$ and $\hat\Gamma : \hat W \to E_A$ (19.76), such that:
- (1) $(\Psi, \hat\Psi)$ is a uniformizer for $P$ at $\beta$ (Definition 19.7.3);
- (2) $(\Gamma, \Psi)$ is a strong bundle uniformizer for $P_B$ (Definition 19.6.1) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · SquareUniformizer.toStrongBundleOver⟧, so that $(\Psi, \hat\Psi, \Gamma)$ is a uniformizer for $(P, \mu_B)$ (§XIX.33) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · SquareUniformizer.toPullbackUniformizer⟧;
- (3) $(\hat\Gamma, \hat\Psi)$ is a strong bundle uniformizer for $P_A$ over the ep-groupoid $\hat E$, in the variant of Remark 19.6.2 (point G.269);
- (19.77) $\hat P \circ \hat\Gamma = \Gamma \circ \Xi$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · SquareUniformizer.definition_19_8_10⟧.

The relations (19.82), (19.83) follow ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · SquareUniformizer.relations_19_82⟧. The zero sections correspond, $\hat\Gamma(0_{\hat e}) = (\hat\Psi(\hat e), 0)$ and $\Gamma(0_o) = (\Psi(o), 0)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizer.remark_19_6_2⟧; this is the consequence of Remark 19.6.2 for (2) and (3). A uniformizer of a pull-back gives one ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · PullbackUniformizer.toSquare⟧. The witness at $\beta = 0$ is non-degenerate ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSquareU_nondegenerate⟧.

*Remark 19.8.11* (p. 944, new in the 2021 edition; nothing): it points to applications to stable maps, and there is nothing to formalize (point G.274).

*Lemma 19.8.12* (p. 946, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · SquareUniformizer.lemma_19_8_12⟧: $\hat\Gamma$ determines $\hat\Psi$, $\Psi$ and $\Gamma$, and these determine $\hat\Gamma$ by (19.84), with (19.85) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · SquareUniformizer.formula_19_84⟧.

**Proof of Definition 19.8.7.**
- *Step 1, $\hat P$.* On objects $\hat P(\alpha, \hat v) = (P(\alpha), \Theta_\alpha(\hat v))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · BundleCoveringSquare.ThetaE⟧. On morphisms $\hat P(\hat\varphi, \hat v) = (P(\hat\varphi), \Theta_{s(\hat\varphi)}(\hat v))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · BundleCoveringSquare.PhatMor⟧. The target of the image is $\Theta$ of the target ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · BundleCoveringSquare.act_PhatMor⟧: this is (19.64). Composition and units come from those of $P$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · BundleCoveringSquare.Phat⟧.
- *Step 2, the square.* $P_B \circ \hat P = P \circ P_A$ on objects, since $\Theta_\alpha$ lands in the fibre over $P(\alpha)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · BundleCoveringSquare.P_ThetaE⟧, and on morphisms by definition. On the fibre over $\alpha$, $\hat P$ is $\Theta_\alpha$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · BundleCoveringSquare.ThetaE_eq⟧, so it is injective on each fibre ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · BundleCoveringSquare.ThetaE_injective_fiber⟧.
- *Step 3, the pull-back.* $\mu_A = \mu_B \circ P$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · BanFunctor.pull⟧. $\Theta_\alpha$ is the identification of the fibres ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · BanFunctor.pullFiberCLE⟧, and (19.64) is the action formula of $P^*E_B$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackDiagram · BanFunctor.pull_act⟧.

**Proof of Definition 19.8.8 and Remark 19.8.9.**
- *Step 1, the action groupoid* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · GroupoidalCategory.epAction⟧. A strong bundle $(W, \mu)$ over an ep-groupoid $X$ defines the action groupoid with morphisms $(\sigma, w)$, $s(\sigma) = Q(w)$, with source $w$ and target $\mu(\sigma, w)$.
- *Step 2, the action of $G$ on $W$.* Put $g \ast w = \mu((g, Q(w)), w)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.smul⟧. It covers the action on $O$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.Q_smul⟧ and is a group action ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.instMulAction⟧. It acts by strong bundle maps ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.isStrongBundleMap_smul⟧: $w \mapsto g \ast w$ is $\mu$ composed with the strong bundle map $w \mapsto ((g, Q(w)), w)$ into the pull-back.
- *Step 3, $\Xi$ as a functor* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.xiFunctor⟧: $(\hat\sigma, \hat w) \mapsto (\hat\tau(\hat\sigma), \Xi(\hat w))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.xiMor⟧. Its compatibility with targets is the compatibility of $\Xi$ with $\hat\mu$ and $\mu$ in Definition 8.5.9 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.xi_mu'⟧, which is (19.75).
- *Step 4, the definition* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.definition_19_8_8⟧. Definition 8.5.9 gives:
  - $Q \circ \Xi = \hat\tau \circ \hat Q$;
  - $\Xi$ is surjective;
  - $\hat\tau$ is a proper covering functor in the sense of Definition 8.5.1.

  Remark 19.8.9 records that the base groupoid is the translation groupoid ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.remark_19_8_9⟧.
- *Step 5, the model of a pull-back* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · PullbackUniformizer.coveringModel⟧. Let $(\Psi, \hat\Psi, \Gamma)$ be a uniformizer for $(P, \mu_B)$ with $P$ finite-to-one.
  - $W$ is a strong bundle over $G \ltimes O$ with $\mu((g, o), w) = g \ast w$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · StrongBundleOver.epBundle⟧.
  - $\hat\tau$ is a proper covering functor, since its fibres embed into those of $P$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · PullbackUniformizer.isProperCovering_τ⟧.
  - $\hat W = \hat\tau^* W$ with $\Xi(\hat e, w) = w$ is a proper strong bundle covering ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · PullbackUniformizer.isProperStrongBundleCovering2021_tauW⟧: near every point $\Xi$ is the projection of the pull-back along the local sc-diffeomorphism $\hat\tau$, a strong bundle isomorphism.

**Proof of Definition 19.8.10.**
- *Step 1, (2)* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · SquareUniformizer.toStrongBundleOver⟧: the data of $\Gamma$ are those of Definition 19.6.1, with the action of Step 2 above.
- *Step 2, morphisms.* $\Gamma(g, w) = (\Psi(g, Q(w)), \Gamma(w))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · SquareUniformizer.Γ_mor_val⟧ and $\hat\Gamma(\hat\sigma, \hat w) = (\hat\Psi(\hat\sigma), \hat\Gamma(\hat w))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · SquareUniformizer.Γh_mor_val⟧. The first components are (19.79) and (19.81), the second ones the source conditions.
- *Step 3, (19.77) on morphisms* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · SquareUniformizer.comm_mor⟧. It follows from four facts:
  - Step 2;
  - (19.77) on objects;
  - $P \circ \hat\Psi = \Psi \circ \hat\tau$ on morphisms;
  - $Q \circ \Xi = \hat\tau \circ \hat Q$.
- *Step 4, (19.82), (19.83)* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · SquareUniformizer.relations_19_82⟧. $P \circ \hat\Psi = \Psi \circ \hat\tau$ on objects and morphisms is (1). $\Theta_{\hat\Psi(\hat e)} \circ \hat A = A \circ \Xi$ is (19.77) on objects, read in the fibres. $Q \circ \Xi = \hat\tau \circ \hat Q$ is part of Definition 8.5.9.
- *Step 5, the zero sections* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizer.remark_19_6_2⟧: $\hat A$ and $A$ are linear on the fibres, so they map $0$ to $0$.
- *Step 6, the uniformizer of a pull-back* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · PullbackUniformizer.toSquare⟧. Take the model of Step 5 above and $\hat\Gamma(\hat e, w) = (\hat\Psi(\hat e), \Gamma(w))$ of (19.55) (§XIX.33). Then $\hat A$ is $A$ on the fibre of $W$ over $\hat\tau(\hat e)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · PullbackUniformizer.fiberCLEh⟧.
- *Step 7, the witness* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSquareU⟧. At each $\beta$, the member of §XIX.36 gives a uniformizer ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSquareU_toPullbackUniformizer⟧. Over $\beta = 0$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSquareU_zero⟧, $\Xi$ is two-to-one, and $-1 \in G$ acts on $W$ by $(v, a) \mapsto (-v, -a)$, non-trivially on the fibre over $0$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSquareU_nondegenerate⟧.

**Proof of Lemma 19.8.12.**
- *Step 1, $\hat\Psi$.* $\hat\Psi(\hat e) = P_A(\hat\Gamma(0_{\hat e}))$ by (19.80) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · SquareUniformizer.zeroH⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · SquareUniformizer.Qh_zeroH⟧. On morphisms, (19.81) at $(\hat\sigma, 0_{s(\hat\sigma)})$ gives $\hat\Psi(\hat\sigma)$.
- *Step 2, $\Psi$.* Every functor $\Psi'$ with $P \circ \hat\Psi = \Psi' \circ \hat\tau$ is $\Psi$, by Lemma 19.7.5 and Remark 19.7.6 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.remark_19_7_6⟧.
- *Step 3, $\Gamma$.* $\Gamma(\Xi(\hat w)) = \hat P(\hat\Gamma(\hat w))$ by (19.77), $\Xi$ is surjective, and $\Gamma$ on morphisms is given by Step 2 of the previous proof.
- *Step 4, (19.84).* $\hat\Gamma(\hat w)$ lies over $\hat\Psi(\hat Q(\hat w))$ by (19.80), and $\Theta$ maps it to $\Gamma(\Xi(\hat w))$ by (19.77). Hence $\hat\Gamma(\hat w) = \Theta_{\hat\Psi(\hat Q(\hat w))}^{-1}(\Gamma(\Xi(\hat w)))$. On morphisms $\hat\Gamma$ is given by Step 2 of the previous proof. (19.85), $P_B(\hat P(\hat\Gamma(\hat w))) = \Psi(\hat\tau(\hat Q(\hat w)))$, follows from (19.80) and (1) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · SquareUniformizer.formula_19_84⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* In Definition 19.8.7, naturality is stated on the fibres, as (19.64). In Definition 19.8.8, the base model is one of Definition 17.8.4; Definition 8.5.9 is used as printed for $\Xi$, and it contains that $\hat\tau$ is a proper covering functor in the sense of Definition 8.5.1. In Definition 19.8.10 (2), the action of $G$ on $W$ is the one given by $\mu$. Part (3) is read as Definition 19.6.1 over the base ep-groupoid $\hat E$ in place of $G \ltimes O$ (Remark 19.6.2): $\hat\Gamma$ is a fully faithful functor from the action groupoid of $\hat W$, injective on objects, over $\hat\Psi$, and a topological linear isomorphism on the fibres. This variant is the form Definition 19.8.10 uses and is not built separately (point G.269). In Lemma 19.8.12, "determines" is read as the formulas of Steps 1–4.

### XIX.38 Definitions 19.8.13–19.8.15 (2017: Definitions 19.23, 19.24), pp. 948–949

**Statement.** *Definition 19.8.13* (p. 948, 2017: Definition 19.23; restated) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · GroupoidalCategory.SquareUniformizerConstruction⟧: a uniformizer construction $\tilde F$ for $\mathfrak E$ assigns to every object $\beta$ of $\mathcal B$ a set $\tilde F(\beta)$ of uniformizers at $\beta$. The definition refers to itself for the notion of uniformizer; we read this as a reference to Definition 19.8.10. The triples $(\Psi, \hat\Psi, \Gamma)$ of the members form a uniformizer construction for $(P, \mu_B)$ in the sense of Definition 19.8.2 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.toPullback⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_8_13⟧. The witness ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSqFt⟧ has the underlying construction of §XIX.36 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSqFt_toPullback⟧.

*Definition 19.8.14* (p. 948, 2017: Definition 19.24; same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.CompatibleTransitionConstruction⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_8_14⟧: a compatible transition construction is a transition construction $\mathcal F$ for the underlying $F$ such that:
- (1) $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ is metrizable, under the standing reading that every class of $\mathcal B$ has a representative $\beta$ with $F(\beta) \ne \emptyset$;
- (2) $(\bar F, \mathcal F)$ is coherent;
- (3) $(\hat F, \mathcal F)$ is a basic construction for $P$.

This is Definition 19.8.4 for the underlying construction (§XIX.34). The witness is ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSqTransition⟧.

*Definition 19.8.15* (p. 949, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · GroupoidalCategory.SquareBasicConstruction⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · GroupoidalCategory.definition_19_8_15⟧: a basic construction for $\mathfrak E$ is a pair $(\tilde F, \mathcal F)$ of a uniformizer construction and a compatible transition construction. Its underlying basic construction for $(P, \mu_B)$ is that of Definition 19.8.5 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareBasicConstruction.toPullbackBasic⟧. The witness is ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSqBasic⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSqBasic_definition_19_8_15⟧. As for Definition 19.8.4, the compatibility of Definition 19.7.11 (§XIX.24) is not part of the definition. The statements that need it carry it as a hypothesis, and Definitions 19.8.4 and 19.8.5 do not imply it (§XIX.34).

**Proof.**
- *Step 1, the members* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.Unif⟧. A member is $\beta$ with $\hat\Gamma \in \tilde F(\beta)$, and its triple is a member of the underlying construction ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.Unif.toPU⟧. The base object of each member is $\beta$ by Definition 19.8.10 (1), and the triple lies in the set of the underlying construction at $\beta$ by construction ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_8_13⟧.
- *Step 2, Definition 19.8.14* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.definition_19_8_14⟧. The fields give four facts:
  - every class of $\mathcal B$ has a representative with uniformizers;
  - $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ is metrizable;
  - $(\bar F, \mathcal F)$ is coherent;
  - the uniformizers $\hat F$ of the basic construction for $P$ are those of the underlying construction.
- *Step 3, Definition 19.8.15* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · GroupoidalCategory.definition_19_8_15⟧: the underlying basic construction of Definition 19.8.5 has the underlying uniformizer construction, and its transition construction is coherent.
- *Step 4, the witnesses.*
  - $\tilde F(\beta) = \{\hat\Gamma_\beta\}$, with $\hat\Gamma_\beta$ the uniformizer of §XIX.37, Step 7 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSqFt⟧.
  - The transition construction is that of §XIX.36, transported along the equality of the underlying constructions ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSqTransition⟧. It satisfies Definition 19.8.4, coherence included.
  - The basic construction ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSqBasic⟧ contains the non-degenerate member at $0$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSqBasic_definition_19_8_15⟧.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The self-reference in Definition 19.8.13 is read as Definition 19.8.10. Conditions (1)–(3) of Definition 19.8.14 are read as in Definition 19.8.4 (§XIX.34). Condition (1) includes the standing reading of the classes.

### XIX.39 Lemma 19.8.16, Definition 19.8.17, Lemma 19.8.18, Definition 19.8.19 (new in the 2021 edition), pp. 951–954

**Statement.** Let $\hat\Gamma$, $\hat\Gamma'$ be members of $\tilde F$ and $\mathcal F$ a transition construction of the underlying $F$. *Lemma 19.8.16* (p. 951, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.lemma_19_8_16⟧: consider the following data.
- The transition set $M(\hat\Gamma, \hat\Gamma')$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.MSq⟧ consists of the triples $(\hat w, (\hat\varphi, \hat A(\hat w)), \hat w')$ with $\mu_A(\hat\varphi)(\hat A(\hat w)) = \hat A'(\hat w')$.
- $\hat m(\hat w, \ldots, \hat w') = (\hat Q(\hat w), \hat\varphi, \hat Q'(\hat w'))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.mSq⟧.
- (19.87) $\bar p(\hat w, \ldots, \hat w') = (\Xi(\hat w), (P(\hat\varphi), A(\Xi(\hat w))), \Xi'(\hat w'))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.pbarSq⟧.

The diagram (19.86) $m \circ \bar p = p \circ \hat m$ commutes ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.mGmap_pbarSq⟧. The map $U = (\hat m, \bar p) : M(\hat\Gamma, \hat\Gamma') \to p^*M(\Gamma, \Gamma')$ of (19.90) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.USq⟧ is a bijection ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.USqInv⟧ covering the identity of $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ (19.89). On the fibre over $(\hat e, \hat\varphi, \hat e')$ it is $\hat w \mapsto \Xi(\hat w)$, a topological linear isomorphism $\hat Q^{-1}(\hat e) \to Q^{-1}(\hat\tau(\hat e))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.fiberCLEXi⟧. The argument uses that $\langle \hat Q, \Xi\rangle : \hat W \to \hat\tau^* W$ is a strong bundle isomorphism over $\hat E$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.isStrongBundleIso_liftK⟧.

*Definition 19.8.17* (p. 952, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.bundleSq⟧: $\hat m$ carries the strong bundle structure for which $U$ is a strong bundle isomorphism covering the identity ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_USq⟧. It is the only such structure ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.bundleSq_unique⟧.

*Lemma 19.8.18* (p. 953, new in the 2021 edition; same) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.lemma_19_8_18⟧: define the map $S : p^*M(\Gamma, \Gamma') \to \hat W$ of (19.93) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.SMap⟧. It sends $((\hat e, \hat\varphi, \hat e'), (w, \ldots, w'))$ to the point $\hat w$ over $\hat e$ with $\Xi(\hat w) = w$. Then:
- $S$ is a local strong bundle isomorphism covering $\hat s : M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi') \to \hat E$;
- $\hat Q \circ S = \hat s \circ \mathrm{pr}_1$ and $\Xi \circ S = s \circ \mathrm{pr}_2$.

Hence the source map $s : M(\hat\Gamma, \hat\Gamma') \to \hat W$ is a local strong bundle isomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isLocalStrongBundleIso_srcSq⟧.

*Definition 19.8.19* (p. 954, new in the 2021 edition; restated) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · IsProperStrongBundleCoveringSquare⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · IsProperStrongBundleCoveringSquareBook⟧: a proper strong bundle covering square consists of:
- strong bundles $\hat W \to \hat X$ and $W \to X$;
- a surjective local sc-diffeomorphism $a : \hat X \to X$ with finite fibres $a^{-1}(x) = \{\hat x_1, \ldots, \hat x_k\}$;
- a strong bundle map $\hat a$ covering $a$ such that every $x$ has open neighbourhoods $U(x)$, $U(\hat x_i)$ with $\hat a : \hat W|U(\hat x_i) \to W|U(x)$ a strong bundle isomorphism.

We read "proper" as also requiring $a^{-1}(U(x)) = \bigsqcup_i U(\hat x_i)$, with mutually disjoint $U(\hat x_i)$, as for proper coverings in Definition 17.8.1. The printed form follows from it ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · IsProperStrongBundleCoveringSquare.toBook⟧. Every model is a proper square ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.isProperSquare⟧; witness ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSquareU_isProperSquare⟧.

**Proof that $\langle \hat Q, \Xi\rangle$ is a strong bundle isomorphism.**
- *Step 1, $\Xi$ on the fibres.* $\Xi$ is a strong bundle map over $\hat\tau$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.isStrongBundleMap_Ξ⟧, and near every point a strong bundle isomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.exists_isStrongBundleIsoOn_Ξ⟧. So it is a topological linear isomorphism on every fibre ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.fiberCLEXi⟧.
- *Step 2, the lift.* For $Q(w) = \hat\tau(\hat e)$ there is exactly one $\hat w$ over $\hat e$ with $\Xi(\hat w) = w$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.liftPt⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.liftPt_eq⟧. In particular $\hat w$ is determined by $\hat Q(\hat w)$ and $\Xi(\hat w)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.eq_of_Qh_Ξ⟧.
- *Step 3, the isomorphism.* $\langle \hat Q, \Xi\rangle$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.liftK⟧ has the inverse $(\hat e, w) \mapsto \hat w$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.liftKinv⟧. It is a strong bundle map over the identity by the pairing lemma for pull-backs ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.isStrongBundleMap_liftK⟧. Near $\hat e$, its composite with the projection $\hat\tau^* W \to W$ is $\Xi$, a local strong bundle isomorphism, and the projection is one over the local sc-diffeomorphism $\hat\tau$. So $\langle \hat Q, \Xi\rangle$ is one near $\hat e$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.isStrongBundleIsoOn_liftK⟧, over the same set, since it covers the identity ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · StrongBundle.IsStrongBundleIsoOn.target_eq_of_id⟧. A bijection over the identity that is everywhere locally a strong bundle isomorphism is one ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTransport · isStrongBundleIsoOn_univ_of_local⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleIso_of_isStrongBundleIsoOn_univ⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.isStrongBundleIso_liftK⟧.

**Proof of Lemma 19.8.16.**
- *Step 1, the maps.* $\hat m$ and $\bar p$ land in the transition sets. (19.86) holds because $Q \circ \Xi = \hat\tau \circ \hat Q$ and both middle components are $P(\hat\varphi)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.mGmap_pbarSq⟧.
- *Step 2, the inverse* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.USqInv⟧. For $(a, g)$ with $m(g) = p(a)$, let $\hat w$ and $\hat w'$ be the lifts of $s(g)$ and $t(g)$ over $s(a)$ and $t(a)$ (Step 2 above). Take the morphism $(a_{\mathrm{mor}}, \hat\Gamma(\hat w))$. The triple lies in $M(\hat\Gamma, \hat\Gamma')$ by (19.92). $\Theta$ is injective on the fibres ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · BundleCoveringSquare.ThetaE_injective_fiber⟧, so it suffices to compare the images under $\hat P$. By (19.64) and (19.77) these are the two sides of the target condition of $g$.
- *Step 3, bijectivity.* The two composites are identities by the uniqueness of the lifts ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.USqInv_USq⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.USq_USqInv⟧. $U$ covers the identity of $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$, and on the first coordinate $\bar p$ is $\Xi$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.USq_src⟧. So on each fibre $U$ is the topological linear isomorphism of Step 1 above ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.lemma_19_8_16⟧.

**Proof of Definition 19.8.17.** The construction is that of Theorem 19.8.6 (3) (§XIX.35).
- $M(\hat\Gamma, \hat\Gamma')$ gets the topology making $U$ a homeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.instTopologicalSpaceMSq⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.USqHomeo⟧. It is Hausdorff and paracompact ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.instT2SpaceMSq⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.instParacompactSpaceMSq⟧.
- The fibres of $\hat m$ are those of the pull-back ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.fiberSqEquiv⟧, with the linear structures making $U$ fibrewise linear ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.fiberSqLin⟧.
- The structure is the transport of $p^*M(\Gamma, \Gamma')$ along $U^{-1}$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.bundleSq⟧, and $U$ is a strong bundle isomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_USq⟧.
- *Uniqueness* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.bundleSq_unique⟧: if $U$ is a strong bundle isomorphism for a second structure as well, the identity is a strong bundle isomorphism between the two.

**Proof of Lemma 19.8.18.**
- *Step 1, the bottom source* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.srcG⟧. $s : M(\Gamma, \Gamma') \to W$ is $H^{-1}$ followed by the projection of the pull-back along the local sc-diffeomorphism $s : M(\Psi, \Psi') \to O$. Hence it is a local strong bundle isomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isLocalStrongBundleIso_srcG⟧.
- *Step 2, the map $T$.* Define $T : p^*M(\Gamma, \Gamma') \to \hat\tau^* W$ by $((\hat e, \ldots), (w, \ldots)) \mapsto (\hat e, w)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.TMap⟧. It is a strong bundle map covering $\hat s$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_TMap⟧. It is near every point a strong bundle isomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isLocalStrongBundleIso_TMap⟧, for two reasons:
  - its composite with the projection $\hat\tau^* W \to W$ is $s \circ \mathrm{pr}_2$;
  - the projections are local strong bundle isomorphisms, $\hat s$ being a local sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_srcH⟧.
- *Step 3, $S$.* $S = \langle \hat Q, \Xi\rangle^{-1} \circ T$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_SMap⟧ is a composite of a local and a global strong bundle isomorphism. The two diagrams commute ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.Qh_SMap⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.xi_SMap⟧.
- *Step 4, the source of $M(\hat\Gamma, \hat\Gamma')$.* $s = S \circ U$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.SMap_USq⟧ is a strong bundle map ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_srcSq⟧. It is near every point an isomorphism, since $U$ is a global one ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isLocalStrongBundleIso_srcSq⟧.

**Proof of Definition 19.8.19 for the models and the top arrow.**
- *Step 1, the printed form* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · IsProperStrongBundleCoveringSquare.toBook⟧: drop the covering equality.
- *Step 2, the models* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · StrongEpBundleCoveringModel.isProperSquare⟧.
  - $\Xi$ is a strong bundle map over $\hat\tau$.
  - $\hat\tau$ is a surjective local sc-diffeomorphism (Definition 17.8.4) with finite fibres (Definition 8.5.1).
  - The neighbourhoods with the covering equality are part of Definition 8.5.9 (2).
- *Step 3, the top arrow $\bar p$.* $\bar p = \mathrm{pr}_2 \circ U$ is a strong bundle map covering $p$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_pbarSq⟧. It is near every point a strong bundle isomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isLocalStrongBundleIso_pbarSq⟧, and it is surjective ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTheorem · SquareUniformizerConstruction.surjective_pbarSq⟧ (§XIX.35, Step 6). The properness of this square is part of Theorem 19.8.22 (§XIX.41).

*Where Lean departs from the text.* In Lemma 19.8.16, the fibre of $\hat m$ over $(\hat e, \hat\varphi, \hat e')$ is mapped by $U$ to the fibre of the pull-back by $\hat w \mapsto \Xi(\hat w)$. As in Theorem 19.8.6, its linear structure and topology are the ones making $U$ fibrewise linear and a homeomorphism (Definition 19.8.17). The text cites Definition 8.5.9 for the existence and uniqueness of $\hat w$ with $\Xi(\hat w) = w$ and $\hat Q(\hat w) = \hat e$. We read this as the fibrewise bijectivity of $\Xi$, which is part of the local strong bundle isomorphisms of Definition 8.5.9. In Definition 19.8.19, "proper" is read with the covering equality, which the models satisfy; the printed form is kept and implied.

### XIX.40 Definition 19.8.20 and Lemma 19.8.21 (new in the 2021 edition), p. 956

**Statement.** *Definition 19.8.20* (p. 956, new in the 2021 edition; explicit) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.NatOpenEA⟧: a set $U \subset |E_A|$ belongs to $\bar T_A$ if every $\hat z \in U$, with $z = |P_A|(\hat z)$, has the following data:
- an object $\beta$ representing $|P|(z) \in |\mathcal B|$;
- a member $\hat\Gamma \in \tilde F(\beta)$;
- a point $\hat w \in \hat W$ with $|\hat\Gamma(\hat w)| = \hat z$;
- an open $V \ni \hat w$ with $|\hat\Gamma(V)| \subset U$.

*Lemma 19.8.21* (p. 956, new in the 2021 edition; explicit) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.lemma_19_8_21_hyp⟧: let $(\tilde F, \mathcal F)$ be a basic construction for $\mathfrak E$, so that:
- $\mathcal F$ is coherent;
- every class of $\mathcal B$ has a representative with uniformizers;
- $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ is metrizable.

Assume the compatibility of Definition 19.7.11 (§XIX.24), every top target map $\hat t$ continuous, with the witness ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopologyExamples · absSqBasic_isCompatible⟧. Then:
- $\bar T_A$ is a topology ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.natTopEA⟧;
- it is metrizable ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.metrizableSpace_natTopEA⟧;
- $|P_A| : (|E_A|, \bar T_A) \to (|\mathcal A|, T_{|\mathcal A|})$ is continuous ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.continuous_orbitProjA⟧;
- each $|\hat\Gamma| : |\hat W| \to |E_A|$ is a homeomorphism onto an open set ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isOpenEmbedding_orbitMap_Γh⟧.

The only hypothesis beyond the text is the compatibility. The text leaves the proof to the reader by reference to Theorem 19.6.8; it is written out here (point G.275). The topology on $|E_B|$ is that of §XIX.35 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.natTopEB⟧.

**Proof.**
- *Step 1, representing points.* If $|P_A|(\hat z) = |\hat\Psi(\hat e)|$, then $\hat z = |\hat\Gamma(\hat w)|$ for some $\hat w$ with $\hat Q(\hat w) = \hat e$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.exists_Γh_of_orbitProj_eq⟧: move $\hat z$ by a morphism into the fibre over $\hat\Psi(\hat e)$; $\hat A$ is onto that fibre. With the point lemma of §XIX.27 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringOrbitTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.exists_hat_of_orbitMap_eq_pt⟧, every $\hat z$ over $|\beta|$ is some $|\hat\Gamma(\hat w)|$ for $\hat\Gamma \in \tilde F(\beta)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.exists_Γh_of_pt⟧. By the class hypothesis every class of $\mathcal B$ contains the base object of a member ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.exists_unif⟧.
- *Step 2, transition elements* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.exists_sq_of_π_eq⟧: an isomorphism $\hat\Gamma(\hat w) \cong \hat\Gamma'(\hat w')$ is an element of $M(\hat\Gamma, \hat\Gamma')$ from $\hat w$ to $\hat w'$.
- *Step 3, the key step* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.exists_nbhd_sq⟧. For $h \in M(\hat\Gamma, \hat\Gamma')$ and an open $V' \ni t(h)$, the set $N = s(t^{-1}(V'))$ is an open neighbourhood of $s(h)$ with $|\hat\Gamma(N)| \subset |\hat\Gamma'(V')|$.
  - The target map is $t = \langle \hat Q', \Xi'\rangle^{-1} \circ (\hat t \circ \hat m, t \circ \bar p)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.tgtSq_eq⟧.
  - So $t$ is continuous ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.continuous_tgtSq⟧. This uses the continuity of $\hat t$, which is the compatibility, and that of the bottom target map, which is coherence (§XIX.35) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.continuous_tgtGmap⟧.
  - The source map is a local homeomorphism by Lemma 19.8.18 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isLocalHomeomorph_srcSq⟧, hence open.
- *Step 4, a topology.*
  - $\emptyset$ and unions are immediate ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.natOpenEA_empty⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.natOpenEA_sUnion⟧.
  - $|E_A|$ belongs by Step 1 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.natOpenEA_univ⟧.
  - For $\hat z \in U_1 \cap U_2$, represented through $(\hat\Gamma_1, V_1)$ and $(\hat\Gamma_2, V_2)$: Step 2 gives $h$ from the first representative to the second, and Step 3 gives $N$ for $V' = V_2$. Then $N \cap V_1$ represents $\hat z$ in $U_1 \cap U_2$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.natOpenEA_inter⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.natTopEA⟧.
- *Step 5, the open embeddings.* For open $V \subset \hat W$, $|\hat\Gamma(V)| \in \bar T_A$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isOpen_image_Γh⟧. At a point of $|\hat\Gamma(V)|$ represented through another member, Step 2 gives a transition element and Step 3 gives the neighbourhood. Hence $\hat w \mapsto |\hat\Gamma(\hat w)|$ is continuous ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.continuous_π_Γh⟧. $|\hat\Gamma|$ is injective because $\hat\Gamma$ is full ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.injective_orbitMap_Γh⟧. So it is an open embedding ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isOpenEmbedding_orbitMap_Γh⟧, as in Theorem 19.6.8 (3).
- *Step 6, continuity of $|P_A|$* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.continuous_orbitProjA⟧. Let $W$ be open in $T_{|\mathcal A|}$ and let $\hat z \in |P_A|^{-1}(W)$ be represented by $\hat w$ (Step 1). The map $d \mapsto |\hat\Psi(d)|$ is continuous (Lemma 19.7.17), so the preimage $V$ of $W$ under $\hat w \mapsto |\hat\Psi(\hat Q(\hat w))|$ is open. By (19.80), $|\hat\Gamma(V)| \subset |P_A|^{-1}(W)$. This is the proof of Theorem 19.6.8 (5).
- *Step 7, metrizability* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.metrizableSpace_natTopEA⟧.
  - $|\mathcal A|$ is metrizable by Theorem 19.7.25 (1) (§XIX.30), hence paracompact.
  - Every point of $|\mathcal A|$ lies in the open set $|\hat\Psi(\hat E)|$ of some member (Lemma 19.7.17 (2)), and $|\hat E|$ is metrizable as a subspace.
  - $|P_A|^{-1}(|\hat\Psi(\hat E)|) = |\hat\Gamma(\hat W)|$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.orbitProj_preimage_range⟧, homeomorphic to $|\hat W|$ by Step 5.
  - $|\hat W|$ is metrizable by Proposition 8.4.1 ⟦Chapter8/BundleOrbitParacompactOne · proposition_8_4_1⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.metrizableSpace_orbitSpace_Wh⟧: the orbit space of the action groupoid of $\hat W$ is that of the ep-groupoid of the strong bundle $(\hat W, \hat\mu)$.
  - The metrization criterion through a map applies ⟦Chapter2/Metrizable · metrizableSpace_of_pullback⟧: a space over a metrizable space, with a continuous map whose preimages of the sets of an open cover are metrizable, is metrizable.
- *Step 8* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.lemma_19_8_21_hyp⟧ collects Steps 4, 6 and 7.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* $\bar T_A$ is Definition 19.8.20 as printed. The phrase "follow the proof of Theorem 19.6.8" is read as Steps 5–7. Coherence, the class hypothesis and the metrizability of $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ are the conditions of Definition 19.8.14. The compatibility of Definition 19.7.11 enters through Step 3 and through $T_{|\mathcal A|}$ (Theorem 19.7.25).

### XIX.41 Theorem 19.8.22 (2017: Theorem 19.11), p. 956

**Statement.** *Theorem 19.8.22* (p. 956, 2017: Theorem 19.11; explicit) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareFibredProper · SquareBasicConstruction.theorem_19_8_22_hyp⟧: let $(\tilde F, \mathcal F)$ be a basic construction for a bundle covering square $\mathfrak E$ (Definition 19.8.15). Assume the compatibility of Definition 19.7.11 (§XIX.24), every top target map $\hat t$ continuous, with the witness ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopologyExamples · absSqBasic_isCompatible⟧. Then:
- (a) the orbit spaces carry natural metrizable topologies:
  - $T_{|\mathcal B|}$, by Definition 19.8.14 (1);
  - $T_{|\mathcal A|}$, by Theorem 19.7.25;
  - $\bar T_B$, the topology of Definition 19.6.7 for $(\bar F, \mathcal F)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.metrizableSpace_natTopEB⟧;
  - $\bar T_A$, the topology of Definition 19.8.20 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.metrizableSpace_natTopEA⟧;
- (b) for members $\hat\Gamma$, $\hat\Gamma'$, the square $\bar p : M(\hat\Gamma, \hat\Gamma') \to M(\Gamma, \Gamma')$ over $p : M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi') \to M(\Psi, \Psi')$ is a proper strong bundle covering square ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isProperSquare_pbarSq⟧;
- (c) the source and target maps of $M(\hat\Gamma, \hat\Gamma')$ and of $M(\Gamma, \Gamma')$ are local strong bundle isomorphisms over $\hat s$, $\hat t$, $s$, $t$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_8_22_topology_hyp⟧;
- (d) the inversion is an isomorphism between proper strong bundle covering squares, diagram (19.98) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareFibredProper · SquareBasicConstruction.theorem_19_8_22_inv_hyp⟧;
- (e) the unit is a strong bundle map between proper strong bundle covering squares, diagram (19.99) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_8_22_unit⟧;
- (f) the multiplication $M(\hat\Gamma', \hat\Gamma'')\,{}_s\!\times_t M(\hat\Gamma, \hat\Gamma') \to M(\hat\Gamma, \hat\Gamma'')$ is a strong bundle map between proper strong bundle covering squares, diagram (19.100) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareFibredProper · SquareUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_8_22_mul_hyp⟧, the fibred-product square being proper ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareFibredProper · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isProperSquare_pbarFP⟧.

The only hypothesis beyond the text is the compatibility, and the unit needs none. The text gives no separate argument for the multiplication and the fibred-product square. They are written out with the read form of Definition 19.8.19 (§XIX.39), through general lemmas:
- the fibred-product lemma ⟦Chapter19/FibredProductCovering · isProperStrongBundleCoveringSquare_fibredProduct⟧;
- the local sc-diffeomorphism lemma ⟦Chapter19/FibredProductCovering · MPolyfold.isLocalScDiffeo_fibredProductMap⟧;
- the sheet criterion ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · isProperStrongBundleCoveringSquare_of_sheets⟧.

**Proof.**
- *Step 1, (a)* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_8_22_topology_hyp⟧. $T_{|\mathcal A|}$ is metrizable by Theorem 19.7.25 (1) (§XIX.30). $\bar T_B$ is metrizable by §XIX.35, Step 8, and $\bar T_A$ by §XIX.40.
- *Step 2, the sheet criterion* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · isProperStrongBundleCoveringSquare_of_sheets⟧. Suppose:
  - $\hat a$ is a strong bundle map over a surjective local sc-diffeomorphism $a$ with finite fibres;
  - $\hat a$ is near every point a strong bundle isomorphism;
  - every $x$ has an open $U \ni x$ such that $a^{-1}(U)$ is the disjoint union of open sets $U'(\hat x_i) \ni \hat x_i$ on which $a$ is injective.

  Then the read form of Definition 19.8.19 holds. Let $U_2 \subset U$ be the intersection of the images of the finitely many local isomorphism neighbourhoods at the $\hat x_i$, and shrink the sheets to $U'(\hat x_i) \cap a^{-1}(U_2)$.
- *Step 3, the sheets of $p$* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.exists_sheets_pB⟧. For $\hat h \in p^{-1}(k)$, put $U'(\hat h) = \hat s^{-1}(U(y))$, where $U(y)$ is the sheet of the proper covering model of $\hat\Psi$ containing $\hat s(\hat h)$. By Lemma 19.7.9, $\hat s$ maps each fibre of $p$ bijectively onto $\hat\tau^{-1}(s(k))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringConstruction · CoveringUniformizer.lemma_19_7_9⟧. Hence:
  - these sets are open, since $\hat s$ is a local homeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringTopology · CoveringBasicConstruction.isLocalHomeomorph_srcHat⟧;
  - they are mutually disjoint;
  - $p$ is injective on each of them;
  - they cover $M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$.

  So $p$ is a trivial covering, and $U$ can be taken to be the whole space.
- *Step 4, (b)* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isProperSquare_pbarSq⟧. Step 2 applies to $\bar p$ (§XIX.39) with the following inputs:
  - $p$ is a surjective local sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.surjective_pBmap⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_pBmap⟧;
  - $p$ has finite fibres, by Lemma 19.7.9;
  - the sheets are those of Step 3.
- *Step 5, (c).*
  - The sources are Lemma 19.8.18 and its Step 1.
  - The bottom target is a local strong bundle isomorphism for coherent $\mathcal F$ (§XIX.35) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isLocalStrongBundleIso_tgtGmap⟧.
  - The top target is $t = T' \circ U$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.SMapT_USq⟧. Here $T'$ sends $(a, g)$ to the lift of $t(g)$ over $\hat t(a)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.SMapT⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.TMapT⟧. Under the compatibility, $\hat t$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism (Theorem 19.7.28 (1), §XIX.31) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_tgtH⟧. So $T'$ is near every point a strong bundle isomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isLocalStrongBundleIso_TMapT⟧, as $T$ in Lemma 19.8.18, and so is $t$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_tgtSq⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isLocalStrongBundleIso_tgtSq⟧.
- *Step 6, (d), the inversion.*
  - The bottom inversion is a strong bundle isomorphism for coherent $\mathcal F$ (§XIX.35) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_invG⟧.
  - On top, $\iota = U^{-1} \circ (\hat\iota \times \iota) \circ U$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.invSq_eq⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.invPB⟧. Under the compatibility $\hat\iota$ is sc-smooth (Theorem 19.7.28 (1)) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isScSmoothMap_invH⟧. So $\hat\iota \times \iota$ is a strong bundle map ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_invPB⟧, and so is $\iota$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_invSq⟧.
  - $\iota$ is an involution ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.invSq_invSq⟧, hence a strong bundle isomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleIso_invSq⟧.
  - The faces of (19.98) commute ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.mSq_invSq⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.pbarSq_invSq⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.pBmap_invH⟧, and both squares are proper by (b) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_8_22_inv_hyp⟧.
  - For a basic construction, coherence is a field ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareFibredProper · SquareBasicConstruction.theorem_19_8_22_inv_hyp⟧.
- *Step 7, (e), the unit* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_8_22_unit⟧.
  - $u = U^{-1} \circ (\hat u \circ \hat Q, u \circ \Xi)$ on $\hat W$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.unitSq_eq⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.unitPB⟧.
  - $\hat u$ is sc-smooth ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isScSmoothMap_unitH⟧, and the pairing lemma gives a strong bundle map ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_unitPB⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_unitSq⟧.
  - The bottom unit is §XIX.35 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_unitG⟧.
  - The faces commute ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.mSq_unitSq⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.pbarSq_unitSq⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.pBmap_unitH⟧.
  - The squares $K$ and $M(\hat\Gamma, \hat\Gamma)$ are proper by §XIX.39 and (b).

  Neither coherence nor the compatibility is used.
- *Step 8, (f), the multiplication maps* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_mul_and_faces⟧.
  - The fibred product $M(\hat\Psi', \hat\Psi'')\,{}_s\!\times_t M(\hat\Psi, \hat\Psi')$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.HFP⟧ is a sub-M-polyfold of the product (Definition 17.1.8 (c)), since $\hat s$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism and, under the compatibility, $\hat t$ is sc-smooth ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.HFPpoly⟧.
  - The strong bundle over it is the pull-back of $\hat m$ by $\mathrm{pr}_2$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.mulBundleSq⟧. Its total space is the fibred product $M(\hat\Gamma', \hat\Gamma'')\,{}_s\!\times_t M(\hat\Gamma, \hat\Gamma')$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.FPSq⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.fpEquivSq⟧: a point $((a', a), h)$ with $\hat m(h) = a$ determines the element $h'$ over $a'$ with $s(h') = t(h)$, by the lift of §XIX.39.
  - The multiplication is $U^{-1} \circ (\hat m \circ \pi, m \circ (\bar p\,{}_s\!\times_t \bar p))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.mulSq⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.mulPB⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_mulPB⟧. It is a strong bundle map covering the top multiplication ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isScSmoothMap_mulH⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_mulSq⟧, and on the fibred product it is the composition $(h', h) \mapsto h' \circ h$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.mulSq_toFPSq⟧.
  - The bottom multiplication is §XIX.35 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_mulG⟧.
  - $\bar p\,{}_s\!\times_t \bar p$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.pbarFP⟧ is a strong bundle map over $p\,{}_s\!\times_t p$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.pFP⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isStrongBundleMap_pbarFP⟧.
  - The faces of (19.100) commute ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.mSq_mulSq⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.pbarSq_mulSq⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizerConstruction.pBmap_mulH⟧.
- *Step 9, the general fibred-product lemmas.* Let $\Theta(x_1, x_2) = (f_1(x_1), f_2(x_2))$ be a map between fibred products $X_1\,{}_s\!\times_t X_2$ and $X_1'\,{}_{s'}\!\times_{t'} X_2'$, with $s$, $s'$ local sc-diffeomorphisms and $t$, $t'$ sc-smooth.
  - If $f_2$ is a local sc-diffeomorphism, so is $\Theta$ ⟦Chapter19/FibredProductCovering · MPolyfold.isLocalScDiffeo_fibredProductMap⟧: $\mathrm{pr}_2' \circ \Theta = f_2 \circ \mathrm{pr}_2$, and both second projections are local sc-diffeomorphisms.
  - Assume that $s$ is fibrewise bijective over $f_1$: for $s'(k_1) = t'(f_2(a_2))$ there is $a_1$ over $k_1$ with $s(a_1) = t(a_2)$, and $s$ is injective on the fibres of $f_1$. Then the fibre of $\Theta$ over $k$ is the fibre of $f_2$ over $k_2$ ⟦Chapter19/FibredProductCovering · MPolyfold.fibredProductMap_eq_of_snd_eq⟧, so $\Theta$ is surjective ⟦Chapter19/FibredProductCovering · MPolyfold.surjective_fibredProductMap⟧ with finite fibres ⟦Chapter19/FibredProductCovering · MPolyfold.finite_fibredProductMap_fiber⟧. The sheets of $f_2$ over $V$ give sheets of $\Theta$ over $\mathrm{pr}_2'^{-1}(V)$ ⟦Chapter19/FibredProductCovering · MPolyfold.exists_sheets_fibredProductMap⟧.
  - For a strong bundle map $\Phi$ over $f_2$, the map $\tilde\Theta = (\Theta \circ \pi, \Phi \circ \pi')$ between the pull-backs by the second projections ⟦Chapter19/FibredProductCovering · StrongBundle.fibredProductLift⟧ is a strong bundle map over $\Theta$ ⟦Chapter19/FibredProductCovering · StrongBundle.isStrongBundleMap_fibredProductLift⟧. It is near every point an isomorphism when $\Phi$ is ⟦Chapter19/FibredProductCovering · StrongBundle.isLocalStrongBundleIso_fibredProductLift⟧.
  - Hence, if $\Phi$ over $f_2$ is a proper strong bundle covering square, so is $\tilde\Theta$ over $\Theta$ ⟦Chapter19/FibredProductCovering · isProperStrongBundleCoveringSquare_fibredProduct⟧.
- *Step 10, (f), properness* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareFibredProper · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isProperSquare_pbarFP⟧. Apply Step 9 with $f_1 = f_2 = p$ and $\Phi = \bar p$, which is proper by (b). $\hat s$ is fibrewise bijective over $p$ by Lemma 19.7.9 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareFibredProper · SquareUniformizerConstruction.liftSrc_HFP⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareFibredProper · SquareUniformizerConstruction.injOn_srcH_HFP⟧. Thus $p\,{}_s\!\times_t p$ is a surjective local sc-diffeomorphism ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareFibredProper · SquareUniformizerConstruction.surjective_pFP⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareFibredProper · SquareUniformizerConstruction.isLocalScDiffeo_pFP⟧. Its fibre over $(k', k)$ corresponds to that of $p$ over $k$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareFibredProper · SquareUniformizerConstruction.bijOn_pFP_fiber⟧. That fibre has as many points as $\hat\tau^{-1}(s(k))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareFibredProper · SquareUniformizerConstruction.ncard_pFP_fiber⟧ and is finite ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareFibredProper · SquareUniformizerConstruction.finite_pFP_fiber⟧. With Step 8 this is (f) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareFibredProper · SquareUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_8_22_mul_hyp⟧.
- *Step 11, assembling* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareFibredProper · SquareBasicConstruction.theorem_19_8_22_hyp⟧: coherence, the class hypothesis and the metrizability of $T_{|\mathcal B|}$ are fields of the basic construction, and the compatibility is the one added hypothesis.

*Where Lean departs from the text.* The phrase "the orbit spaces of $\mathcal A$, $E_A$, $\mathcal B$ and $E_B$ have natural metrizable topologies" is read with the four topologies of (a). The phrase "the local isomorphisms $s$, $t$" is read as: the source and target maps of both transition sets are local strong bundle isomorphisms over the local sc-diffeomorphisms of the base. The strong bundle structure on the fibred products, which (19.100) uses without spelling it out, is read as the pull-back of $\hat m$, respectively $m$, by $\mathrm{pr}_2$, as for $\bar m$ in §17.8 and §19.6. The compatibility is not derived from Definitions 19.8.13–19.8.15. Coherence concerns the bottom strong bundles $M(\Gamma, \Gamma')$. The top target maps depend on the sheets of the $\hat\Psi$, on which Definition 19.8.10 imposes nothing beyond Definition 19.7.4. For covering basic constructions $\hat t$ need not be continuous without it ⟦Chapter19/CoveringLocalExamples · proposition_19_7_15_two_without_compatible⟧ (§XIX.34). The top fibred product is a sub-M-polyfold only once $\hat t$ is sc-smooth.

### XIX.42 The witness: the absolute-value square

**Statement.** *The absolute-value square* (no counterpart in the text; extra) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSqBasic⟧ consists of three parts:
- the bundle covering square of the absolute-value covering $P : \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes (\mathbb Z^\times \times \mathbb R) \to \mathbb Z/2 \ltimes \mathbb R$ (§XIX.24) with the reflection bundle $\mu_B$, pulled back ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · reflAbsSquare⟧. Here $|\mathcal A| \cong \mathbb R$, $|\mathcal B| \cong [0, \infty)$, and $|P|$ is the absolute value;
- the uniformizers of §XIX.37, Step 7 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSquareU⟧;
- the basic construction of §XIX.38.

Every hypothesis of Lemma 19.8.21 and Theorem 19.8.22 holds for it, the compatibility included ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopologyExamples · absSqBasic_hypotheses⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopologyExamples · absSqBasic_isCompatible⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSqBasic_definition_19_8_15⟧. Lemma 19.8.21 holds for it ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopologyExamples · absSqBasic_lemma_19_8_21⟧, and so does Theorem 19.8.22 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopologyExamples · absSqBasic_theorem_19_8_22⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplicationExamples · absSqBasic_theorem_19_8_22_structure⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareFibredProperExamples · absSqBasic_theorem_19_8_22_fibredProper⟧. It is non-degenerate:
- the isotropy at $0$ is non-trivial and acts on the fibre by $-1$;
- the covering is two-sheeted away from $0$;
- the base map $p\,{}_s\!\times_t p$ of the fibred-product square has a fibre with two points.

**Proof.**
- *Step 1, the square* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · reflAbsSquare⟧. It is the square of the pull-back with $\mu_A = \mu_B \circ P$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · reflAbsSquare_μA⟧. $\mu_A(-1, (1, 0))$ maps $((1, 0), (0, v))$ to $((-1, 0), (0, -v))$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackExamples · reflAbsSquare_act_neg⟧.
- *Step 2, the uniformizers.* At each $\beta$ the uniformizer is the square uniformizer of the member of §XIX.36 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSquareU_toPullbackUniformizer⟧. Its model is $\hat\tau^* W_V \to H \ltimes (\mathbb Z^\times \times V)$ over the sheet model. At $\beta = 0$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSquareU_zero⟧:
  - $\Xi$ is two-to-one, and $-1$ acts on $W$ by $(v, a) \mapsto (-v, -a)$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSquareU_nondegenerate⟧;
  - its model is a proper strong bundle covering square ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · absSquareU_isProperSquare⟧;
  - the fibre of $\hat\tau$ over the centre has two points ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareFibredProperExamples · absSqUnif₀_fiber_ncard⟧.
- *Step 3, the hypotheses* ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopologyExamples · absSqBasic_hypotheses⟧.
  - Coherence, the class hypothesis and the metrizability of $T_{|\mathcal B|} \cong [0, \infty)$ come from §XIX.36.
  - The compatibility is that of the absolute-value construction, transported along the equality of the underlying constructions ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackCoherence · absFt_isCompatible⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopologyExamples · absSqBasic_isCompatible⟧.
  - The member at $0$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopologyExamples · absSqUnif₀⟧ is the non-degenerate one of Step 2.
- *Step 4, the conclusions.*
  - Lemma 19.8.21 is the general lemma applied with Step 3 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopologyExamples · absSqBasic_lemma_19_8_21⟧.
  - At the member at $0$, the following hold ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopologyExamples · absSqBasic_theorem_19_8_22⟧: $|\mathcal A|$, $|E_B|$ and $|E_A|$ are metrizable; $\bar p$ over $p$ is a proper square; and the source and target maps of $M(\hat\Gamma, \hat\Gamma)$ are local strong bundle isomorphisms. $|E_B|$ is metrizable and $|P_B|$ is continuous ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopologyExamples · absFt_metrizableSpace_natTopEB⟧.
  - The inversion is a strong bundle isomorphism, the unit and the multiplication are strong bundle maps, and the multiplication is the composition ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplicationExamples · absSqBasic_theorem_19_8_22_structure⟧.
  - The complete theorem applies ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareFibredProperExamples · absSqBasic_theorem_19_8_22_fibredProper⟧. The fibred-product square at the member at $0$ is proper, and the fibre of $p\,{}_s\!\times_t p$ over the pair of units at the centre ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareFibredProperExamples · absSqUnitPair⟧ has two points, so it is not a one-sheeted covering.

### XIX.43 The points of §§19.6–19.8 and what is not formalized

**The points.** Seven points of §§19.6–19.8 are left to the reader or have no formal content.
- *G.269, Remark 19.6.2* (p. 892; formalized in the form used). Strong bundle uniformizers with a strong bundle $W \to E$ as model are used only in §19.8. They enter there through Definition 19.8.10 (3) ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · SquareUniformizer.definition_19_8_10⟧, and the zero-section consequence is proved ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareMultiplication · SquareUniformizer.remark_19_6_2⟧. The general variant for $\mathcal S$ alone is not built separately (§§XIX.16, XIX.37).
- *G.270, the proof of Theorem 19.6.8* (p. 897; formalized). The text calls the theorem easily verified. The proof is written out from §19.2 for the basic construction of $E_\mu$ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_8_one⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.theorem_19_6_8_four⟧ (§XIX.18).
- *G.271, the direction $\Leftarrow$ of Proposition 19.6.9* (p. 898; formalized). The metric of $|E_\mu|$ is restricted along the zero section, which is a continuous embedding ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.isEmbedding_zeroOrbit⟧ ⟦Chapter19/StrongBundleTopology · BasicStrongBundleConstruction.proposition_19_6_9⟧ (§XIX.18).
- *G.272, Remark 19.6.14* (p. 900), and *G.274, Remark 19.8.11* (p. 944): nothing to formalize. The remarks describe how the constructions will be used and contain no statement (§§XIX.20, XIX.37).
- *G.273, Theorem 19.8.6 (5) for $|P^*E_B|$* (pp. 938–939; not stated separately). (5) is proved for $|\mathcal A|$ and $|\mathcal B|$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringPullbackTheorem · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.theorem_19_8_6_hyp⟧ and for $|E_B|$ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · PullbackUniformizerConstruction.metrizableSpace_natTopEB⟧. For $|P^*E_B|$ the square result applies to the square of the pull-back ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquare · BundleCoveringSquare.ofPull⟧ ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareExamples · PullbackUniformizer.toSquare⟧, followed by Lemma 19.8.21, but this instance is not written out (§XIX.35).
- *G.275, the proof of Lemma 19.8.21* (p. 956; formalized). The text refers to the proof of Theorem 19.6.8. The proof is written out under the compatibility of Definition 19.7.11 ⟦Chapter19/CoveringSquareTopology · SquareUniformizerConstruction.lemma_19_8_21_hyp⟧ (§XIX.40).

**What is not formalized, and what holds in another form.**
- Remark 19.6.2 in its general form (G.269) and Theorem 19.8.6 (5) for $|P^*E_B|$ (G.273), as above.
- Four statements of §19.7 do not hold as we read them, and the proved forms are recorded in the sections indicated.
  - The local homeomorphism property of $|P|$ in Proposition 19.7.18, Corollary 19.7.20 (3) and Theorem 19.7.25 (4b): one example, the absolute value $|\mathcal A| \cong \mathbb R \to |\mathcal B| \cong [0, \infty)$, serves for all three. $|P|$ is a local homeomorphism at the classes where the isotropy maps $G_a \to G_{P(a)}$ are onto (§§XIX.28–XIX.30).
  - The divisibility remark after Lemma 19.7.19 (§XIX.29). It is not used later.
- Definition 19.7.11 in its literal form. The statements of §§19.7–19.8 that need the continuity of the lifted target maps are proved under the compatibility reading. Two examples satisfy the literal definition but not the compatibility, and for them Proposition 19.7.15 (2) and Lemma 19.7.17 do not hold as we read them (§XIX.26). Definitions 19.8.4 and 19.8.5 do not imply the compatibility (§XIX.34).
